## Ordered content

Phase 0 time-boxed plan

You asked for minute-by-minute. A literal minute-by-minute plan for 7–10 days would become unreadable, so I am giving you a professional time-boxed minute schedule in 15–60 minute blocks. This is how a senior product/full-stack founder would actually execute it.

Phase 0 — 7-Day Standard Plan

Day 1: Vision, business model, and market frame

Goal of Day 1

Define exactly what you are building and what you are not building.

Day 1 schedulePetSaathi – Daily Business Planning Schedule

09:00–09:15 — Write a One-Line Business Definition

During this time, I will write one clear sentence explaining what PetSaathi does.

The sentence should mention the target customers, the main services and the special value of the platform.

Business definition:“PetSaathi is a trusted online pet-care platform that helps pet owners book verified dog walkers, pet sitters and pet boarding services.”

Output: A clear and simple product statement.

09:15–09:45 — Define the Customer Problem

During this session, I will identify the main problems faced by urban pet owners.

The top five customer problems are:

Pet owners find it difficult to locate trusted and verified caregivers.

They may not receive live updates while their pets are being cared for.

Dog-walking services may not provide GPS tracking or route information.

Emergency veterinary support may not be available during a booking.

Available pet-care services may be scattered and difficult to compare.

Output: A list of the five most important customer pain points.

09:45–10:15 — Define the First Services

During this time, I will decide which services should be included in the first version of PetSaathi.

The first service will be dog walking because it can be used regularly by working pet owners.

The second service will be pet sitting, where a caregiver looks after the pet at the owner’s home.

The third service will be pet boarding, where the pet stays safely with a verified caregiver when the owner is travelling.

Output: A prioritized list of dog walking, pet sitting and pet boarding services.

10:15–10:30 — Break

I will take a short break to refresh my mind before starting the research work.

Output: No project output is required during this period.

10:30–11:15 — Study the Rover Model

During this session, I will study how Rover operates as an online pet-care marketplace.

I will examine how pet owners search for local caregivers, compare profiles, review services, make bookings and complete payments. Rover officially offers services such as dog walking, pet sitting, boarding and daycare through local sitter profiles.

I will also study how the platform manages sitter profiles, identity verification, safety processes, reviews and customer support. Rover requires prospective sitters and dog walkers to complete safety-related steps, identity verification and a background check.

Output: A clear understanding of the complete pet-care marketplace flow.

11:15–12:00 — Study Indian Competitors

During this time, I will study existing pet-care companies and platforms operating in India.

I will check what services they provide, which cities they cover, how they present caregiver profiles and how their booking process works.

I will also identify the features that are missing or not properly developed on those platforms.

Output: A list of relevant Indian pet-care competitors.

12:00–12:30 — Create Competitor Matrix Columns

During this session, I will prepare a comparison table for PetSaathi and its competitors.

The table will include important columns such as:

Company name

Services offered

Service price

Cities covered

Caregiver verification

GPS tracking

Photo and video updates

Customer reviews

Emergency support

Main strengths

Main weaknesses

This matrix will help identify market gaps and opportunities for PetSaathi.

Output: A structured competitor matrix covering prices, features and trust gaps.

12:30–13:30 — Lunch Break

I will take a lunch break and return with a fresh mind for the next planning activities.

Output: No project output is required during this period.

13:30–14:15 — Define Customer Personas

During this session, I will create profiles of the main types of customers who may use PetSaathi.

Possible customer personas include:

A working professional who needs daily dog-walking support.

A working couple who needs pet sitting during office hours.

A frequent traveller who requires safe pet boarding.

A senior citizen who needs help managing an active pet.

A pet parent who needs special care during festivals or emergencies.

Each persona will include the customer’s needs, problems, behaviour and preferred services.

Output: Three to five clearly defined customer personas.

14:15–15:00 — Define Sitter Personas

During this time, I will identify the different types of caregivers who can provide services through PetSaathi.

Possible sitter personas include:

A college student who loves animals and wants part-time work.

A homemaker who can provide pet boarding from home.

A retired person with experience caring for pets.

A veterinary student who understands animal health and behaviour.

A professional dog walker or trainer.

Each sitter persona will include experience, availability, skills, service area and expected income.

Output: Three to five suitable sitter or caregiver personas.

15:00–15:15 — Break

I will take a short break before beginning the business model and brand-positioning work.

Output: No project output is required during this period.

15:15–16:00 — Define Revenue Streams

During this session, I will decide how PetSaathi can earn revenue.

The platform may charge a commission on every completed booking.

It may also offer monthly subscription plans to pet owners who regularly use walking or sitting services.

Additional income can come from premium sitter listings, emergency vet services, pet-care protection plans, society partnerships and grooming-service commissions.

Output: A clear revenue model based on commissions, subscriptions and add-on services.

16:00–16:45 — Define the Trust Promise

During this time, I will define the main trust and safety promise of PetSaathi.

Caregivers will be verified through identity documents, interviews and available police or background-verification processes.

Pet owners will be able to track dog walks through GPS and receive photos, videos and service updates.

After every service, the caregiver will provide a pet report card containing information about food, water, walking, behaviour and health concerns.

Trust promise:“Every PetSaathi booking will provide a verified caregiver, visible service updates and clear proof of pet care.”

Output: A trust system based on verification, tracking and pet report cards.

16:45–17:30 — Write the Positioning Statement

During this session, I will write a final statement explaining how PetSaathi should be presented in the market.

The statement should clearly show the target customer, the main services and the reason why PetSaathi is different from other platforms.

Positioning statement:“PetSaathi is an India-focused, trust-first pet-care marketplace that connects urban pet parents with verified caregivers for dog walking, pet sitting and safe boarding, supported by live tracking, report cards and emergency-care assistance.”

Output: A final brand direction and positioning statement.

17:30–18:00 — Day Review

At the end of the day, I will review all the decisions made during the planning process.

I will check whether the business definition, customer problems, services, personas, revenue model and trust features are connected properly.

I will also prepare a list of unanswered questions, required research and tasks for the next working day.

Output: A summary of completed decisions and a list of open questions.

Day 1 example output

Your product definition:

PetSaathi is a verified pet-care marketplace for Indian pet parents to book dog walking, pet sitting, and safe boarding with live updates, sitter verification, and emergency vet support.

Your first target:

Working pet parents in premium apartments who need reliable daily dog walking and travel-time pet care.

Your first trust promise:

Verified sitter, live updates, pet report card, emergency support.

Day 2: City selection research

Goal of Day 2

Compare Bengaluru, Pune, Mumbai, Gurugram, Ahmedabad, and Surat.

PetSaathi City Selection Scorecard

The purpose of this scorecard is to identify the best city for launching the first PetSaathi pilot.

Each city should be rated from 1 to 5 for every factor:

5 – Excellent

4 – Good

3 – Average

2 – Weak

1 – Very weak

The final score should be calculated using this formula:

Weighted Score = City Rating ÷ 5 × Factor Weight

The total possible score is 100 points.

1. Pet Parent Density — Weight: 20%

Pet parent density means the estimated number of pet-owning households in a city or selected area.

This is the most important factor because PetSaathi needs enough pet owners who may require dog walking, pet sitting or pet boarding services.

A city should receive a high score when it has a large number of registered pets, active pet communities, veterinary visits, pet stores and pet-related social-media groups.

Municipal pet-registration data, dog-licence records, veterinary information and local surveys can be used to estimate this factor.

Scoring guidance:

Give 5 points when the city has a very large and active pet-parent population.

Give 3 points when the city has moderate pet ownership.

Give 1 point when pet ownership appears limited or demand is difficult to identify.

Simple explanation:“This factor tells us whether enough potential customers own pets in the selected city.”

2. Premium Apartment Density — Weight: 15%

Premium apartment density means the number of gated societies, residential towers and organised housing communities in the target area.

PetSaathi can operate more efficiently in areas where many pet owners live close to one another.

One sitter may be able to handle several bookings within the same society or nearby societies. This can reduce travel time, improve trust and lower customer-acquisition costs.

The evaluation should consider the number of premium societies, their occupancy, average household profile and pet-friendly policies.

Scoring guidance:

Give 5 points when the city has many premium and high-density apartment communities.

Give 3 points when apartment demand exists but is spread across different areas.

Give 1 point when customers mainly live in scattered or low-density locations.

Simple explanation:“This factor shows whether PetSaathi can find many customers within a small service area.”

3. Disposable Income — Weight: 15%

Disposable income means the amount of money customers can spend after paying for their basic needs.

Dog walking, pet sitting and boarding are convenience-based services. Therefore, customers with stable and higher incomes are more likely to use them regularly.

The analysis should consider household consumption, average salary levels, premium housing demand and spending on non-essential services.

A high score does not mean that every resident is wealthy. It means that the target neighbourhoods contain enough customers who can afford regular pet-care services.

Scoring guidance:

Give 5 points when the target customers have strong spending capacity.

Give 3 points when spending capacity is moderate.

Give 1 point when customers are highly price-sensitive or unlikely to book regularly.

Simple explanation:“This factor helps us understand whether customers can afford professional pet-care services.”

4. Competitor Pressure — Weight: 15%

Competitor pressure means the number and strength of existing pet-care platforms, local boarding centres, walkers and sitter services.

This factor should use favourability scoring. A high score means that the market has visible demand but does not have too many strong direct competitors.

The competitor analysis should examine services, prices, ratings, service areas, sitter quality, verification systems and customer complaints.

A city with no competitors should not automatically receive the highest score because the absence of competition may also indicate weak demand.

The ideal city has clear pet-care demand but still has important gaps in trust, tracking, verification or service quality.

Scoring guidance:

Give 5 points when demand is visible and direct competition is limited or weak.

Give 3 points when several competitors operate but differentiation is still possible.

Give 1 point when the market is crowded with strong and trusted competitors.

Simple explanation:“This factor checks whether PetSaathi can enter the market and still create a clear advantage.”

5. Sitter Supply — Weight: 10%

Sitter supply means the availability of people who can work as dog walkers, pet sitters or boarding providers.

Possible sitters may include college students, homemakers, retired people, veterinary students, trainers and existing pet-care workers.

The city should receive a high score when potential sitters are available, interested in flexible work and willing to complete verification and training.

The analysis should also consider expected payouts, travel distance, working hours and retention.

Scoring guidance:

Give 5 points when verified sitters can be recruited quickly and affordably.

Give 3 points when recruitment is possible but requires additional training or incentives.

Give 1 point when suitable caregivers are difficult to find or retain.

Simple explanation:“This factor tells us whether PetSaathi can build enough service providers to complete customer bookings.”

6. Vet and Groomer Ecosystem — Weight: 10%

The vet and groomer ecosystem means the availability of veterinary clinics, emergency hospitals, groomers, trainers, pet shops and other pet-service partners.

PetSaathi requires reliable veterinary support for emergencies during walking, sitting or boarding bookings.

Vets and groomers may also become referral partners and help the platform reach more pet parents.

The analysis should consider the number of service providers, their quality, operating hours, emergency availability and interest in partnerships.

Scoring guidance:

Give 5 points when the city has a strong and accessible pet-care ecosystem.

Give 3 points when standard services are available but emergency coverage is limited.

Give 1 point when professional pet-care support is difficult to access.

Simple explanation:“This factor checks whether the city has enough professional partners to support safe pet-care operations.”

7. Operational Difficulty — Weight: 10%

Operational difficulty includes traffic, travel distance, local regulations, service-area size, staff costs, weather conditions and booking-management complexity.

This factor should also use favourability scoring. A high score means the city is easier to operate in.

PetSaathi depends on caregivers arriving on time. Therefore, heavy traffic, long travel distances and widely scattered customers can reduce service quality.

The city should also be checked for pet-registration rules, boarding requirements, society restrictions and other local compliance needs.

Scoring guidance:

Give 5 points when operations are simple, affordable and geographically manageable.

Give 3 points when operations have manageable challenges.

Give 1 point when traffic, costs, compliance or distance may create serious problems.

Simple explanation:“This factor tells us how easy or difficult it will be to deliver services consistently in the city.”

8. Founder Network Advantage — Weight: 5%

Founder network advantage means the personal and professional connections already available to the founding team.

These connections may include apartment managers, pet parents, veterinarians, groomers, colleges, animal-welfare groups, potential sitters and business mentors.

A strong local network can make the first pilot faster and less expensive because it supports customer interviews, sitter recruitment, partnerships and early bookings.

This factor has the lowest weight because the market opportunity should remain more important than personal convenience.

Scoring guidance:

Give 5 points when the founder already has strong and useful local contacts.

Give 3 points when a small network exists but needs to be expanded.

Give 1 point when the founder has almost no local support or contacts.

Simple explanation:“This factor checks whether the founder’s existing connections can help launch and test the platform.”

Final Decision Rule

After scoring every city, calculate its weighted total out of 100.

The city with the highest score should not be selected automatically. The team should also verify the result through:

Interviews with pet parents

Interviews with potential sitters

Discussions with vets and groomers

Competitor research

A small landing-page or WhatsApp booking test

A pilot inside selected apartment societies

The final launch city should have:

Strong customer demand

Enough verified sitter supply

Customers who can afford the service

A supportive veterinary ecosystem

Manageable competition and operations

Access to a small but useful founder network

Simple Explanation for Professor

“I created a city selection scorecard to choose the best location for launching PetSaathi. Every city is rated according to pet-parent demand, premium apartments, customer spending capacity, competitors, sitter availability, veterinary support, operational difficulty and the founder’s local network. Each factor has a different weight according to its importance. The city with the strongest weighted score will be validated through customer interviews and a small pilot before the final launch decision.”

PetSaathi — Day 2 City Selection Schedule

09:00–09:20 — Create the City Comparison Sheet

During this session, I will prepare one structured comparison sheet for all six shortlisted cities: Bengaluru, Pune, Mumbai, Gurugram, Ahmedabad and Surat.

The sheet will contain the following evaluation factors:

Pet-parent density

Premium apartment density

Disposable income

Competitor pressure

Sitter supply

Veterinary and grooming ecosystem

Operational difficulty

Founder network advantage

I will add the correct weight for every factor and create columns for city ratings, weighted scores, evidence sources and important notes.

Every city will be rated on the same scale from 1 to 5 so that the comparison remains fair.

Output: A complete city-selection scorecard ready for research and scoring.

09:20–10:00 — Research Bengaluru

During this period, I will collect information about Bengaluru as a possible launch city for PetSaathi.

I will identify areas with large apartment communities, working professionals and active pet-owner groups.

I will research existing dog-walking, pet-sitting and pet-boarding companies to understand the level of competition.

I will also study the availability of veterinary clinics, groomers, trainers and possible emergency-care partners.

Another important task will be checking whether college students, animal lovers, homemakers and existing pet-care workers can be recruited as verified sitters.

Traffic, travel time and the distance between service areas will also be studied because these factors may affect punctuality and operating costs.

BBMP publishes animal-welfare guidance for residents’ associations, pet owners and community animal caregivers, which can help in understanding society-level pet-care conditions and operational expectations.

Output: Detailed Bengaluru notes covering demand, competition, sitter supply, partnerships and operational challenges.

10:00–10:40 — Research Pune

During this session, I will evaluate Pune using the same criteria used for Bengaluru.

I will identify premium apartment areas, IT and business zones, student locations and neighbourhoods with visible pet-care demand.

I will research local pet sitters, boarding centres, walkers, veterinary clinics and grooming providers.

I will examine whether customers appear willing to pay regularly for professional dog walking, pet sitting and boarding.

I will also consider Pune’s suitability for recruiting part-time sitters from colleges, residential communities and veterinary networks.

The research must use the same scoring definitions as every other city so that Pune does not receive an unfair advantage or disadvantage.

Output: Detailed Pune notes with evidence for every scorecard factor.

10:40–11:00 — Break

I will take a short break after completing the first two city assessments.

Before the break, I will save all collected links, notes and preliminary ratings so that no research is lost.

Output: No new project output is required, but all Bengaluru and Pune research should be saved properly.

11:00–11:40 — Research Mumbai

During this period, I will study Mumbai as a potential PetSaathi launch market.

I will identify apartment-dense locations where several pet owners may be served within a limited operating area.

I will assess customer spending capacity, existing premium pet-care services and the number of competing walkers, sitters and boarding providers.

I will also examine service-delivery challenges such as travel time, transport expenses, sitter availability and the distance between bookings.

The objective is to determine whether Mumbai’s potential customer demand is strong enough to justify its possible operating complexity.

Mumbai’s municipal corporation provides an official dog-licence application and renewal process, which can be used as one source for understanding local registration requirements and possible pet-ownership data.

Output: Mumbai city notes covering customer opportunity, competition, service density and operational risk.

11:40–12:20 — Research Gurugram

During this session, I will evaluate Gurugram as a possible launch city.

The research will focus on gated communities, premium residential societies, working professionals and customers who may require regular pet-care support.

I will identify existing pet-care companies, local independent caregivers, veterinary hospitals and grooming centres.

I will assess whether the city provides enough sitter supply and whether sitters can travel between shortlisted societies efficiently.

I will also examine the possibility of launching PetSaathi through apartment partnerships instead of targeting the complete city from the beginning.

The Municipal Corporation of Gurugram lists pet registration among its municipal services, so its official portal can be used to study local registration and compliance processes.

Output: Gurugram research notes focused on premium societies, customer affordability, sitter availability and partnership potential.

12:20–13:15 — Lunch Break

I will take a lunch break after completing research on four cities.

Before leaving, I will check that all sources, ratings and observations have been entered into the comparison sheet.

Output: No new project output is required during this period.

13:15–13:55 — Research Ahmedabad

During this session, I will study Ahmedabad using the same weighted city-selection factors.

I will examine pet-parent demand, apartment clusters, customer spending ability, local competition and the availability of caregivers.

I will identify veterinary clinics, groomers, trainers and pet shops that may become PetSaathi referral or emergency-support partners.

I will also evaluate whether the founder has useful personal or professional connections in Ahmedabad because a strong local network may reduce the cost of the first pilot.

Ahmedabad Municipal Corporation provides an official pet-dog registration service, which can support research into local compliance and pet-registration practices.

Output: Ahmedabad city notes covering market potential, operational feasibility and founder-network advantage.

13:55–14:35 — Research Surat

During this period, I will evaluate Surat as another possible launch city.

I will research pet ownership, premium societies, working households, local pet-care providers and customer willingness to use professional services.

I will examine whether Surat has lower competition while still providing enough demand for dog walking, pet sitting and pet boarding.

The availability of sitters, veterinary partners and groomers will also be checked.

Because Surat may offer a founder-network advantage, I will assess whether local contacts can help with customer interviews, society partnerships, sitter recruitment and pilot bookings.

Surat Municipal Corporation publishes forms related to pet dogs and also provides an online fauna-registration service, making its official portal useful for local research.

Output: Surat research notes covering demand, competition, sitter supply, partnerships and network advantage.

14:35–15:00 — Score All Cities

During this session, I will convert the research findings into numerical ratings.

Each city will receive a score from 1 to 5 for every evaluation factor.

I will calculate the weighted value using the following formula:

Weighted score = Rating ÷ 5 × Factor weight

For example, if a city receives 4 out of 5 for pet-parent density, which has a weight of 20%, its weighted score will be:

4 ÷ 5 × 20 = 16 points

I will follow two important scoring rules:

A high competitor-pressure score means competition is manageable or leaves a clear market gap.

A high operational-difficulty score means the city is easier to operate in.

Every score must include a short reason and an evidence source. I will not assign ratings only through personal opinion.

Output: A completed ranking of all six cities out of 100 points.

15:00–15:15 — Break

I will take a short break after completing the preliminary ranking.

This break will allow me to review the results with a fresh mind before selecting the final cities.

Output: No new output is required during this time.

15:15–16:00 — Select the Top Two Cities

During this session, I will select the strongest city as the primary launch option and the second strongest city as the backup option.

The primary city should have strong demand, sufficient customer spending capacity, available sitters, good pet-care partners and manageable operations.

The backup city should also be commercially suitable and should remain available if the primary city faces regulatory, recruitment or partnership difficulties.

I will not make the decision only by looking at the total score. I will also check whether any city has a serious weakness that could prevent a successful pilot.

For example, a city with strong demand but extremely difficult sitter recruitment may not be suitable for the first launch.

Output: One primary launch city and one backup city, supported by clear reasons.

16:00–17:00 — Select Three Areas in Each Top City

During this period, I will move from city-level research to neighbourhood-level research.

I will identify three suitable areas in the primary city and three suitable areas in the backup city.

Each area will be evaluated according to:

Number of premium apartments

Visible pet-owner activity

Nearby veterinary clinics

Nearby grooming services

Competitor presence

Sitter travel convenience

Society partnership potential

Average travel time between bookings

Founder or partner contacts

The ideal micro-market should contain several apartment societies within a small service radius.

Starting in a concentrated area will make sitter management, customer support, marketing and service quality easier.

Output: A shortlist of six micro-markets—three in the primary city and three in the backup city.

17:00–18:00 — Write the City Launch Hypothesis

During the final session, I will write a clear recommendation explaining where PetSaathi should launch and why.

The launch hypothesis will contain:

Recommended city

Recommended starting areas

Target customer group

Initial services

Expected sitter sources

Main competitive advantage

Important operational risks

Pilot success measurements

Backup city and backup areas

A suitable launch hypothesis may follow this structure:

“PetSaathi should begin its pilot in [selected city], focusing on [three selected areas], because these locations provide a strong combination of pet-parent demand, premium apartment density, customer spending ability, sitter availability and veterinary support. The pilot should begin with dog walking, pet sitting and pet boarding and should be validated through apartment partnerships and the first 50–100 bookings.”

The recommendation will remain a hypothesis until it is tested through customer interviews, sitter interviews, society discussions and a small paid pilot.

Output: A final and evidence-supported city launch recommendation.

Simple Explanation for Professor

“On Day 2, I will compare six possible launch cities for PetSaathi. I will research Bengaluru, Pune, Mumbai, Gurugram, Ahmedabad and Surat using the same weighted scorecard. After collecting information about pet demand, apartment density, customer spending, competition, sitter supply, veterinary support and operational difficulty, I will rank the cities. I will then choose one primary city, one backup city and three suitable launch areas in each. Finally, I will prepare a city launch hypothesis that can be validated through interviews and pilot bookings.”

City-by-city analysis

1. Bengaluru

What to expect

Bengaluru is probably the strongest first city if you want premium early adopters.

Why:

high tech-worker population

many apartment societies

high willingness to pay

strong pet-care startup activity

good market for dog walking and pet sitting

users are comfortable with app-based services

PetBacker already shows services in Bengaluru, including dog walking examples, so you should expect existing competition. PawSpace is also Bengaluru-based and has built structured services in India’s fragmented pet-care market.

Best areas to test

### Table 1

| Area | Why |
| --- | --- |
| Whitefield | IT workers, premium apartments |
| HSR Layout | young professionals, pet-friendly homes |
| Indiranagar | premium users, café/pet culture |
| Koramangala | startup crowd, high app adoption |
| Sarjapur Road | societies, families, IT workers |

What action to take

In Bengaluru, your first offer should be:

Verified daily dog walking for apartment residents.

Do not lead with cheap pricing. Lead with trust.

Expected pricing

### Table 2

| Service | Bengaluru test price |
| --- | --- |
| 30-min walk | ₹149–₹299 |
| 60-min walk | ₹299–₹499 |
| Pet sitting 1 hour | ₹299–₹599 |
| Boarding/night | ₹899–₹1,999 |

Bengaluru risk

High competition and high customer expectations. You need polished UX, fast support, and strong verification.

2. Pune

What to expect

Pune is a very strong second option. It has working professionals, students, premium residential areas, and a growing pet-care culture. It may be slightly easier than Bengaluru operationally.

IncrediPets reportedly gained traction in cities including Pune, Mumbai, Delhi, Gurgaon, Faridabad, Ghaziabad, and Chandigarh, showing active demand in these urban markets.

Monthly dog-walking plans + weekend boarding.

5. Ahmedabad

Ahmedabad is interesting if you want a Gujarat-first strategy.

Cons:

less dense startup/pet-care ecosystem than Bengaluru/Pune/Mumbai

PetBacker has Ahmedabad-specific listings for pet boarding, grooming, daycare, and dog walking, and Sulekha also lists Ahmedabad pet-sitting providers, so demand exists but may be fragmented.

| Pet sitting 1 hour | ₹199–₹399 |
| Boarding/night | ₹699–₹1,499 |

Ahmedabad risk

Demand may be real but not always visible online. Society partnerships and offline trust will matter more.

6. Surat

What to expect

Surat is a good future city, but I would not choose it first unless you have a strong local network.

Pros:

wealthy business families

rising premium lifestyle

lower competition

good for trust-led local brand

Cons:

less mature app-based pet-service demand

harder to find trained sitters

may require more offline selling

smaller early adopter pool than Bengaluru/Pune/Gurugram

Best areas to test

What action to take

In Surat, sell:

Family-trusted pet sitting and boarding with verified local caretakers.

Offline marketing will be more important than digital ads.

Day 3: Competitor and feature-gap research

Goal

Find where competitors are weak and where your product can win.

PetSaathi — Day 3 Competitor Analysis Schedule

09:00–09:30 — List the Competitors

During this session, I will prepare a list of businesses that provide services similar to PetSaathi.

The list will include direct competitors offering dog walking, pet sitting or pet boarding. It will also include broader pet-care platforms that offer grooming, training, veterinary support or pet transportation.

I will divide the competitors into three categories:

Indian online pet-care platforms

Local service providers in the selected launch city

International platforms that can be used as business-model references

A suitable starting list may include:

PetBacker

Snouters

PawSpace

Floofers

wagNbush

Petofy

ThePetNest

Pawricle

IncrediPets

Rover

Wag!

Pawshake

TrustedHousesitters

Local dog walkers

Local home-boarding providers

Local pet-sitting agencies

For each competitor, I will record its website, service city, main services and business type.

Output: A structured list of approximately 10–20 relevant competitors.

09:30–10:30 — Analyse PetBacker

During this session, I will study PetBacker in detail.

I will first check how a pet owner searches for a service and submits a booking request.

I will examine whether the customer receives fixed prices or multiple quotations from different service providers.

I will review the services available on the platform, such as pet boarding, house sitting, dog walking, grooming, daycare and pet transportation.

I will also study the following trust and safety features:

Sitter or provider verification

Customer ratings and reviews

Payment protection

Pet-care protection or insurance

GPS dog-walk reports

Photo and video updates

Customer support

Cancellation and refund process

I will select sample providers from two or three Indian cities and record their prices, completed bookings, ratings and verification status.

Prices must be recorded with the city, service duration and research date because different providers may charge different amounts.

Output: Detailed notes about PetBacker’s features, prices, trust system and marketplace process.

10:30–11:15 — Analyse Snouters

During this period, I will study the services and customer experience offered by Snouters.

I will examine the services listed on its website, including pet boarding, daycare, grooming and walking.

I will check how customers select a service, provide pet details and contact or book the company.

I will study its health and safety requirements, such as:

Vaccination requirements

Tick-and-flea rules

Hygiene standards

Pet behaviour requirements

Medication support

Trial sessions

Emergency procedures

I will check whether standard prices are displayed publicly or whether customers must contact the company for a quotation.

When a price is not displayed, I will write “quotation required” instead of estimating or inventing a price.

I will also record the company’s service cities, customer communication process and important boarding conditions.

Output: Clear notes about Snouters’ services, pricing method, safety rules and customer process.

11:15–12:00 — Analyse PawSpace

During this session, I will analyse PawSpace as a full pet-care service platform.

I will study its main services, such as:

Pet sitting

Pet boarding

Dog walking

Pet grooming

Dog training

Pet taxi

Pet relocation

Other supporting services

I will check whether the platform uses its own employees, external service providers or a combination of both.

I will compare the prices shown for different cities and different service durations.

For example, I will record whether the service is charged:

Per walk

Per hour

Per day

Per night

Per week

Per month

Through a subscription package

I will also check whether additional charges apply during festivals, for additional pets, food, transportation or special care.

Finally, I will study the PawSpace booking flow, provider information, customer updates, reviews and support options.

Output: Detailed PawSpace notes covering services, package prices, booking experience and business model.

12:00–12:45 — Analyse Local Providers

During this period, I will study small and independent pet-care providers in the selected city.

I will search Google Maps, Instagram and local pet-parent groups using terms such as:

“Dog walker near me”

“Pet sitter in [area name]”

“Dog boarding in [city name]”

“Home pet boarding near [society name]”

“Pet daycare in [area name]”

For each provider, I will record:

Business or provider name

Service area

Services offered

Starting price

Contact method

Google rating

Number of reviews

Instagram activity

Photos of the facility

Verification claims

Customer-update method

Emergency support

Response time

I will also note whether the provider accepts bookings through a website, telephone call, WhatsApp message, Instagram message or physical visit.

Local providers are important because they may not have advanced technology, but they may already have strong neighbourhood trust and loyal customers.

Output: Google Maps and Instagram research notes for approximately five to ten local providers.

12:45–13:45 — Lunch Break

I will take a lunch break after completing the individual competitor research.

Before the break, I will save all website links, screenshots, prices and important observations.

Output: No new project work is required during this period.

13:45–14:30 — Compare Trust Features

During this session, I will compare how every competitor creates trust between the pet parent and the caregiver.

I will prepare a trust-feature comparison table containing the following columns:

Government ID or KYC verification

Police or background verification

Provider interview

Home inspection for boarding

Vaccination-record requirement

Verified customer reviews

Meet-and-greet option

GPS dog-walk tracking

Photo and video updates

Pet report card

Masked communication

Secure online payment

Insurance or protection

Emergency veterinary support

Replacement sitter

Complaint-resolution process

I will mark every feature as:

Available

Partially available

Not available

Information not found

I will not mark a feature as available unless I find clear evidence on the website, app or booking process.

After completing the table, I will identify the trust features that are missing from most competitors.

Output: A list of important trust and safety gaps in the current market.

14:30–15:15 — Compare Pricing

During this period, I will create a pricing matrix for the main services.

The matrix will include:

Thirty-minute dog walk

Sixty-minute dog walk

Short pet-sitting visit

Four-hour pet sitting

Full-day pet sitting

Daycare

Overnight boarding

Monthly walking package

Every price must include the city, duration, pet type and date of research.

I will also check for additional charges such as:

Taxes

Platform fees

Festival surcharges

Additional-pet fees

Food charges

Transport charges

Medicine-administration charges

Cancellation charges

Tick or flea treatment charges

Prices with different durations must not be compared directly.

For example, a thirty-minute walk should be compared with another thirty-minute walk, not with a monthly walking package.

Where necessary, I will calculate the approximate price per walk, per hour or per night.

Output: A clear and fairly normalised competitor-pricing matrix.

15:15–15:30 — Break

I will take a short break after completing the pricing comparison.

This will allow me to review the booking processes with a fresh mind during the next session.

Output: No new project output is required.

15:30–16:15 — Compare the Booking User Experience

During this session, I will compare how easy or difficult it is to book a pet-care service on each platform.

I will document every important step in the user journey:

Open the website or app

Select a city or location

Select the required service

Enter the date and time

Add pet details

View available caregivers

Compare profiles and prices

Contact or message the caregiver

Make a booking request

Complete payment

Receive confirmation

Track the service

Contact customer support

Submit a review

I will record how many steps are required and whether the instructions are clear.

I will also check:

Mobile responsiveness

Page-loading speed

Search filters

Profile quality

Price visibility

Calendar availability

Form clarity

Error messages

Payment convenience

Booking confirmation

Cancellation access

Support visibility

I will not make a fake paid booking. I will analyse only the publicly available flow and stop before payment when necessary.

Output: User-experience notes showing the strengths and weaknesses of each booking flow.

16:15–17:00 — Compare Reviews and Complaints

During this session, I will study customer feedback from websites, app stores, Google Maps and social-media pages.

I will read both positive and negative reviews.

Positive reviews will help identify the features customers value, such as:

Caring service providers

Regular updates

Easy booking

Clean boarding facilities

On-time dog walkers

Helpful customer support

Negative reviews will help identify possible business risks, such as:

Late arrival

Sitter cancellation

Poor communication

Unclear pricing

Refund delays

Inadequate updates

Hygiene problems

Pet injury or illness

Incorrect service information

Weak complaint handling

I will focus mainly on recent and detailed reviews.

I will not treat one negative review as confirmed proof of a general problem. I will look for repeated complaint patterns across several reviews.

Where the platform labels reviews from completed bookings as verified, I will give those reviews greater importance.

Output: A list of repeated customer complaints, service risks and customer expectations.

17:00–18:00 — Write the Differentiation Plan

During the final session, I will decide how PetSaathi can provide stronger value than existing competitors.

The differentiation plan will not be based only on offering a lower price.

PetSaathi should compete through trust, transparency, local convenience and reliable service proof.

The proposed differentiation plan may include:

1. Strong Caregiver Verification

Every caregiver should complete identity verification, address verification, a video interview and basic pet-care training.

Police verification may be added where it is legally and operationally available.

2. Verified Apartment Network

PetSaathi can recruit caregivers within or near premium apartment societies.

This can reduce travel time and create stronger local trust.

3. Live GPS Walk Tracking

Pet parents should be able to view the dog-walk route, start time, end time, distance and important walk updates.

4. Pet Report Card

After every service, the caregiver should provide a report containing information about food, water, toilet activity, walking, behaviour and health concerns.

5. Photo and Video Proof

The caregiver should send service photos or videos at defined stages instead of providing only general text updates.

6. Emergency Veterinary Support

Each service area should have a clearly identified veterinary partner or emergency contact process.

7. Backup Caregiver System

When the assigned caregiver cancels, PetSaathi should try to provide a verified replacement instead of immediately cancelling the customer’s booking.

8. Verified-Booking Reviews

Only customers who completed a booking should be allowed to submit the main service rating.

9. Clear Pricing

The customer should see the service price, platform fee, additional charges and cancellation conditions before making payment.

10. Society-Level Service Plans

PetSaathi can offer walking and sitting packages specifically designed for apartment societies and residential communities.

These connected features will create PetSaathi’s feature moat.

A feature moat means a group of useful features and operational systems that competitors cannot easily copy as one complete experience.

Output: A clear differentiation strategy explaining why customers should select PetSaathi instead of another provider.

Simple Explanation for Professor

“On Day 3, I will study the main competitors of PetSaathi. I will analyse PetBacker, Snouters, PawSpace and local providers. I will compare their services, prices, trust features, booking processes and customer reviews. After identifying their strengths and weaknesses, I will prepare a differentiation plan for PetSaathi. The main advantage of PetSaathi will be stronger caregiver verification, live GPS tracking, pet report cards, clear pricing, emergency support and apartment-society partnerships.”

PetSaathi Competitor Matrix

Purpose of the Competitor Matrix

The competitor matrix helps us compare different pet-care businesses using the same criteria.

It shows what each competitor offers, where it operates, how it builds customer trust, how it charges customers and where PetSaathi can provide a better experience.

The matrix should be updated using official websites, application listings, customer reviews and direct enquiries.

Recommended Competitor Matrix

### Table 14

| Competitor | Services | Cities | Trust Features | Pricing | Possible Weakness | PetSaathi Opportunity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PetBacker | Pet walking, pet sitting, boarding, grooming and pet taxi | Multiple Indian and international locations | Provider profiles, reviews, secure payments, pet protection, GPS walk reports, photo/video updates and support | Prices vary according to provider, service and city | Service quality may vary between independent providers | Introduce more standardised verification, local society networks and consistent service rules |
| Snouters | Pet walking, boarding, daycare and grooming | Coverage must be confirmed city by city | Care-focused messaging, trained caregivers and professional supervision | Standard prices are not clearly visible for every service | Limited public information about verification, live tracking and pricing | Offer transparent pricing, live tracking and detailed caregiver verification |
| PawSpace | Pet sitting, boarding, dog walking, grooming, training, pet taxi and related services | Selected cities, with city-specific service pages | Background-checked or verified caregivers, regular updates, trained service providers and customer support | Prices are published for some services and locations | A broad service range may make the brand less focused on one core customer problem | Build a specialised society-first platform for walking, sitting and boarding |
| Local Instagram Sitters | Mainly pet sitting, home boarding and dog walking | Specific neighbourhoods or cities | Personal relationships, referrals and direct communication | Usually decided through direct messages or calls | Verification, pricing, cancellation rules and protection may not be standardised | Build a verified local network with secure booking, support and clear service standards |

Explanation of Every Column

1. Competitor

The competitor column contains the name of the company, platform or local service provider being studied.

Every competitor should have a separate row so that its services, prices and trust features can be compared clearly.

Simple explanation:

“This column tells us which pet-care company or provider we are analysing.”

2. Services

The services column shows the types of pet-care services offered by the competitor.

Possible services include:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Pet boarding

Pet daycare

Pet grooming

Dog training

Pet taxi

Pet relocation

Veterinary assistance

Only services confirmed through the competitor’s official website, application or verified business page should be included.

Simple explanation:

“This column shows what services customers can book from each competitor.”

3. Cities

The cities column shows where the competitor currently provides its services.

Instead of writing only “multiple cities” or “India,” the final research sheet should include exact city names whenever they are available.

For example:

Bengaluru

Mumbai

Pune

Delhi

Hyderabad

Ahmedabad

Surat

City coverage is important because a competitor may be strong in one location but unavailable in another.

Simple explanation:

“This column tells us where each competitor is currently operating.”

4. Trust Features

The trust-features column shows how the competitor makes customers feel safe when booking a caregiver.

The following features should be checked:

Government ID verification

Address verification

Police or background verification

Caregiver interview

Training or skill assessment

Customer reviews

Verified-booking reviews

Secure online payment

Pet-care protection

GPS dog-walk tracking

Photo and video updates

Emergency support

Replacement caregiver

Customer complaint support

A feature should be marked as available only when reliable evidence is found.

Simple explanation:

“This column explains how the company proves that its caregivers and services are safe and reliable.”

5. Pricing

The pricing column shows how much customers pay for the competitor’s services.

Pricing should be recorded using the same service duration and conditions.

For example:

Price for a 30-minute walk

Price for a 60-minute walk

Price for one pet-sitting visit

Price for full-day sitting

Price for one night of boarding

Price for a weekly or monthly package

The city, service duration, number of pets and research date should also be recorded.

When prices are not publicly available, the matrix should state:

“Quotation required” or “Price not publicly displayed.”

Prices should never be estimated without evidence.

Simple explanation:

“This column helps us understand how much customers pay and whether the prices are clear.”

6. Possible Weakness

The weakness column records areas where the competitor may provide an incomplete or inconsistent customer experience.

Possible weaknesses may include:

Limited verification information

No live GPS tracking

Unclear prices

No emergency support

Inconsistent caregiver quality

Weak cancellation support

No replacement caregiver

Limited service locations

Complicated booking process

No standard pet report card

A weakness should be written carefully.

It should be based on website research, customer reviews or booking-flow analysis rather than personal assumptions.

Simple explanation:

“This column identifies the problems or gaps that customers may experience with the competitor.”

7. PetSaathi Opportunity

The opportunity column explains how PetSaathi can provide stronger value than the competitor.

The opportunity should directly match the identified weakness.

For example:

If verification is weak, PetSaathi can provide KYC, address checks, interviews and police verification where available.

If tracking is missing, PetSaathi can provide live GPS dog-walk tracking.

If prices are unclear, PetSaathi can display complete prices before payment.

If service updates are limited, PetSaathi can provide photos, videos and pet report cards.

If local trust is missing, PetSaathi can create apartment-society caregiver networks.

If cancellations are a problem, PetSaathi can provide a backup caregiver system.

Simple explanation:

“This column explains what PetSaathi can do better than the existing competitor.”

Competitor-by-Competitor Explanation

PetBacker

PetBacker is a marketplace that connects pet parents with different independent pet-care providers.

It offers services such as dog walking, pet sitting, boarding, grooming and pet transportation.

Customers can compare provider profiles, reviews, quotations and available services.

Its trust system includes features such as secure payment, pet protection, GPS walk reports, service updates and customer support.

Prices are not fixed across the complete platform because individual providers may set different rates according to their city, experience and service.

A possible weakness is that the customer experience may vary between different independent providers.

PetSaathi can improve this area by introducing standard caregiver training, stronger verification rules, society-level networks and consistent service report cards.

Short explanation for professor:

“PetBacker has a strong marketplace and many trust features, but PetSaathi can provide more consistent local service through standard verification and apartment-society networks.”

Snouters

Snouters provides pet boarding, daycare, grooming and walking services.

Its brand communication focuses on treating pets like family and providing professional care.

The company mentions trained caregivers and continuous attention during services such as boarding.

However, detailed information about verification, live GPS tracking and standard prices is not clearly visible for every service.

PetSaathi can provide a more transparent experience by showing complete caregiver verification, live tracking, report cards and clear prices before booking.

Short explanation for professor:

“Snouters provides several important pet-care services, but PetSaathi can differentiate itself through better tracking, transparent pricing and detailed verification.”

PawSpace

PawSpace provides a wide range of services, including pet boarding, sitting, grooming, dog walking, training and pet taxi.

Its service pages mention verified or background-checked caregivers, training, customer updates and support.

Some PawSpace service pages also display weekly, monthly or quarterly package prices.

Because PawSpace provides many different services, its positioning is broader than only walking, sitting and boarding.

PetSaathi can begin with a more focused service model and build strong relationships within selected apartment societies.

Short explanation for professor:

“PawSpace is a broad pet-care service platform. PetSaathi can remain more focused by specialising in verified walking, sitting and boarding within apartment communities.”

Local Instagram Sitters

Local Instagram sitters are independent people or small businesses that promote pet sitting, boarding or walking through social media.

Customers may trust them through personal communication, referrals, photographs and local recommendations.

Their prices are normally discussed directly through telephone calls, WhatsApp messages or Instagram messages.

However, each provider must be studied individually because not every local sitter follows the same verification, payment, cancellation or emergency procedures.

PetSaathi can organise these independent caregivers into a verified network with standard prices, secure booking, customer support and service protection.

Short explanation for professor:

“Local sitters may have strong personal relationships with customers, but PetSaathi can add professional verification, secure payments and standard service rules.”

Final Differentiation Statement

PetSaathi should not compete only by offering lower prices.

It should compete through a complete trust system that includes:

Verified caregiver profiles

KYC and address verification

Police verification where possible

Live GPS dog-walk tracking

Photo and video updates

Pet report cards

Transparent pricing

Emergency veterinary support

Backup caregivers

Verified customer reviews

Apartment-society partnerships

Simple Explanation for Professor

“I created this competitor matrix to compare PetBacker, Snouters, PawSpace and local Instagram sitters. I compared their services, locations, trust features, prices and weaknesses. PetBacker has a strong marketplace, Snouters focuses on professional care, PawSpace offers many services and local sitters depend mainly on personal trust. PetSaathi can differentiate itself through stronger verification, live GPS tracking, transparent pricing, pet report cards, emergency support and apartment-society networks.”

Day 4: Customer persona and interview design

Goal

Prepare the exact people you will interview in Phase 1.

PetSaathi — Day 4 Customer Validation Schedule

Main Goal of Day 4

The goal of Day 4 is to prepare PetSaathi for direct customer research.

By the end of the day, I should clearly understand:

Who the main customers are

What problems they face

Which customers are most likely to use PetSaathi

What questions should be asked during interviews

How survey responses should be collected

How interested customers should be classified

How willingness to pay should be tested

How every customer conversation should be recorded

How customers should be contacted through WhatsApp

09:00–09:30 — Define Customer Segments

During this session, I will divide potential PetSaathi customers into five clear groups.

Customer segmentation is important because different pet owners have different problems, service needs and spending behaviour.

Segment 1: Working Professionals

These are pet owners who work from an office or have long working hours.

They may require regular dog-walking or short pet-sitting services during office hours.

Their main problem is that they cannot always give their pets enough exercise, attention or toilet breaks during the day.

Segment 2: Working Couples

These are households where both partners are employed.

They may need daily dog walks, daycare or pet sitting when both people are away from home.

Their main need is a dependable caregiver who can follow a fixed weekly schedule.

Segment 3: Frequent Travellers

These customers travel regularly for business, holidays, family events or weekend trips.

They may need overnight pet boarding, home pet sitting or multiple daily home visits.

Their main concern is finding someone trustworthy who will safely care for their pet while they are outside the city.

Segment 4: Senior Citizens and People with Limited Mobility

These pet owners may love and care for their pets but may find regular dog walking physically difficult.

They may need a reliable walker who can visit their home at a fixed time.

Their main concern is service reliability, caregiver behaviour and safety.

Segment 5: Festival and Emergency Customers

These customers may need temporary pet care during Diwali, weddings, medical emergencies or unexpected travel.

Their demand may not be regular, but they may urgently require safe boarding or pet sitting.

Their main concerns are immediate availability, emergency support and clear updates.

Output: Five clearly defined customer segments.

Simple explanation:

“I divided PetSaathi customers into five groups based on their lifestyle, pet-care needs and reasons for booking a service.”

09:30–10:15 — Define Three Core Pet-Parent Personas

During this session, I will create three detailed customer personas.

A persona is a simple profile representing one important type of customer.

It helps the development team understand the customer’s lifestyle, problems, expectations and buying behaviour.

Persona 1: The Busy Working Professional

Name: RahulAge: 28 yearsOccupation: Software developerLocation: Premium apartment in a metropolitan cityPet: Two-year-old LabradorRequired service: Daily dog walkingPreferred time: Between 1:00 PM and 3:00 PMMain problem: Rahul works long hours and cannot return home during the afternoon.Main fear: An unknown walker may mishandle his dog or fail to complete the walk.Expected features: Verified walker, live GPS tracking, photographs and a walk report.Payment behaviour: Willing to purchase a monthly plan if the walker is reliable.

Simple explanation:

“Rahul represents working professionals who need regular dog-walking support during office hours.”

Persona 2: The Frequent-Travelling Couple

Names: Neha and AmitAge: 31 and 33 yearsOccupation: Marketing manager and business consultantLocation: Gated residential societyPet: Four-year-old Indie dogRequired service: Home pet sitting or overnight boardingMain problem: They travel several times every year and cannot always leave their pet with relatives.Main fear: Their pet may feel anxious, receive poor care or face an emergency without support.Expected features: Verified sitter, meet-and-greet, video updates, feeding reports and emergency-vet access.Payment behaviour: Willing to pay more for a trusted and experienced sitter.

Simple explanation:

“Neha and Amit represent travelling pet parents who need safe sitting or boarding services.”

Persona 3: The Senior Pet Parent

Name: MeenaAge: 62 yearsOccupation: Retired teacherLocation: Established residential societyPet: Six-year-old BeagleRequired service: Morning and evening dog walkingMain problem: Walking the dog twice every day has become physically difficult.Main fear: The walker may arrive late, change frequently or behave carelessly.Expected features: A regular assigned walker, simple booking, telephone support and service confirmation.Payment behaviour: Prefers a fixed monthly package with predictable pricing.

Simple explanation:

“Meena represents senior pet owners who need dependable and easy-to-manage dog-walking support.”

Output: Three detailed customer personas representing PetSaathi’s main users.

10:15–11:00 — Define Customer Pains

During this session, I will create a pain map showing the major problems experienced by pet owners.

The pain map should cover practical, emotional, financial and trust-related problems.

Pain 1: Difficulty Finding Trusted Caregivers

Pet parents may find many service providers online, but they cannot easily confirm whether those providers are trained, verified or experienced.

Pain 2: Fear About Pet Safety

Customers may worry that their pet could escape, become injured, fight with another animal or receive improper treatment.

Pain 3: Lack of Service Proof

Pet parents may not know whether a full walk or sitting visit was actually completed.

They may receive only a short message without GPS data, photographs or a detailed report.

Pain 4: Last-Minute Cancellations

A caregiver may cancel shortly before the service, leaving the customer without a safe alternative.

Pain 5: Unclear Pricing

Customers may not know the complete cost until they speak directly with the provider.

Additional-pet fees, festival charges, transport charges or cancellation charges may not be clear.

Pain 6: Weak Emergency Support

Customers may not know what will happen if their pet becomes sick or injured during a service.

Pain 7: Inconsistent Caregivers

Some customers may receive a different caregiver for every booking.

This can reduce trust and increase the pet’s anxiety.

Pain 8: Complicated Booking

Customers may need to make several calls or send multiple messages before confirming availability.

Pain 9: Poor Communication

A caregiver may respond slowly or fail to provide updates during the service.

Pain 10: Lack of Local Availability

A reliable provider may not be available near the customer’s apartment or society.

Output: A complete pain map showing the main customer problems.

Simple explanation:

“I identified the practical and emotional problems that pet owners face when arranging pet care.”

11:00–11:15 — Break

I will take a short break after completing the customer-segment and pain analysis.

Before taking the break, I will save the customer personas and pain map in the project folder.

Output: No new project output is required during this period.

11:15–12:00 — Write the Customer Interview Questions

During this session, I will prepare a structured interview script for speaking with pet parents.

The purpose of the interview is to understand what customers currently do, what difficulties they experience and whether the problem is important enough for them to pay for a solution.

The interview should focus on the customer’s real experience rather than only asking whether they like the PetSaathi idea.

Pet Parent Interview Script

Introduction

“Hello, I am researching how pet parents manage walking, sitting and boarding services. This is not a sales call. I would like to understand your real experience and the problems you face. The conversation will take approximately 10 to 15 minutes.”

General Questions

What type of pet do you have?

What is your pet’s age and breed?

Which area or society do you live in?

Who normally takes care of your pet during working hours?

How often do you need help with pet care?

Problem Questions

Tell me about the last time you needed a dog walker, sitter or boarding service.

How did you search for the caregiver?

How long did it take to find someone?

What was the most difficult part of the process?

What made you trust or distrust that person?

Have you ever faced a cancellation, delay or communication problem?

Have you ever avoided travelling because you could not arrange pet care?

What is your biggest concern when another person handles your pet?

Current Solution Questions

Do you currently use a professional provider, friend, relative or domestic helper?

How satisfied are you with the current arrangement?

How much do you currently pay?

What type of updates do you receive during the service?

What happens if the regular caregiver is unavailable?

Feature Questions

How important is caregiver ID verification to you?

Would police or background verification increase your trust?

Would live GPS tracking be useful during dog walks?

Would you like to receive photographs, videos and a pet report card?

Would an emergency-vet support system influence your decision?

Would you prefer the same caregiver for repeated bookings?

Would you use a caregiver recommended by your apartment society?

Payment Questions

How much would you consider reasonable for a 30-minute dog walk?

How much would you consider reasonable for one night of pet boarding?

Would you prefer to pay per booking or through a monthly package?

Would you pay a small premium for stronger verification and service protection?

Would you be willing to book a discounted trial service?

Closing Questions

What would make you immediately reject a pet-care platform?

What is the most important feature PetSaathi should provide?

May I contact you when the pilot service becomes available?

Would you be comfortable recommending another pet parent for this research?

Output: A final customer-interview script that can be used consistently with every participant.

Simple explanation:

“I prepared interview questions to understand the customer’s previous experience, current solution, main concerns and willingness to pay.”

12:45–13:45 — Lunch Break

I will take a lunch break after completing the interview script and survey draft.

Before the break, I will test the form once to check whether all questions, options and sections are working correctly.

Output: No additional project output is required during this period.

13:45–14:30 — Define Lead Qualification Rules

During this session, I will classify potential customers as hot, warm or cold leads.

The classification should be based on the customer’s need, location, urgency, affordability and willingness to take the next step.

Hot Lead

A customer should be marked as a hot lead when most of the following conditions are true:

Owns a dog or cat

Lives in the selected pilot area

Currently needs walking, sitting or boarding

Has experienced a recent pet-care problem

Is actively searching for a caregiver

Accepts the expected price range

Is willing to try the pilot

Is willing to pay a small reservation deposit

Gives permission for a follow-up call

Recommended action: Contact within 24 hours.

Warm Lead

A customer should be marked as a warm lead when:

Owns a suitable pet

Lives in or near the selected service area

Needs pet care occasionally

Shows interest in the platform

Likes the proposed trust features

Is not ready to make a booking immediately

May require the service during future travel or festivals

Recommended action: Follow up when the pilot date, festival package or relevant service becomes available.

Cold Lead

A customer should be marked as a cold lead when:

Does not currently need outside pet care

Lives outside the planned service area

Does not accept the approximate price

Does not want to be contacted

Has a permanent family-based solution

Shows no interest in a trial

Recommended action: Do not repeatedly contact the customer. Keep only anonymous research insights where appropriate.

Simple Lead Score

The following points can be used:

Lives in pilot area: +20

Needs service within 30 days: +20

Has used paid pet care before: +10

Accepts target price: +15

Wants a trial: +15

Agrees to follow-up contact: +10

Accepts refundable deposit: +10

Classification

70–100 points: Hot lead

40–69 points: Warm lead

0–39 points: Cold lead

Output: Clear hot, warm and cold lead-classification rules.

Simple explanation:

“I created rules to identify which customers are ready to book, which customers may book later and which customers are currently not suitable.”

14:30–15:15 — Define the Payment Test

During this session, I will prepare a small test to check whether customers are genuinely willing to pay.

A customer saying, “This is a good idea,” does not always mean that the customer will make a real booking.

A payment test asks the interested customer to take a small financial action.

Option 1: Refundable Reservation Deposit

The customer pays a small amount, such as ₹99, to reserve priority access to the pilot.

The conditions must clearly state:

The payment is refundable.

The service is part of a limited pilot.

The final date and caregiver are not confirmed until communicated.

The customer can request a refund before confirmation.

No hidden charge will be added.

Option 2: Discounted Trial Walk

The customer books one discounted 30-minute dog walk.

Example offer:

“Book your first verified 30-minute dog walk at the pilot price of ₹99.”

The trial should include basic service proof such as start and end confirmation, a photograph and a short walk report.

Option 3: Boarding Reservation Request

Customers planning future travel can reserve a boarding consultation or meet-and-greet.

Payment should be collected only after clearly explaining the service, price, refund policy and availability.

Success Measurement

The payment test should record:

Number of people who viewed the offer

Number of people who requested details

Number of people who accepted the trial

Number of people who paid

Number of refunds

Main reasons for refusal

Output: A clearly defined refundable-deposit or discounted-trial test.

Simple explanation:

“I prepared a small payment test to check whether customer interest is strong enough to produce a real booking.”

15:15–15:30 — Break

I will take a short break after preparing the lead rules and payment test.

Output: No new project work is required during this time.

16:30–17:30 — Create WhatsApp Outreach Templates

The message should clearly identify the sender, explain why the customer is being contacted and give the customer an easy way to decline.

17:30–18:00 — Final Review

During the final session, I will review all Day 4 outputs.

I will check whether:

The three personas represent important PetSaathi users.
