Simple Explanation for Professor

“On Day 4, I prepared PetSaathi for customer validation. I first divided pet owners into five customer segments and created three main personas. I then identified their important problems and prepared interview questions and an online survey. After that, I created rules for classifying customers as hot, warm or cold leads. I also designed a refundable-deposit or discounted-trial test to measure real willingness to pay. Finally, I prepared a CRM tracking sheet and WhatsApp messages for contacting and following up with potential customers.”

Day 5: Sitter supply and verification model

Goal

Define how you will find, verify, train, and rate sitters.

PetSaathi — Day 5 Sitter Recruitment and Safety Schedule

Day 5 focuses on building the caregiver side of PetSaathi. The objective is to decide who can become a sitter, what services they may provide, how they will be verified, how much they will earn, and how unsafe applicants will be rejected.

Established marketplaces separate identity verification from broader background screening. PetBacker requires providers to list their service and verify identity documents with a selfie, but its policy also states that identity verification should not be treated as a complete background-record check. Rover separately uses profile review, background checks and profile badges. PetSaathi should therefore display precise labels such as “Identity Verified” or “Police Verification Completed” only when the relevant check has genuinely been completed.

09:00–09:30 — Define Sitter Personas

During this session, I will identify five types of people who may work as caregivers on PetSaathi.

Each sitter persona will include the person’s background, availability, suitable services, strengths, limitations and training requirements.

Persona 1: College Student and Part-Time Dog Walker

This sitter is a college student who loves animals and wants flexible part-time income.

The student may be available during mornings, evenings, weekends or college holidays.

The most suitable services for this persona are dog walking, short home visits and feeding visits.

The student should not immediately be approved for difficult dogs, medical cases or overnight boarding without additional experience and training.

Example:“A veterinary or engineering student who lives near a premium apartment area and can complete two or three walks every evening.”

Persona 2: Homemaker and Home-Boarding Host

This sitter is a homemaker who has time, sufficient space and family support to care for pets at home.

The sitter may provide daycare, overnight boarding and long-duration pet care.

Before approval, PetSaathi should inspect the home, confirm society or landlord permission, check doors and balconies, review other pets in the house and assess the number of animals that can be handled safely.

Example:“A pet-experienced homemaker who lives in a secure apartment and can board one vaccinated dog at a time.”

Persona 3: Retired Pet Lover

This sitter is a retired person who has previous experience caring for dogs or cats.

The person may prefer regular clients, fixed timings and calm pets.

Suitable services may include short walks, feeding visits, companionship visits and sitting for senior pets.

The physical ability of the sitter should be matched with the pet’s size, strength and behaviour.

Example:“A retired teacher who can provide morning walks and home visits for small or medium-sized dogs.”

Persona 4: Veterinary Student or Animal-Care Student

This sitter is studying veterinary science, animal husbandry or another animal-care subject.

The sitter may understand basic animal behaviour, hygiene and health warning signs.

Suitable services may include pet sitting, senior-pet care, post-treatment observation and pets with written feeding or medication instructions.

The sitter must not be presented as a qualified veterinarian unless they hold the necessary professional qualifications.

Example:“A veterinary student who can provide sitting services and recognise when a pet requires professional veterinary attention.”

Persona 5: Professional Walker, Trainer or Experienced Caregiver

This sitter already works with animals and may have experience in dog walking, training, boarding or grooming.

The person may be suitable for active dogs, large breeds, anxious pets or multi-pet households after a practical assessment.

Professional experience should still be verified through references, documents, demonstrations and customer reviews.

Example:“An experienced dog trainer who can manage structured walks and high-energy dogs.”

Output

Five clearly defined sitter personas with suitable service categories and approval requirements.

Simple explanation for professor:“I identified five types of caregivers who can join PetSaathi, including students, homemakers, retired people, veterinary students and experienced animal-care professionals.”

09:30–10:15 — Define Sitter Services

During this session, I will decide which services each approved sitter can offer.

A sitter should not automatically receive permission to provide every service. Service access should depend on the sitter’s experience, home conditions, practical assessment and training.

1. Dog Walking

The sitter collects the dog, completes a scheduled walk and safely returns the dog to the owner.

The service may include:

A 30-minute or 60-minute walk

GPS route tracking

Start and end photographs

Water and toilet updates

Distance and duration information

A short walk report

A walker must understand leash control, harness checking, escape prevention and safe interaction with other animals.

2. Home-Visit Pet Sitting

The sitter visits the pet owner’s home for a fixed period.

The visit may include:

Feeding the pet

Providing fresh water

Cleaning bowls or litter areas

Playing with the pet

Providing a toilet break

Sending photographs and videos

Completing a visit report

The sitter must follow the owner’s written instructions and respect the customer’s home and privacy.

3. Extended Pet Sitting

The sitter remains with the pet for several hours or overnight at the owner’s home.

This service may be suitable for pets that experience anxiety when left alone.

The sitter should provide scheduled updates and must not invite another person into the customer’s home without written permission.

4. Pet Daycare

The pet stays with the approved caregiver during the daytime and returns home in the evening.

The caregiver must have a safe environment, drinking water, rest space, supervision and a controlled process for introducing animals.

5. Pet Boarding

The pet stays overnight at the caregiver’s approved home or boarding location.

Boarding approval should require a home inspection, capacity limit, secure doors and windows, safe sleeping arrangements and a clear emergency plan.

The provider must comply with applicable local requirements. PetBacker similarly instructs service providers to ensure that their listed services comply with local laws and regulations.

6. Special-Care Add-On

Experienced and specifically approved sitters may support senior pets, pets with anxiety or pets with written care routines.

Medication support should only be allowed when instructions are clear and the sitter is trained and authorised to perform the task.

Output

A service-permission list showing what each sitter can and cannot provide.

Simple explanation for professor:“I defined different sitter services and decided that every caregiver should receive access only to the services for which they are trained and approved.”

10:15–11:00 — Define the Sitter Screening Process

During this session, I will create a complete verification and screening process.

Step 1: Basic Eligibility Check

The applicant must:

Meet the minimum legal working-age requirement

Have a valid telephone number and email address

Live inside or near the active service area

Have a smartphone with internet and GPS

Be able to communicate clearly with customers

Accept PetSaathi’s safety and conduct rules

Step 2: Identity Verification

The applicant should submit:

Government-issued identity document

Recent photograph

Live selfie or selfie with the document

Current residential address

Bank or payout details after approval

The document name, photograph and date of birth should match the application.

Identity verification confirms who the applicant is, but it should not be described as a complete criminal or police-background check. This distinction is also visible in PetBacker’s separate identity-verification and policy information.

Step 3: Address Verification

The applicant should provide acceptable proof of the current address.

For home-boarding applicants, the address should also be physically or virtually inspected.

Step 4: Police or Background Verification

Where legally and operationally possible, PetSaathi should request an official police-verification certificate or use an authorised background-check provider.

The platform should display the Police Verified badge only after checking a valid and current document.

If only identity verification has been completed, the profile must display Identity Verified, not Police Verified.

Rover similarly treats background checking as a separate profile process rather than the same thing as account creation.

Step 5: References

The applicant should provide one or two references.

Suitable references may include:

Previous pet-care customers

Employer or teacher

Veterinarian

Trainer

Apartment manager

Responsible community member

PetSaathi should contact at least one reference before final approval.

Step 6: Video Interview

A team member should interview the applicant and check communication, judgement, reliability and attitude toward animals.

Step 7: Knowledge Test

The applicant should complete a short test covering:

Dog and cat body language

Safe leash handling

Feeding instructions

Escape prevention

Emergency escalation

Customer privacy

Incident reporting

Platform rules

Step 8: Practical Assessment

Dog walkers should demonstrate:

Correct leash and harness use

Safe door entry and exit

Control without force

Safe road crossing

Response to pulling or distractions

Boarding hosts should complete a home-safety inspection.

Step 9: Training Completion

The applicant must complete the required PetSaathi training modules before activation.

Step 10: Trial or Probation Period

The first three to five bookings should be treated as probationary bookings.

During this period, the sitter’s punctuality, reporting quality, customer feedback and rule compliance should be reviewed carefully.

Output

A complete verification process covering eligibility, identity, address, background checks, references, interviews, training and probation.

Simple explanation for professor:“I prepared a multi-step screening process so that a person cannot become an active PetSaathi sitter by creating a profile only.”

11:00–11:15 — Break

I will take a short break after completing the sitter-service and screening definitions.

Before the break, I will save the screening checklist and service-approval criteria.

Output: No new project work is required during this period.

12:00–12:45 — Write the Sitter Interview Script

During this session, I will prepare standard questions so that every applicant is evaluated fairly.

Introduction

“Thank you for applying to become a PetSaathi caregiver. This interview will help us understand your pet-care experience, availability, safety knowledge and suitability for the services you selected.”

Background Questions

Please introduce yourself.

Why do you want to become a pet sitter or dog walker?

What animals have you cared for previously?

How long have you been caring for pets?

Have you received any formal animal-care training?

Experience Questions

Tell us about the most difficult pet you have handled.

Have you cared for an anxious, aggressive or senior pet?

How would you approach a pet that is afraid of you?

How would you introduce yourself to a new dog?

What pet sizes and breeds are you comfortable handling?

Safety Questions

What would you check before starting a dog walk?

What would you do if a dog escaped from its leash?

What would you do if another loose dog approached?

What would you do if a pet refused food?

What would you do if a pet became injured or ill?

When would you contact the owner, PetSaathi support or a veterinarian?

The CDC advises handlers not to disturb dogs that are eating, sleeping, hiding or showing fearful, sick or angry behaviour. These principles should be included in the interview and training assessment.

Reliability Questions

How will you ensure that you arrive on time?

What will you do if you cannot complete a confirmed booking?

How many bookings can you safely handle in one day?

Are you comfortable providing GPS, photo and report-card updates?

Customer-Service Questions

How would you handle an unhappy customer?

How would you protect the customer’s keys and personal information?

Would you allow another person to help with a booking?

How would you respond if a customer asked you to ignore a PetSaathi safety rule?

Scenario Questions

A dog begins pulling strongly toward traffic. What would you do?

A pet shows signs of breathing difficulty. What would you do?

The owner is not answering during an emergency. What would you do?

You notice that the pet’s vaccination information is incomplete. What would you do?

Another customer requests an overlapping booking. What would you do?

A customer offers direct cash payment outside PetSaathi. What would you do?

Closing Questions

Which services would you like to provide?

What is your expected earning per service?

What days and timings are you available?

Are you willing to complete training and a practical assessment?

Do you have any questions about the platform?

Output

A consistent sitter-interview script covering experience, safety, reliability and customer service.

12:45–13:45 — Lunch Break

I will take a lunch break after preparing the sitter application and interview script.

Before the break, I will review the form to remove unnecessary or repeated questions.

Output: No new project output is required during this period.

13:45–14:30 — Define Sitter Badges and Trust Levels

During this session, I will create clear sitter badges.

Badges should communicate verified facts and must not make unsupported safety claims.

Badge 1: Identity Verified

This badge confirms that PetSaathi has checked the sitter’s identity document and live photograph.

Badge 2: Address Verified

This badge confirms that the submitted address has been verified through an accepted process.

Badge 3: Interview Completed

This badge confirms that the sitter has completed a structured video or in-person interview.

Badge 4: PetSaathi Trained

This badge confirms that the sitter has passed the required safety and platform training.

Badge 5: Police Verification Completed

This badge should be displayed only when an acceptable and current official verification document has been checked.

Badge 6: Home Inspected

This badge is mainly for daycare and boarding providers whose service location has been reviewed.

Badge 7: Pet First-Aid Trained

This badge confirms completion of an accepted pet first-aid course.

The American Red Cross pet first-aid curriculum covers vital signs, breathing and cardiac emergencies, wounds, bleeding, seizures and preventive care, illustrating the type of topics a credible course should contain.

Badge 8: Trial Bookings Completed

This badge confirms that the sitter has successfully completed the probation period.

Badge 9: Top Rated

This badge may require:

Minimum number of completed bookings

Strong average customer rating

Low cancellation rate

No unresolved safety complaint

Consistent reporting and punctuality

Recommended Trust Levels

Level 1 — Applicant: Application submitted but not bookable.

Level 2 — Basic Verified: Identity and contact information verified.

Level 3 — Trained Sitter: Interview, test and training completed.

Level 4 — Service Approved: Approved for specific services after practical assessment.

Level 5 — Trusted Sitter: Successful bookings and strong performance history.

Output

A badge system that communicates the sitter’s actual verification and experience level.

14:30–15:15 — Define the Sitter Payout Model

During this session, I will decide how sitters earn money and how the platform receives revenue.

Option 1: Commission Model

PetSaathi keeps a percentage of every completed booking.

A possible pilot structure is:

Sitter payout: 80–85%

Platform share: 15–20%

Rover currently states that it applies a 20% provider service fee in its standard model, meaning the provider generally receives 80% of their booking earnings. This is a useful marketplace benchmark, but PetSaathi should test its own India-specific costs and sitter expectations.

Option 2: Lower Introductory Commission

PetSaathi may charge a lower commission during the first pilot to attract reliable caregivers.

After the platform provides regular bookings, support, tracking and protection, the standard commission can be reviewed.

Option 3: Fixed Payout

For standard society-based walks, PetSaathi may define a fixed customer price and a fixed sitter payout.

For example, the pricing structure should clearly show:

Customer service price

Platform fee

Taxes, when applicable

Sitter payout

Optional add-on fees

Sitter Earnings Rules

The payout model should explain:

When a booking becomes payable

What happens after customer cancellation

What happens after sitter cancellation

Payment for additional pets

Festival or peak-time earnings

Travel allowance, where applicable

Incentives for repeated bookings

Referral rewards

Dispute and refund handling

Recommended Payment Principle

Payment should be released after the service has been marked complete and the required tracking or report has been submitted.

The sitter should see the expected payout before accepting a booking.

Output

A clear payout system showing sitter earnings, platform commission and additional-payment rules.

Simple explanation for professor:“I prepared a transparent payout model so that sitters know their earnings before accepting a booking.”

15:15–15:30 — Break

I will take a short break after preparing the badge and payout systems.

Output: No new project work is required during this period.

15:30–16:15 — Define the Training Checklist

During this session, I will prepare the compulsory safety training for approved sitters.

Module 1: Animal Behaviour

The sitter should learn:

Basic dog and cat body language

Signs of fear, anxiety and aggression

Safe first contact

When not to touch or approach an animal

How to reduce stress

Module 2: Safe Dog Walking

The sitter should learn:

Harness and collar checks

Safe leash handling

Door and gate control

Road-crossing rules

Heat and weather precautions

Safe distance from unknown animals

Escape-prevention procedure

Module 3: Feeding and Home Visits

The sitter should learn:

Following written feeding instructions

Providing clean water

Avoiding unauthorised food

Hygiene and bowl cleaning

Litter or toilet-area cleaning

Protecting customer property

Module 4: First Aid and Emergencies

The sitter should understand:

Warning signs that require urgent escalation

Wounds and bleeding

Breathing difficulty

Seizures

Heat-related illness

Bite or scratch incidents

Contacting the owner and emergency veterinarian

Recording the incident accurately

Formal first-aid material should be prepared or reviewed with a qualified veterinarian. Established pet first-aid courses cover vital signs, preventive care, breathing emergencies, wounds, bleeding and seizures.

Module 5: Customer Communication

The sitter should learn:

Booking confirmation

Arrival notification

Clear photo and video updates

Professional language

Handling delays

Reporting unexpected behaviour

Avoiding unnecessary customer contact

Module 6: Pet Report Cards

The sitter should record:

Arrival and departure time

Walk duration

Food and water

Toilet activity

Mood and behaviour

Medication given under instructions

Photographs

Health concerns

Module 7: Platform and Privacy Rules

The sitter should learn:

Protecting customer addresses and keys

Not sharing customer photographs publicly

Not inviting unauthorised people

Not moving confirmed bookings off-platform

Using GPS only during the relevant service

Reporting incidents immediately

Output

A practical safety-training checklist covering animal care, service delivery, emergencies and customer privacy.

16:15–17:00 — Define the Approval Workflow

During this session, I will create the administrator’s step-by-step approval process.

Stage 1: Application Submitted

The system records the application and checks whether all mandatory fields are complete.

Stage 2: Document Review

The admin reviews identity, address and supporting documents.

Stage 3: Initial Eligibility Decision

Incomplete, duplicate or clearly unsuitable applications are placed on hold or rejected.

Stage 4: Reference Check

The admin contacts the selected reference and records the result.

Stage 5: Interview

The applicant completes the standard interview.

Stage 6: Knowledge Test

The applicant completes the safety and platform test.

Stage 7: Practical or Home Assessment

Walkers complete a handling assessment.

Boarding and daycare applicants complete a home inspection.

Stage 8: Training Completion

The applicant completes all required training modules.

Stage 9: Service-Level Approval

The admin approves only the services the sitter is qualified to perform.

For example, an applicant may be approved for dog walking but not boarding.

Stage 10: Profile Review

The admin checks:

Profile photograph

Introduction

Experience description

Services

Service area

Availability

Rates

Verified badges

Stage 11: Probation Activation

The sitter becomes visible for a limited number of trial bookings.

Stage 12: Full Activation

After successful trial bookings, the sitter receives full approved status.

Stage 13: Continuous Monitoring

PetSaathi monitors:

Ratings

Complaints

Cancellation rate

Late arrivals

Missed tracking

Report-card quality

Safety incidents

Output

A clear admin workflow from application submission to full activation and continuous monitoring.

17:00–18:00 — Define Sitter Risk Rules and Red Flags

During the final session, I will define the warning signs that may cause rejection, suspension or further investigation.

Application Red Flags

Applicant is below the required working age.

Identity information does not match the documents.

Documents appear edited, expired or false.

Applicant refuses identity verification.

Address details are inconsistent.

Applicant provides false experience or references.

Applicant wants to use another person’s account.

Applicant refuses training or the practical assessment.

Interview Red Flags

Uses harsh or violent handling methods.

Treats fear or aggression as disobedience only.

Cannot explain basic escape-prevention steps.

Shows poor judgement in emergency scenarios.

Refuses to provide customer updates.

Plans to leave pets unsupervised without permission.

Suggests using physical punishment.

Shows disrespect toward customers or animals.

Boarding-Home Red Flags

Unsafe balcony, gate, door or fencing.

No control over entry and exit.

Excessive number of animals.

No clean water or safe sleeping space.

Family members do not consent to boarding.

Existing animals show uncontrolled aggression.

Applicant hides information about other pets.

Society, landlord or local rules do not allow the proposed activity.

No emergency transport or veterinary plan.

Booking Behaviour Red Flags

Repeated lateness

Frequent last-minute cancellation

Missed GPS or check-in requirements

False photographs or service reports

Leaving before the service ends

Allowing an unauthorised person to complete the booking

Requesting customers to cancel and pay directly

Sharing customer information

Ignoring written pet-care instructions

Serious Incident Red Flags

The account should be immediately paused when there is a credible report involving:

Animal cruelty

Pet loss or escape

Serious injury

Theft

Harassment

Intoxication during service

Falsified verification

Deliberate safety-rule violation

The complaint should be investigated fairly, and emergency action should prioritise the pet’s safety.

Risk Action Levels

Level 1 — Coaching

Used for a minor first-time issue such as an incomplete report card.

Level 2 — Warning

Used for repeated minor problems, lateness or communication failure.

Level 3 — Temporary Suspension

Used when safety, verification or complaint investigation is required.

Level 4 — Service Restriction

The sitter may lose access to boarding or another high-risk service while remaining eligible for a lower-risk service.

Level 5 — Permanent Removal

Used for confirmed serious misconduct, cruelty, fraud, theft or dangerous rule violations.

Output

A risk-management policy containing application, interview, boarding, booking and serious-incident red flags.

Simple Explanation for Professor

“On Day 5, I designed the complete sitter-management system for PetSaathi. I first identified five types of sitters and decided which services each person can provide. I then created an application form, identity and background-verification process, interview questions, training checklist and trust badges. I also prepared the sitter payout model and the administrator’s approval workflow. Finally, I created red-flag rules for rejecting, suspending or removing unsafe caregivers. This process will help PetSaathi create a reliable and professional caregiver network.”

Day 6: Technical foundation and data architecture

Goal

Design your future software system before coding.

This is where your full-stack thinking matters most.

PetSaathi — Day 6 Technical Planning Schedule

Main Goal of Day 6

The goal of Day 6 is to convert PetSaathi’s business requirements into a clear technical structure.

By the end of the day, I should have:

A map of all system users

A list of database entities

Structured form fields

A complete booking-status flow

An administrator workflow

An analytics-event plan

A suitable no-code or low-code technology stack

Basic security and privacy rules

A list of future MVP modules

A technical Product Requirements Document outline

09:00–09:30 — Define User Roles

During this session, I will identify every type of person who will use the PetSaathi platform.

Each role will have different responsibilities and access permissions.

Role 1: Visitor

A visitor is a person who opens the PetSaathi website without creating an account.

The visitor can:

View the home page

Read service information

Search available service areas

View limited sitter information

Read pricing information

Open registration and login pages

The visitor should not be able to see private sitter documents, customer addresses or booking information.

Role 2: Pet Parent

A pet parent is a registered customer who books pet-care services.

The pet parent can:

Create and update a personal profile

Add one or more pet profiles

Search for available sitters

View sitter services, badges and reviews

Submit booking requests

Make payments

Send messages through the platform

View live walk updates

Receive pet report cards

Submit reviews

Raise complaints or support requests

A pet parent should only see their own pets, bookings, messages, payments and reports.

Role 3: Sitter Applicant

A sitter applicant is a person who has submitted an application but has not yet been approved.

The applicant can:

Complete the application form

Upload verification documents

Select preferred services

Add availability

Attend an interview

Complete training

View application status

The applicant cannot accept customer bookings until the administrator approves the required services.

Role 4: Approved Sitter

An approved sitter is a verified caregiver who can receive booking requests.

The sitter can:

Manage their public profile

Update service area and availability

View relevant booking requests

Accept or reject requests

Contact customers through masked chat

Start and complete services

Share GPS, photo and video updates

Submit pet report cards

View earnings and payout status

Report incidents

The sitter should only receive the minimum customer information needed to complete an accepted service.

Role 5: Administrator

The administrator manages platform operations.

The administrator can:

Review sitter applications

Check documents and interview results

Approve or reject service permissions

Monitor bookings

Manage customer complaints

Process refunds or payout adjustments

Review platform activity

Suspend unsafe accounts

Manage services, cities, prices and content

View operational reports

Highly sensitive actions should be restricted to authorised administrators.

Role 6: Support or Operations Executive

A support executive handles daily customer and sitter issues.

The support executive may:

View assigned support tickets

Contact relevant users

Help with booking problems

Record complaint notes

Arrange replacement sitters

Escalate serious incidents

The support executive should not automatically receive full access to identity documents, financial records or platform configuration.

Role 7: Veterinary or Service Partner

A veterinary, grooming or emergency partner may receive limited referral information.

The partner may:

Receive an authorised emergency request

Confirm service availability

Update referral status

Provide approved service information

The partner should not receive the customer’s complete account history.

Output

A role map showing who uses PetSaathi and what each role is allowed to view or change.

Simple Explanation

“I defined every type of PetSaathi user and decided what information and actions should be available to each role.”

Data-Type Rules

Each field should have a defined type.

Examples:

Names should use text.

Prices should use decimal numbers.

Dates should use date format.

Start and end times should use time or timestamp formats.

Yes-or-no fields should use Boolean values.

Status values should use controlled options.

Uploaded documents should store secure file references instead of raw files inside ordinary tables.

Validation Rules

The form should check:

Required fields are not empty.

Mobile numbers contain the correct number of digits.

Email addresses use a valid format.

End time is later than start time.

Pet age and weight are not negative.

Boarding capacity is not exceeded.

Files use accepted formats and sizes.

A user cannot submit the same active booking repeatedly.

Output

A structured mapping document showing how frontend forms create or update database records.

Simple Explanation

“I connected every form field to a specific database field so that the data can be stored consistently.”

11:00–11:15 — Break

Output: No new project output is required during this period.

11:15–12:00 — Define the Booking Lifecycle

During this session, I will define every stage through which a booking moves.

Status 1: Draft

The pet parent has started entering booking information but has not submitted the request.

Status 2: Requested

The customer has submitted the booking request.

The system should record the selected service, pet, date, time and sitter.

Status 3: Awaiting Sitter Response

The request has been sent to the selected sitter.

A response deadline should be displayed.

Status 4: Accepted

The sitter has agreed to complete the booking.

Status 5: Payment Pending

The customer must complete the required payment or reservation.

Status 6: Confirmed

The sitter has accepted and the required payment condition has been completed.

The customer and sitter receive confirmation.

Status 7: Upcoming

The confirmed booking is waiting for its scheduled date and time.

Status 8: Sitter En Route

The sitter has started travelling to the service location.

This status may be optional during the first manual MVP.

Status 9: Service Started

The sitter has checked in and begun the service.

GPS tracking should start only when required for the selected service.

Status 10: Service in Progress

The booking is currently active.

The sitter may send photographs, videos, messages and service updates.

Status 11: Service Completed

The sitter has finished the service and submitted the required report.

Status 12: Awaiting Customer Review

The customer can review the report and submit a rating.

Status 13: Closed

The booking has been completed, reviewed or automatically closed after the review period.

Alternative Booking Statuses

The flow should also support:

Rejected by sitter

Expired without response

Cancelled by customer

Cancelled by sitter

Replacement sitter requested

Replacement sitter assigned

Refund pending

Refunded

Disputed

Emergency hold

No-show

Failed payment

Main Status Flow

Draft → Requested → Accepted → Payment Pending → Confirmed → Service Started → Service Completed → Closed

Every important status change should record the user, date, time and reason.

Output

A complete booking-status lifecycle covering successful bookings, cancellations, replacements, payments and disputes.

Simple Explanation

“I defined every stage of a booking, starting from the customer’s request and ending when the service and review are completed.”

14:30–15:15 — Define the No-Code or Low-Code Stack

During this session, I will select tools that can support the first prototype and pilot.

The final tool should be selected according to cost, flexibility, security, integration support and the development team’s skills.

Option A: Simple Validation Stack

This option is suitable before building a complete product.

Landing page: Framer, Webflow or WordPress

Forms: Google Forms or Tally

Data tracking: Google Sheets or Airtable

CRM: Airtable, Notion or Google Sheets

Communication: WhatsApp and email

Prototype: Figma

Payments: Payment-link provider

Automation: Make or Zapier

This stack is suitable for customer interviews, sitter applications and manually managed pilot bookings.

Option B: No-Code Application Stack

This option is suitable for a functional web application without extensive custom coding.

Possible components include:

Frontend application: Bubble, Glide or Softr

Database: Built-in database, Airtable or MongoDB-backed authenticationentication: Platform authentication or MongoDB-backed authentication

File storage: Secure platform storage

Automation: Make, Zapier or platform workflows

Analytics: Google Analytics or Firebase Analytics

Payments: Indian payment-gateway integration

Option C: Developer-Friendly Low-Code Stack

This option is suitable when custom frontend development is required.

Possible structure:

Frontend: Next.js or React PWA

Styling: Tailwind CSS or a component library

Backend and database: MongoDB Atlas or Firebase

Authentication: Phone OTP and email authentication

File storage: Private cloud storage

Maps and tracking: Mapping SDK

Payments: Payment-gateway API

Analytics: Google Analytics or Firebase Analytics

Deployment: Managed web-hosting platform

Recommended Approach

The earliest PetSaathi pilot can begin with a landing page, forms, spreadsheet-based CRM and manual WhatsApp coordination.

After validating demand and completing initial bookings, the team can build the structured PWA and backend.

Output

A phased technology-stack recommendation for validation, manual pilot and future MVP development.

Simple Explanation

“I selected tools that allow PetSaathi to start with a low-cost manual pilot and later develop into a complete web application.”

15:15–15:30 — Break

I will take a short break after finalising the proposed technology stack.

Output: No new project work is required during this period.

15:30–16:15 — Define Security and Privacy Basics

During this session, I will establish the minimum data-protection rules for PetSaathi.

1. Collect Only Necessary Information

PetSaathi should collect only the information required for registration, verification, booking, payment and support.

Unnecessary personal information should not be requested.

2. Separate Public and Private Information

Public sitter information may include:

First name

Profile photograph

Service area

Experience

Approved services

Verified badges

Customer ratings

Private information should include:

Complete address

Identity documents

Police-verification documents

Bank details

Internal assessment notes

3. Role-Based Access

Pet parents should only access their own information.

Sitters should only access information required for their accepted bookings.

Support staff should only access assigned cases.

Administrators should receive permissions according to their responsibilities.

4. Secure Authentication

The platform should support secure login through approved authentication methods.

Important administrator accounts should use stronger protection, such as multi-factor authentication.

5. Protect Uploaded Documents

Verification documents should be stored privately.

Public URLs should not expose identity documents.

Access should be time-limited or permission-controlled.

6. Protect Payment Information

PetSaathi should use an authorised payment provider.

The platform should not directly store complete card numbers, UPI PINs or other payment credentials.

7. Data Encryption

Sensitive information should be protected during transmission and storage using the security features of the selected platform.

8. Consent and Privacy Notice

Users should be told:

What information is being collected

Why it is required

How it will be used

Who may receive it

How long it may be retained

How the user can raise a privacy request

9. GPS Privacy

GPS tracking should start only when the relevant service begins.

It should stop when the service ends.

Location history should not remain publicly available.

10. Access Logs

Important actions should be logged, including:

Administrator access to sensitive records

Document verification

Account suspension

Refund approval

Changes to booking status

11. Data Retention and Deletion

The team should define how long it keeps:

Inactive profiles

Verification documents

Booking history

GPS records

Support tickets

Payment references

Data should not be retained indefinitely without a valid operational or legal reason.

12. Incident Response

The platform should have a process for:

Identifying a security issue

Restricting affected access

Preserving evidence

Informing responsible team members

Investigating the incident

Correcting the weakness

Completing required legal or user communication

Output

A basic security and privacy checklist covering access control, documents, payments, GPS data, consent and incident handling.

Simple Explanation

“I defined the basic rules required to protect customer, sitter, pet, payment and location information.”

16:15–17:00 — Define Future MVP Modules

During this session, I will divide the future application into clear modules.

Module 1: Authentication and User Accounts

This module will provide registration, login, password recovery, OTP and role management.

Module 2: Pet Profiles

This module will store pet identity, behaviour, health and care instructions.

Module 3: Sitter Onboarding

This module will handle applications, document uploads, interviews, training and approval.

Module 4: Search and Discovery

This module will help customers search by service, location, date, price, rating and verification level.

Module 5: Booking Management

This module will create, confirm, modify, cancel and complete bookings.

Module 6: Availability and Scheduling

This module will manage sitter calendars, blocked time and booking conflicts.

Module 7: Payments and Payouts

This module will manage customer payments, refunds, platform fees and sitter earnings.

Module 8: Communication

This module will provide masked chat, notifications and service updates.

Module 9: Live Tracking

This module will manage check-in, GPS tracking, route information and service duration.

Module 10: Pet Report Cards

This module will collect food, water, toilet, walk, mood and health updates.

Module 11: Reviews and Ratings

This module will allow verified customers to review completed services.

Module 12: Admin Dashboard

This module will manage users, sitters, bookings, payments, complaints and configuration.

Module 13: Support and Incident Management

This module will manage help requests, emergency cases, evidence and resolutions.

Module 14: Analytics and Reporting

This module will show booking funnels, customer behaviour, sitter performance and business metrics.

Future Advanced Modules

Later phases may include:

AI-based sitter matching

Society partnership dashboard

Subscription plans

Emergency-vet integration

Pet insurance add-ons

Referral system

Multilingual support

Android and iOS applications

Dynamic pricing

Fraud and risk detection

Output

A prioritised list of functional modules for future MVP development.

Simple Explanation

“I divided the future PetSaathi product into separate modules so that development can be planned and completed step by step.”

A PRD explains what should be built, why it is required and how success will be measured.

As a sitter, I want to update my availability so that I receive suitable booking requests.

PetSaathi — Day 7 Operations, Safety and Phase 1 Launch Schedule

09:00–09:45 — Define Service Rules

During this session, I will define what is allowed and what is not allowed during every PetSaathi service.

Clear service rules will protect pets, pet parents, sitters and the platform.

General Rules for All Services

Every service should follow these basic rules:

Only an approved PetSaathi sitter may complete the booking.

The sitter must follow the instructions written in the pet profile.

The sitter must arrive and complete the service within the confirmed time.

The sitter must not bring an unauthorised person to the customer’s home.

The sitter must not transfer the booking to another person.

The sitter must not use physical punishment or harmful handling methods.

The sitter must not share the customer’s address, photographs or private information.

The sitter must report delays, incidents or health concerns immediately.

The sitter must provide the required photographs, tracking information or report card.

The sitter must not make medical decisions outside their training or authority.

Dog-Walking Rules

Allowed

The sitter may:

Complete a 30-minute or 60-minute walk.

Use the owner-approved collar, harness and leash.

Start GPS tracking when the walk begins.

Provide toilet, water and behaviour updates.

Send start and end photographs.

Follow the route and instructions agreed with the owner.

Not Allowed

The sitter must not:

Remove the leash in an open or unsecured location.

Take the dog to an unknown dog park without permission.

Allow another person to walk the dog.

Walk more dogs than the approved limit.

Use a choke, prong or harmful training tool unless legally permitted, owner-authorised and handled by a properly qualified professional.

Continue the walk when the dog shows a serious health or safety concern.

Pet-Sitting Rules

Allowed

The sitter may:

Feed the pet according to written instructions.

Replace drinking water.

Clean feeding bowls or litter areas.

Play with and supervise the pet.

Provide approved toilet breaks.

Give authorised updates to the customer.

Not Allowed

The sitter must not:

Invite friends or family into the customer’s home.

Open private cupboards or rooms without a service-related reason.

give unapproved food or medication.

Leave the property unlocked.

Share photographs of the customer’s home publicly.

Stay beyond the approved service period without permission.

Pet-Boarding Rules

Allowed

The boarding host may:

Accept only the number of pets approved during the home inspection.

Keep the pet in the approved boarding location.

Follow feeding, walking and sleeping instructions.

Provide regular photographs and videos.

Separate pets when safe introductions are not possible.

Contact the owner and veterinary partner during an emergency.

Not Allowed

The boarding host must not:

Move the pet to another property without permission.

Accept more animals than the approved capacity.

Mix unfamiliar animals without a safe introduction.

Leave the pet unsupervised for an unreasonable period.

Use unsafe balconies, terraces, gates or rooms.

Hide the presence of other pets or boarded animals.

Animal Welfare Board of India guidance advises pet owners and caregivers to maintain pet health, cleanliness, vaccination and responsible care. These principles should form part of PetSaathi’s service rules.

Output

A clear document showing what sitters may and may not do during walking, sitting and boarding services.

Simple Explanation

“I prepared service rules so that every sitter follows the same safety, privacy and quality standards.”

09:45–10:30 — Define the Pricing Hypothesis

During this session, I will create temporary trial prices for the PetSaathi pilot.

These prices are only hypotheses. They must be tested through customer interviews, competitor research and actual trial bookings.

Current marketplace examples show that prices vary significantly by provider, city, pet size and service conditions. PetBacker currently displays examples such as ₹100–₹300 per walk and around ₹1,200 per boarding night on its Indian homepage. PawSpace publishes some city-specific packages, including ₹899 for up to 24 hours of dog boarding and ₹849–₹1,149 for different pet-sitting durations on selected pages.

Proposed Pilot Prices

### Table 22

| Service | Proposed Trial Price | Possible Regular Price |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 30-minute dog walk | ₹149 | ₹199–₹249 |
| 60-minute dog walk | ₹249 | ₹299–₹399 |
| One-hour pet-sitting visit | ₹249 | ₹299–₹399 |
| Four-hour pet sitting | ₹599 | ₹699–₹899 |
| Full-day pet sitting | ₹899 | ₹999–₹1,399 |
| Overnight pet boarding | ₹699 | ₹799–₹1,199 |
| Additional pet | ₹100–₹250 | Based on service |
| Festival surcharge | No surcharge during first test | 15–30% after validation |

Pricing Rules

The displayed price should clearly show:

Service duration

Number of pets included

Sitter payout

Platform fee

Taxes, when applicable

Additional-pet charge

Travel charge, when applicable

Festival surcharge

Cancellation conditions

The customer should see the complete payable amount before confirming the booking.

Pricing Test

I will ask customers:

Is the trial price affordable?

Does the price feel too low, reasonable or too high?

Would they pay more for stronger verification?

Would they prefer payment per booking or a monthly plan?

Would they book at the regular price after a successful trial?

Output

A trial-pricing table ready to be tested with customers.

Simple Explanation

“I created temporary prices for the pilot, but these prices will be confirmed only after customer testing.”

10:30–11:15 — Define Cancellation and Refund Rules

During this session, I will prepare a simple cancellation and refund policy.

The policy should protect customers while also compensating sitters when customers cancel at the last minute.

Established pet-care marketplaces commonly provide full or partial refunds according to how early the booking is cancelled. PetBacker, for example, states that some last-minute cancellations may attract a 50% cancellation charge, while provider cancellations may result in refunds for services not delivered. It also attempts to arrange replacement providers in qualifying situations.

Customer Cancellation Rules

More Than 24 Hours Before the Service

The customer receives a full refund, excluding only any clearly disclosed non-refundable payment-processing cost where legally permitted.

Between 6 and 24 Hours Before the Service

The customer receives a 50% refund.

The remaining amount may be divided between sitter compensation and administrative costs according to the final policy.

Less Than 6 Hours Before the Service

The booking may be treated as a late cancellation.

The customer may not receive a cash refund, but PetSaathi may provide a partial credit in genuine emergency situations.

Customer No-Show

If the sitter arrives but cannot contact the customer or access the pet after following the waiting procedure, the booking may be marked as a customer no-show.

The sitter should receive the approved payout after providing arrival proof.

Sitter Cancellation Rules

If the sitter cancels:

PetSaathi should first search for a verified replacement.

The customer must be informed immediately.

The replacement should not cost more without customer approval.

If no suitable replacement is found, the customer receives a full refund.

Repeated sitter cancellations should affect the sitter’s performance status.

A serious last-minute cancellation may cause temporary suspension.

Service Not Delivered

The customer should receive a full refund when:

The sitter does not arrive.

The assigned sitter is not the approved person.

The service cannot begin because of a platform or sitter failure.

PetSaathi cancels the booking before the service.

The provider fails to deliver the confirmed service.

Service Complaint

When a customer reports poor or incomplete service:

The complaint should be recorded.

Payment or payout may be temporarily placed on hold.

Photos, GPS information, messages and reports should be reviewed.

Both customer and sitter should be allowed to explain.

PetSaathi should decide whether a full refund, partial refund, credit or no refund is appropriate.

The reason for the decision should be recorded.

The Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules, 2020 apply transparency and fair-practice requirements to e-commerce entities. PetSaathi should display its cancellation, refund, complaint and grievance processes clearly rather than hiding them inside the booking flow.

Output

A basic cancellation and refund policy covering customers, sitters, no-shows, replacements and complaints.

Simple Explanation

“I created clear refund rules so that customers and sitters know what happens when either side cancels a booking.”

11:15–11:30 — Break

I will take a short break after completing the service, pricing and refund policies.

Before the break, I will check that the prices and cancellation rules do not contradict each other.

Output: No new work is required during this period.

11:30–12:15 — Define the Emergency Protocol

During this session, I will prepare a step-by-step safety procedure for pet emergencies.

The sitter should not attempt to diagnose or independently treat a serious medical condition.

The American Veterinary Medical Association advises contacting the regular veterinarian or local veterinary emergency hospital during an emergency so the facility can prepare for the pet’s arrival. It also states that first aid should be followed by veterinary care.

Emergency Examples

The protocol may be activated when:

A pet escapes or becomes lost.

A pet is hit by a vehicle.

A pet is bitten by another animal.

A pet experiences breathing difficulty.

A pet becomes unconscious.

A pet has a seizure.

A pet has serious bleeding.

A pet shows signs of poisoning.

A pet experiences possible heatstroke.

A person is bitten or injured.

A fire, flood or unsafe property condition occurs.

Emergency Response Steps

Step 1: Protect Immediate Safety

The sitter should move the pet away from traffic, aggressive animals, fire or another immediate danger when it can be done safely.

The sitter should not place themselves or another person in unnecessary danger.

Step 2: Contact PetSaathi Emergency Support

The sitter should contact the assigned emergency-support number.

The support executive should open an incident record immediately.

Step 3: Contact the Pet Parent

The sitter or support executive should call the pet parent.

If the owner cannot be contacted, the team should call the registered emergency contact.

Step 4: Contact the Veterinarian

For a medical emergency, the sitter should contact:

The pet’s registered veterinarian

The nearest approved emergency clinic

Another available veterinary hospital when the preferred clinic is unavailable

Step 5: Follow Professional Instructions

The sitter should follow instructions provided by the veterinarian or emergency professional.

First aid should only be provided within the sitter’s actual training.

Step 6: Arrange Safe Transport

The sitter or operations team should arrange approved transport to the veterinary clinic when necessary.

The pet should be transported using an appropriate leash, carrier, crate or safe restraint.

Step 7: Record Evidence

The sitter should record:

Incident time

Location

What happened

Pet’s condition

Actions taken

People contacted

Veterinary clinic

Photographs, when appropriate

Expenses incurred

Final status

Step 8: Post-Incident Review

After the emergency:

The pet parent should receive a written report.

The booking should be placed under review.

Payment and payout decisions should be reviewed.

The sitter’s actions should be assessed.

Preventive improvements should be recorded.

Lost-Pet Protocol

When a pet escapes:

Do not hide or delay reporting the incident.

Inform PetSaathi and the owner immediately.

Confirm the last known location.

Search the immediate safe area.

Check building exits, guards, CCTV and nearby routes.

Contact local animal-support resources when required.

Continue updates at fixed intervals.

Record the complete timeline.

Output

A written emergency-response document for health incidents, injuries, escapes and property emergencies.

Simple Explanation

“I created a safety procedure that explains who should be contacted and what actions should be taken during a pet emergency.”

12:15–13:00 — Define the Legal and Compliance Checklist

During this session, I will prepare a checklist of legal and compliance areas that must be reviewed before accepting public paid bookings.

This checklist is for project planning and should be reviewed by a qualified lawyer, chartered accountant and relevant local authority before launch.

1. Business Registration

Confirm:

The legal entity operating PetSaathi

Registered business address

Authorised representatives

Required business registrations

Contracts between the platform and service providers

2. Tax and Accounting

Confirm:

GST applicability

Correct service classification

Invoice requirements

Platform commission accounting

Sitter payout records

Refund and credit records

Tax-deduction responsibilities, when applicable

The official GST portal provides registration and service-classification procedures, but the exact tax treatment should be confirmed with a qualified tax professional.

3. Consumer Protection

Prepare:

Clear service descriptions

Complete pricing

Cancellation policy

Refund policy

Complaint process

Grievance contact

Provider information

Terms and conditions

Prohibition of misleading claims

Protection against dark or manipulative booking patterns

India’s Department of Consumer Affairs maintains the Consumer Protection Act and the Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules, 2020 as key sources for online marketplace compliance.

4. Data Protection and Privacy

Prepare:

Privacy notice

Consent records

Reason for collecting each data field

Access-control rules

Secure document storage

GPS-location rules

Data-retention periods

Correction and deletion process

Security-incident procedure

Contracts with technology and payment vendors

India’s Digital Personal Data Protection Act, 2023 regulates processing of digital personal data for lawful purposes. The Digital Personal Data Protection Rules, 2025 use a staggered commencement schedule, so PetSaathi should track when each applicable provision becomes effective and design the platform for compliance in advance.

5. Sitter Agreements

The sitter agreement should define:

Independent service-provider relationship

Approved services

Verification requirements

Safety responsibilities

Customer-data confidentiality

Payout calculation

Cancellation rules

Complaint investigations

Suspension and removal rules

Prohibition on unauthorised substitutes

Incident-reporting duties

6. Customer Terms

Customer terms should define:

Accuracy of pet information

Vaccination disclosure

Behaviour disclosure

Booking and payment rules

Cancellation and refund rules

Emergency authority

Complaint process

Prohibited use of the platform

7. Local Boarding and Trade Requirements

For every launch city, check:

Municipal trade licences

Home-business restrictions

Apartment or society permissions

Landlord permission

Local boarding rules

Animal-welfare requirements

Capacity and hygiene requirements

Record-keeping or inspection requirements

Local requirements may differ between cities and states, so approval in one city should not be assumed to apply in another.

8. Insurance and Protection

Investigate:

Public liability insurance

Professional liability

Pet injury coverage

Property-damage coverage

Employee or contractor accident coverage

Cybersecurity insurance

Booking-protection limits

9. Veterinary Partnerships

Prepare written arrangements covering:

Emergency contact procedure

Clinic availability

Payment responsibility

Transport support

Owner consent

Information sharing

Medical decision authority

Output

A compliance checklist covering business, tax, consumer, privacy, animal-care, contractual and local requirements.

“I prepared a checklist of the legal, tax, privacy, consumer and animal-care requirements that must be checked before the paid launch.”
