17:15–18:00 — Conduct the Go/No-Go Review

During the final session, I will decide whether PetSaathi is ready to begin Phase 1.

Go Decision

The project can proceed when:

The landing page is complete.

Forms have been tested.

Service rules are documented.

Trial prices are defined.

Cancellation and refund rules are clear.

The emergency process is ready.

At least one veterinary support contact has been identified.

Customer outreach messages are ready.

Sitter screening has been defined.

The CRM sheet and KPI dashboard are working.

No major legal or operational blocker has been identified.

Conditional Go Decision

The project may proceed with restrictions when:

Only one service is operationally ready.

Only one micro-market has sufficient customers.

Sitter supply is limited.

Boarding requirements are not yet confirmed.

Payment integration is not ready.

For example, PetSaathi may begin with manually managed dog-walking trials while delaying boarding until home inspections and local compliance checks are complete.

No-Go Decision

The pilot should be delayed when:

No qualified sitters are available.

Emergency veterinary support is unavailable.

Service or refund rules are incomplete.

Required local permissions have not been checked.

Customer data cannot be stored safely.

The platform cannot respond to complaints.

Trial payments would be collected without clear terms.

Serious safety concerns remain unresolved.

Final Review Questions

Is the customer problem clearly validated?

Are customers willing to try or pay?

Are qualified sitters available?

Can bookings be managed safely?

Are prices understandable?

Are cancellation and refund rules ready?

Can emergencies be handled?

Is customer data protected?

Can success be measured?

Is the team ready to support the first bookings?

Output

A documented decision to proceed, proceed with restrictions or delay Phase 1.

Simple Explanation

“I reviewed every customer, sitter, safety, legal and technical requirement before deciding whether the PetSaathi pilot should begin.”

Simple Explanation for Professor

“On Day 7, I completed the final preparation for the PetSaathi Phase 1 pilot. I first defined the rules for dog walking, pet sitting and pet boarding. I then created trial prices and cancellation and refund policies. After that, I prepared an emergency protocol and a legal compliance checklist. I finalised the landing page, customer forms, sitter forms and outreach messages. I also created a KPI dashboard to measure customer demand, sitter supply and booking quality. Finally, I completed a go or no-go review to decide whether the pilot is ready to launch.”

PetSaathi — Phase 0 Legal, Safety and Business Preparation

1. Legal and Safety Checklist

Before PetSaathi accepts paid bookings, the platform should prepare the following legal and operational documents.

These documents will not completely remove business risk. However, they will clearly explain the responsibilities of PetSaathi, pet parents and sitters.

1.1 Terms and Conditions

The Terms and Conditions document explains the main rules for using PetSaathi.

It should clearly state:

Who can create a PetSaathi account

Who can apply as a sitter

How services can be searched and booked

How prices and platform fees are calculated

When a booking becomes confirmed

What customers and sitters are responsible for

What actions are prohibited

How cancellations and refunds are handled

How complaints and disputes are managed

When an account may be suspended or removed

Which law and jurisdiction apply to the agreement

The terms should be written in clear language that an ordinary customer can understand.

Important conditions should not be hidden in small text.

The customer should be able to read the terms before making a booking or payment.

Why it is required:

The Terms and Conditions document creates a common set of rules for every platform user.

It helps reduce confusion about bookings, payments, cancellations, safety and account responsibilities.

Simple explanation:

“Terms and Conditions explain how PetSaathi works and what every user is allowed or required to do.”

1.2 Refund and Cancellation Policy

The refund policy explains what happens when a booking is cancelled, rejected or not completed.

It should cover:

Cancellation by the customer

Cancellation by the sitter

Sitter no-show

Customer no-show

Failed payment

Replacement sitter

Service not delivered

Service stopped because of a safety problem

Partial service completion

Customer complaint

Refund processing time

Refund method

Platform credits, where applicable

A possible pilot structure may be:

Customer Cancels More Than 24 Hours Before Service

The customer may receive a full refund, subject to the clearly disclosed payment-processing rules.

Customer Cancels Between 6 and 24 Hours Before Service

The customer may receive a partial refund.

Part of the remaining amount may compensate the sitter for reserving the time.

Customer Cancels Less Than 6 Hours Before Service

The booking may be treated as a late cancellation.

A refund, partial credit or no refund may apply according to the final approved policy.

Sitter Cancels

PetSaathi should first try to arrange a verified replacement sitter.

If a suitable replacement is not available, the customer should receive a full refund for the undelivered service.

Sitter Does Not Arrive

The customer should receive a full refund.

The incident should also affect the sitter’s reliability record.

Why it is required:

A written refund policy prevents confusion and reduces payment disputes.

It allows customers and sitters to understand the financial result of a cancellation before confirming the booking.

Simple explanation:

“The refund policy clearly explains when customers receive a full refund, partial refund or no refund.”

1.3 Sitter Agreement

The sitter agreement defines the relationship between PetSaathi and each approved caregiver.

It should include:

Approved service categories

Service area

Verification requirements

Training requirements

Availability responsibilities

Booking acceptance rules

Punctuality expectations

Safety responsibilities

Customer communication rules

Privacy responsibilities

GPS and report-card requirements

Sitter payout calculation

Platform commission

Cancellation rules

Incident-reporting procedure

Complaint-investigation procedure

Suspension and termination rules

Prohibition on unauthorised substitute sitters

Prohibition on moving bookings outside the platform

The agreement should clearly state whether the sitter is being engaged as an independent service provider, employee or another legally recognised category.

This classification should be reviewed by a lawyer because the written label alone may not determine the actual legal relationship.

Why it is required:

The sitter agreement tells caregivers exactly how services must be delivered.

It also gives PetSaathi a documented basis for warnings, service restrictions, suspension or removal.

Simple explanation:

“The sitter agreement defines the sitter’s work, payment, safety rules and professional responsibilities.”

1.4 Customer Declaration

The customer declaration collects important and truthful information about the pet.

The customer should confirm:

The pet’s name, type, breed and age

Vaccination status

Medical conditions

Current medication

Allergies

Behaviour around strangers

Behaviour around children

Behaviour around other animals

Bite or aggression history

Escape history

Fear or anxiety triggers

Feeding instructions

Walking instructions

Veterinary contact

Emergency contact

Special equipment requirements

The declaration should explain that incomplete or false information may place the pet, sitter and other people at risk.

The customer should update the declaration when the pet’s health or behaviour changes.

Why it is required:

A sitter cannot safely care for a pet without accurate health and behaviour information.

The declaration helps PetSaathi match the pet with a suitable caregiver and service environment.

Simple explanation:

“The customer declaration confirms that the pet parent has shared correct information about the pet’s behaviour, vaccination and health.”

1.5 Photo and Video Consent

PetSaathi should use separate consent options for different purposes.

Consent for Service Updates

This allows the sitter to send photographs and videos to the pet parent during a booking.

Examples include:

Start-of-service photograph

Dog-walk photograph

Feeding update

Boarding update

Pet report-card photograph

This consent may be required when photos form part of the booked service.

Consent for Marketing and Testimonials

This allows PetSaathi to use selected photographs, videos or customer comments on:

The website

Social-media pages

Advertisements

Case studies

Promotional materials

Marketing consent should be optional and separate from service-update consent.

A customer should not be forced to allow promotional use simply to book pet care.

The customer should also be told how to withdraw future marketing consent.

Why it is required:

Photographs and videos may contain personal information, home interiors, society details, customer identity or location information.

Clear consent reduces privacy concerns and prevents unauthorised promotional use.

Simple explanation:

“Service photos are used to update the owner, while marketing photos require separate permission.”

1.6 Emergency Veterinary Consent

The emergency veterinary consent explains what PetSaathi and the sitter may do if the pet becomes injured or ill.

It should include:

The pet’s preferred veterinarian

Alternative emergency clinic

Owner’s primary contact number

Secondary emergency contact

Permission to transport the pet

Approved transport method

Maximum emergency spending limit

Person responsible for veterinary expenses

Procedure when the owner cannot be contacted

Permission to share necessary pet information with the veterinarian

Requirement to follow professional veterinary instructions

A possible process is:

Protect the pet from immediate danger.

Contact PetSaathi emergency support.

Contact the pet parent.

Contact the emergency contact if the owner does not answer.

Contact the preferred veterinarian or nearest emergency clinic.

Follow professional instructions.

Arrange safe transport when necessary.

Record the complete incident.

The consent should not allow an unqualified sitter to diagnose a condition or perform medical treatment beyond their training.

Why it is required:

During an emergency, delays can occur when nobody knows who has authority to transport the pet or approve veterinary treatment.

The consent provides a clear decision process.

Simple explanation:

“Emergency vet consent allows quick and organised action when the owner cannot immediately respond.”

1.7 Liability Limitation

A liability-limitation clause explains the reasonable limits of the platform’s financial and operational responsibility.

It may address:

Events outside reasonable control

Incorrect information provided by a customer

Pre-existing pet conditions

Behaviour not disclosed by the owner

Actions taken against written safety instructions

Third-party veterinary services

Third-party payment services

Maximum contractual compensation where legally valid

However, the clause should not attempt to remove responsibility for:

Fraud

Deliberate misconduct

Animal cruelty

Gross negligence

Misuse of customer data

Obligations that cannot legally be excluded

Unfair or unreasonable consumer conditions

The clause should be reviewed by a lawyer before publication.

Why it is required:

A reasonable liability clause explains how risk is divided between the platform, sitter and customer.

It should reduce uncertainty without unfairly taking away customer rights.

Simple explanation:

“The liability clause sets reasonable risk limits, but it cannot legally excuse every mistake or serious violation.”

1.8 Privacy Policy

The privacy policy explains how PetSaathi collects, uses, shares, stores and deletes personal information.

PetSaathi may collect:

Customer name

Telephone number

Email address

Home or service address

Society name

Pet information

Pet medical information

Emergency contacts

Sitter identity documents

Sitter address documents

Bank or payout information

GPS walk information

Photos and videos

Messages

Payment references

Support and complaint records

The policy should clearly explain:

What data is collected

Why each category is required

Which data is compulsory

Which data is optional

Who can access the data

Which service providers receive data

How long the data is retained

How the data is protected

How users can correct their information

How consent may be withdrawn

How deletion requests are handled

How privacy complaints can be submitted

How security incidents are managed

Sensitive sitter documents should not appear on public profiles.

Complete payment credentials should not be stored directly by PetSaathi.

GPS tracking should begin only when required for the active service and should stop when the service ends.

Why it is required:

PetSaathi will handle private customer, sitter, location, identity and pet information.

A privacy policy creates transparency and defines the platform’s data-protection responsibilities.

Simple explanation:

“The privacy policy explains what information PetSaathi collects and how that information is protected and used.”

2. Special Boarding Compliance

Pet boarding requires more careful legal and operational planning than normal dog walking.

A boarding provider may be caring for an animal for several hours, days or nights inside a commercial facility or private property.

Before activating boarding in any city, PetSaathi should check:

State animal-welfare policy

Municipal rules

Trade-licence requirements

Shops and Establishments registration

Commercial-use restrictions

Society or apartment permission

Landlord permission

Fire and building-safety requirements

Animal capacity limits

Vaccination requirements

Record-keeping requirements

CCTV requirements

Veterinary-support requirements

Hygiene and waste-management standards

Insurance availability

Tamil Nadu Example

Tamil Nadu’s 2026 pet-boarding policy demonstrates why PetSaathi must research each state separately.

The Tamil Nadu framework reportedly requires:

Registration with the Tamil Nadu Animal Welfare Board

Renewal every two years

Records of pets and pet owners

Vaccination information

CCTV surveillance

Retention of CCTV footage

Veterinary support

Facility and kennel standards

Hygiene and disinfection procedures

Commercial operating requirements

Prohibition on home boarding

This policy should not be copied as an all-India rule.

Instead, PetSaathi should prepare a separate compliance sheet for every launch city and state.

Simple explanation:

“Boarding rules can change from one state or city to another, so PetSaathi must verify local requirements before accepting boarding bookings.”

3. Deep 10-Day Phase 0 Plan

A seven-day Phase 0 is suitable for basic planning.

A ten-day Phase 0 is more suitable when the team wants deeper research and stronger documentation.

Day 1 — Vision, Business Model and Service Scope

On Day 1, I will define what PetSaathi is, which customer problem it solves and how the business may earn revenue.

I will decide whether the first version includes:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Pet boarding

I will also define what will not be included in the first pilot.

Output:

A clear business definition, service scope, customer value and revenue model.

Day 2 — City Research

On Day 2, I will compare possible launch cities using the weighted scorecard.

I will study:

Pet-parent density

Premium apartment density

Disposable income

Competitor pressure

Sitter availability

Veterinary ecosystem

Operational difficulty

Founder network

Output:

One primary city, one backup city and a city-ranking sheet.

Day 3 — Competitor Research

On Day 3, I will study platforms such as PetBacker, Snouters, PawSpace and local providers.

I will compare:

Services

Cities

Prices

Verification

Tracking

Customer updates

Booking flow

Reviews

Complaints

Refund conditions

Output:

A competitor matrix and a PetSaathi differentiation plan.

Day 4 — Customer Personas and Interview Scripts

On Day 4, I will define the main customer groups and create detailed pet-parent personas.

I will identify customer pains such as:

Difficulty finding trusted caregivers

Lack of live updates

Last-minute cancellation

Unclear pricing

Weak emergency support

I will then prepare interview questions and a customer survey.

Output:

Customer personas, a pain map, interview script and survey form.

Day 5 — Sitter Supply and Verification

On Day 5, I will define who can become a sitter and which services each person may provide.

I will prepare:

Sitter personas

Application form

Identity-verification process

Interview questions

Training requirements

Trust badges

Approval workflow

Red-flag rules

Output:

A complete sitter recruitment, verification and approval system.

Day 6 — Vet, Groomer and Society Partner Research

On Day 6, I will identify possible business and safety partners.

The research will include:

Veterinary clinics

Emergency animal hospitals

Groomers

Dog trainers

Pet stores

Pet taxis

Apartment managers

Society committees

Animal-welfare organisations

For each partner, I will record the contact person, location, service area and possible partnership model.

Output:

A partner database and initial outreach list.

Day 7 — Pricing and Unit Economics

On Day 7, I will estimate how much the customer pays, how much the sitter receives and how much remains for PetSaathi.

I will analyse:

Customer price

Sitter payout

Platform commission

Payment-gateway cost

Customer-support cost

Refund cost

Marketing cost

Safety and insurance cost

Net contribution

Output:

A pricing hypothesis and unit-economics sheet.

Day 8 — Technical Architecture and Data Model

On Day 8, I will define the technical structure of the platform.

I will prepare:

User roles

Database entities

Form-to-database mapping

Booking statuses

Admin workflow

Analytics events

Security controls

No-code or low-code stack

Output:

A technical architecture document and basic data model.

Day 9 — Legal, Safety, Terms and Operations SOP

On Day 9, I will prepare the important operating and legal drafts.

These will include:

Terms and Conditions

Privacy Policy

Refund Policy

Sitter Agreement

Customer Declaration

Photo and Video Consent

Emergency Veterinary Consent

Service Rules

Emergency SOP

Incident-reporting SOP

Boarding compliance checklist

Output:

A complete legal, safety and operations draft package for professional review.

Day 10 — Final Phase 1 Assets and Go/No-Go Decision

On Day 10, I will review every Phase 0 output.

I will finalise:

Landing-page content

Customer forms

Sitter forms

CRM sheet

Outreach templates

Pricing

KPI dashboard

Pilot area

Pilot services

Initial sitter target

Customer target

I will then decide whether the project should:

Proceed

Proceed with restrictions

Be delayed

Output:

A final Phase 1 launch package and documented go/no-go decision.

4. Phase 0 Unit-Economics Planning

Unit economics explains how much money PetSaathi earns or loses from one booking.

The calculation should be completed separately for dog walking, pet sitting and pet boarding.

4.1 Dog-Walking Example

### Table 24

| Item | Amount |
| --- | --- |
| Customer pays | ₹199 |
| Sitter payout | ₹140 |
| Platform gross margin | ₹59 |
| Payment and operational cost | ₹10–₹20 |
| Estimated net contribution | ₹39–₹49 |

Explanation

The customer pays ₹199 for the service.

PetSaathi pays ₹140 to the sitter.

The remaining ₹59 is the platform’s gross margin.

This is calculated as:

₹199 − ₹140 = ₹59

PetSaathi may then spend approximately ₹10–₹20 on payment processing, customer support, communication and basic operations.

The estimated net contribution is therefore:

₹59 − ₹10 = ₹49

or

₹59 − ₹20 = ₹39

This means PetSaathi may retain approximately ₹39–₹49 before considering larger costs such as marketing, salaries, insurance, software and taxes.

These amounts are planning assumptions, not confirmed market prices.

Simple explanation:

“One ₹199 dog-walking booking may leave approximately ₹39–₹49 after the sitter payout and basic booking costs.”

4.2 Boarding Example

### Table 25

| Item | Amount |
| --- | --- |
| Customer pays | ₹999 per night |
| Sitter payout | ₹750 |
| Platform gross margin | ₹249 |
| Support and risk cost | ₹50–₹100 |
| Estimated net contribution | ₹149–₹199 |

Explanation

The customer pays ₹999 for one boarding night.

PetSaathi pays ₹750 to the boarding provider.

The platform gross margin is:

₹999 − ₹750 = ₹249

Boarding may require higher customer-support, inspection, emergency and risk-management costs.

If these costs are approximately ₹50–₹100, the remaining contribution becomes:

₹249 − ₹50 = ₹199

or

₹249 − ₹100 = ₹149

This means one boarding night may produce approximately ₹149–₹199 before larger business expenses.

These values must be tested using actual city-level prices and operating costs.

Simple explanation:

“Boarding provides a higher booking value, but it also creates higher safety, support and compliance costs.”

5. Important Business Insight

Dog Walking Creates Repeat Frequency

Dog walking may be needed every day or several times per week.

This can create:

Regular customer habits

Monthly packages

Predictable sitter schedules

Repeat revenue

Strong customer retention

Boarding Creates Higher Ticket Value

Boarding is normally booked for full days or multiple nights.

A single boarding booking may generate more revenue than one dog walk.

However, boarding also creates more responsibility, risk and compliance work.

Pet Sitting Builds Trust

Pet sitting allows the sitter to care for the pet in its familiar home environment.

It may be easier for anxious pets and can help PetSaathi build a strong relationship with the pet parent.

Recommended Phase 0 Decision

PetSaathi should use:

Dog walking for regular habit and repeat bookings

Pet boarding for higher booking value

Pet sitting for customer trust and convenience

The three services support different business goals.

6. Phase 0 KPI Dashboard

The KPI dashboard helps PetSaathi measure real demand instead of depending only on opinions.

6.1 Lead Source

This field records where the customer discovered PetSaathi.

Possible sources include:

Instagram

WhatsApp

Apartment society

Veterinarian

Groomer

Friend referral

Google search

Event

College or community group

Why it matters:

It shows which marketing channel produces the best leads and bookings.

6.2 City

This field records the customer’s city.

Why it matters:

It shows where PetSaathi is receiving the strongest demand.

It also helps compare potential launch cities.

6.3 Area

This field records the customer’s neighbourhood or apartment society.

Why it matters:

PetSaathi needs customers to be located close together.

A high number of leads in one small area may create a strong micro-market.

6.4 Service Requested

This field records whether the customer needs:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Pet boarding

Why it matters:

It shows which service should receive the highest launch priority.

6.5 Budget

This field records how much the customer is willing to pay.

Why it matters:

It helps PetSaathi compare the proposed price with real customer expectations.

A customer saying that a service is useful is less valuable than knowing the amount they are prepared to pay.

6.6 Trust Concern

This field records the main reason stopping the customer from booking.

Possible concerns include:

Unknown sitter

Pet safety

No verification

No GPS tracking

No emergency support

Unclear price

Boarding environment

Cancellation risk

Why it matters:

It tells PetSaathi which trust feature should be built or explained first.

6.7 Booking Readiness

Each lead should be classified as:

Hot

Warm

Cold

Hot Lead

The customer currently needs a service, lives in the pilot area and is willing to take the next step.

Warm Lead

The customer may need the service later but is not ready to book immediately.

Cold Lead

The customer has no current need, does not match the service area or is not interested.

Why it matters:

It helps the team prioritise follow-up work.

6.8 Payment Willingness

This field records whether the customer is willing to:

Pay a refundable deposit

Book a discounted trial

Pay the regular price

Request details only

Decline payment

Why it matters:

Payment willingness provides stronger validation than positive comments alone.

6.9 Sitter Availability

This field records how many potential caregivers are available in each service area.

It should also record:

Service type

Available days

Available time

Travel radius

Verification status

Approval status

Why it matters:

Customer demand cannot be served without enough suitable caregivers.

6.10 Society Interest

This field records whether an apartment society is interested in:

Customer survey distribution

Pet-care awareness session

Society dog-walking plan

Sitter recruitment

Pilot partnership

Emergency vet partnership

Resident discount

Why it matters:

One society partnership may provide several customers within a small service area.

This creates business-to-business and community-level opportunities.

7. Phase 0 Final Readiness Checklist

PetSaathi should move into Phase 1 only after completing the following work.

Selected the First City

The team should choose one primary city based on evidence and scoring.

The city should have enough customer demand, sitter supply and supporting partners.

Selected the First Two or Three Areas

The team should identify concentrated micro-markets rather than trying to serve the complete city.

The selected areas should contain premium apartments, pet owners and nearby veterinary support.

Selected the First Three Services

The first service scope should be limited to:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Pet boarding

Any service that is not operationally or legally ready should be delayed.

Created the Competitor Matrix

The team should compare competitor services, prices, trust features, booking flow and weaknesses.

This prevents PetSaathi from copying competitors without creating a clear advantage.

Created the Pricing Hypothesis

The proposed prices should show:

Customer price

Sitter payout

Platform margin

Additional fees

Cancellation effect

Estimated net contribution

Created the Customer Interview Script

The script should collect information about real customer experiences, problems, current solutions and willingness to pay.

Created the Sitter Interview Script

The script should evaluate sitter experience, safety knowledge, reliability and communication.

Created the Booking Form Structure

The form should collect:

Customer information

Pet information

Required service

Date and time

Service address

Behaviour and health information

Emergency contact

Consent

Created the Sitter Application Form Structure

The form should collect:

Identity details

Address

Experience

Services

Availability

References

Verification documents

Boarding-home details

Declarations

Created the CRM Sheet

The CRM should track every lead, conversation, follow-up and booking result.

It should include lead status, service interest, budget and next action.

Created the Landing-Page Copy

The landing page should clearly explain:

What PetSaathi offers

Who it serves

Why it is different

How the pilot works

How customers and sitters can register

Created the Trust and Safety Policy Draft

The draft should explain:

Sitter verification

Service rules

GPS tracking

Report cards

Emergency support

Complaint handling

Sitter suspension

Customer responsibilities

Created the WhatsApp Templates

Templates should be ready for:

Customer survey

Customer interview

Pilot invitation

Follow-up

Sitter recruitment

Society outreach

Thank-you message

Respectful closure

Created the Basic Legal Checklist

The team should identify the documents and professional reviews required before paid services begin.

This includes consumer, privacy, tax, animal-welfare and local operating requirements.

Defined Success Metrics

The team should set measurable Phase 1 targets.

Possible targets include:

Number of customer interviews

Number of survey responses

Number of qualified leads

Number of sitter applications

Number of approved sitters

Number of paid trials

Number of completed bookings

Average rating

Cancellation rate

Serious safety incidents

Final Phase 0 Decision

PetSaathi is ready for Phase 1 only when demand, supply, safety, pricing and operations are sufficiently prepared.

Proceed

Proceed when the required documents, sitters, customer leads, emergency contacts and pilot workflow are ready.

Proceed with Restrictions

Begin with only one service or one small area when the complete model is not ready.

For example, PetSaathi may begin with manually managed dog walking while delaying boarding.

Delay the Pilot

Delay paid bookings when:

Legal or local requirements are unclear

No qualified sitter is available

Emergency support is unavailable

Customer information cannot be protected

Refund and cancellation rules are incomplete

Boarding facilities have not been checked

Serious safety risks remain

Simple Explanation for Professor

“During Phase 0, I will prepare PetSaathi before accepting real paid bookings. I will create the Terms and Conditions, refund policy, sitter agreement, customer declaration, privacy policy and emergency consent. I will also check local boarding rules because these rules may differ between states and cities. The ten-day plan covers the business model, launch city, competitors, customers, sitters, partners, prices, technology, safety and legal requirements. I will calculate the earnings from every booking and track demand through a KPI dashboard. PetSaathi will move to Phase 1 only after the city, areas, services, prices, forms, safety policies, outreach material and success measurements are ready.”

PetSaathi — Phase 1 Validation Sprint

1. Main Purpose of Phase 1

Phase 1 is called the Validation Sprint.

The main purpose of this phase is to confirm whether Indian pet parents are genuinely willing to use and pay for verified dog walking, pet sitting and controlled pet boarding services.

The focus is not on developing a complete mobile application.

The focus is on proving that the business problem, customer demand, caregiver supply, pricing and operational process are strong enough to justify further investment.

Simple Explanation

“Phase 1 helps us test whether PetSaathi can become a real business before we spend heavily on full application development.”

2. Confirmed Plan and Proposed Improvements

Confirmed Phase 1 Plan

The following parts are already included in the approved Phase 1 structure:

Landing page

WhatsApp booking button

Customer booking form

Sitter application form

Society partnership form

Instagram business page

Basic brochure

CRM tracking sheet

Trial-payment method

Manual booking process

Service report

Customer review and testimonial collection

These assets are sufficient for beginning a manually operated validation pilot.

Proposed Improvements

The following improvements make the validation process more structured:

Use three separate forms instead of one large form.

Record customers, sitters and bookings in separate CRM tables.

Use unique IDs for every lead, sitter and booking.

Collect a small real payment instead of measuring interest only.

Track the source of every lead.

Define hot, warm and cold lead rules.

Record every booking status.

Collect structured service reports after trials.

Separate service-update consent from marketing consent.

Track conversion metrics from landing-page visit to paid booking.

Simple Explanation

“The confirmed plan contains the basic tools, while the proposed improvements make the validation data more organised and reliable.”

3. Core Goal of the Validation Sprint

The core goal is to confirm that customers will take real action.

Phase 1 should answer the following six questions.

3.1 Does Real Customer Demand Exist?

PetSaathi must confirm that enough pet parents actually need help with walking, sitting or boarding.

It is not enough for people to say that the idea sounds useful.

Customers should describe a recent situation in which they struggled to arrange pet care.

Strong demand indicators include:

A customer currently needs a service.

A customer has previously paid for pet care.

A customer has experienced a problem with an existing provider.

A customer asks about availability.

A customer agrees to a trial.

A customer pays a deposit or service fee.

Simple Explanation

“This test shows whether the problem is real and important enough for customers to take action.”

3.2 Do Users Trust the Service Promise?

Pet care is a high-trust service because a customer is allowing another person to handle a family pet or enter a private home.

PetSaathi must test whether customers value:

Identity-verified sitters

Background or police verification

Live GPS tracking

Photographs and videos

Pet report cards

Emergency-veterinary support

Replacement caregivers

Verified customer reviews

Customers should also be asked which trust feature is most important and which concern prevents them from booking.

Simple Explanation

“This test identifies the safety features customers require before trusting a PetSaathi caregiver.”

3.3 Are Enough Sitters Available?

The platform requires enough suitable caregivers inside the selected pilot area.

PetSaathi should test:

How many people apply

How many applicants complete the form

How many attend the interview

How many can provide verification

How many pass the safety assessment

Which services they can provide

Which times they are available

What payout they expect

How far they are willing to travel

A large number of applications is not sufficient if very few applicants meet the safety and reliability standards.

Simple Explanation

“This test checks whether PetSaathi can create a dependable caregiver network.”

3.4 Are Customers Willing to Pay?

Customer interest should be tested through a real financial action.

A person who says, “This is a good idea,” may still refuse to pay for the service.

Stronger validation includes:

Paying a refundable reservation amount

Booking a discounted trial

Paying the complete trial price

Selecting a future service date

Accepting the normal expected price after the trial

Simple Explanation

“Real payment is stronger evidence than likes, survey responses or positive comments.”

3.5 Does One City or Area Have Enough Density?

PetSaathi should not attempt to serve an entire city during the first validation sprint.

The pilot should focus on two or three nearby areas containing:

Premium apartment societies

Visible pet-owning households

Nearby sitters

Veterinary clinics

Grooming partners

Manageable travel distances

Customer and sitter density reduces travel time and makes manual operations easier.

Simple Explanation

“This test helps us find one small area where customers, sitters and partners are located close together.”

3.6 Can the Business Work Operationally?

The team must prove that it can manage a booking from beginning to end.

The operational test includes:

Receiving a customer request

Checking pet and service information

Matching a suitable sitter

Confirming the sitter’s availability

Collecting payment

Sharing service instructions

Monitoring the service

Receiving photographs or tracking updates

Collecting the service report

Paying the sitter

Collecting the customer review

Resolving any complaint

Simple Explanation

“This test checks whether PetSaathi can safely complete real bookings without depending on a fully automated application.”

4. Recommended Phase 1 Duration

Three duration options are available.

### Table 26

| Version | Duration | Best Use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Fast sprint | 7 days | Basic interest and problem testing |
| Standard sprint | 15 days | Serious customer, sitter and payment validation |
| Deep sprint | 30 days | Strong operational testing before MVP development |

4.1 Seven-Day Fast Sprint

The fast sprint is suitable when the team wants to test basic market interest quickly.

During seven days, the team can:

Publish the landing page

Share the survey

Contact pet parents

Collect sitter applications

Test a small number of payment offers

The limitation is that seven days may not provide enough time for several completed bookings, repeat usage or reliable society partnerships.

4.2 Fifteen-Day Standard Sprint

The standard sprint is the recommended starting option.

Fifteen days provide enough time to:

Conduct customer interviews

Collect survey responses

Recruit sitters

Verify shortlisted caregivers

Contact societies and veterinary partners

Run paid trial services

Collect reviews

Analyse conversion data

Recommendation

Begin with a 15-day validation sprint.

Extend the phase to 30 days only when the early results show meaningful customer and sitter interest.

4.3 Thirty-Day Deep Sprint

The deep sprint is suitable when the team wants stronger evidence before starting MVP development.

A thirty-day test may include:

More customer interviews

Multiple completed services

Repeat bookings

More society outreach

Stronger sitter assessment

Better pricing evidence

Detailed operational problem analysis

Simple Explanation

“The 15-day sprint provides a balance between speed and reliable validation.”

5. Phase 1 Success Targets

The following targets should be treated as minimum internal goals.

### Table 27

| Metric | Minimum Target |
| --- | --- |
| Pet-parent conversations | 100 |
| Interested pet parents | 50 or more |
| Booking-ready leads | 20 or more |
| Paid trial bookings | 5–10 |
| Sitter applications | 25 or more |
| Shortlisted sitters | 10 or more |
| Vet or groomer contacts | 10 |
| Society-manager contacts | 10 |
| Interested societies | 3–5 |
| Testimonials | 3–5 |

5.1 Pet-Parent Conversations — Target: 100

The team should speak with or collect detailed responses from at least 100 pet parents.

A conversation may include:

A telephone interview

A video call

An in-person discussion

A detailed survey response

A structured WhatsApp conversation

The objective is to understand real customer behaviour rather than receiving only yes-or-no opinions.

5.2 Interested Pet Parents — Target: 50 or More

An interested pet parent should demonstrate at least one meaningful action.

Examples include:

Requesting service details

Asking about the launch date

Selecting a service

Sharing a preferred date

Joining the pilot list

Giving permission for follow-up

A person who only likes an Instagram post should not automatically be counted as an interested lead.

5.3 Booking-Ready Leads — Target: 20 or More

A booking-ready customer should:

Live inside the selected pilot area

Own a suitable pet

Need one of the pilot services

Accept the expected price range

Be willing to select a booking date

Agree to receive follow-up communication

These customers should be marked as hot leads in the CRM.

5.4 Paid Trial Bookings — Target: 5–10

This is the most important early metric.

A paid trial proves that a customer is willing to exchange money for the proposed service.

The booking should be counted only when:

The customer selects a service.

The date and time are confirmed.

The sitter is assigned.

Payment or the agreed deposit is received.

Completed paid trials provide stronger evidence than unpaid reservations.

5.5 Sitter Applications — Target: 25 or More

At least 25 applicants should complete the sitter-interest or application form.

This target helps measure whether the platform can attract potential caregivers.

The team should record:

Applicant location

Experience

Services

Availability

Verification readiness

Expected earnings

5.6 Shortlisted Sitters — Target: 10 or More

A shortlisted sitter should meet the basic service requirements.

The person should:

Complete the application

Provide valid contact information

Attend an interview

Demonstrate suitable experience or attitude

Agree to verification

Accept safety and reporting requirements

Match the pilot service area

Shortlisting does not automatically mean final approval.

5.7 Vet and Groomer Contacts — Target: 10

The team should contact at least ten veterinary clinics, groomers or related pet-care providers.

The purpose is to test:

Emergency-support availability

Referral interest

Customer-introduction opportunities

Service-area coverage

Partnership willingness

5.8 Society-Manager Contacts — Target: 10

The team should contact apartment managers, resident-welfare associations or society committee members.

The discussion should cover:

Approximate number of pet families

Common pet-related problems

Interest in dog-walking plans

Space for a pet-care camp

Willingness to share a survey

Partnership conditions

5.9 Interested Societies — Target: 3–5

A society should be considered interested only when the manager or committee agrees to a real next step.

Examples include:

Sharing the survey with residents

Scheduling a meeting

Allowing a pet-care awareness session

Collecting resident interest

Discussing a pilot

5.10 Testimonials — Target: 3–5

Testimonials should come from customers who have completed a real service or meaningful pilot interaction.

The testimonial may cover:

Ease of booking

Sitter behaviour

Punctuality

Communication

Tracking or updates

Pet report quality

Overall trust

Marketing use should require appropriate customer permission.

6. Most Important Validation Metric

The most important metric is not:

Instagram followers

Likes

Website visitors

Impressions

Survey views

General positive comments

The most important question is:

“How many suitable customers are ready to pay for a trial booking?”

A strong validation signal follows this sequence:

Interested → Qualified → Date Selected → Payment Received → Service Completed → Review Submitted

Simple Explanation

“A startup is validated when customers take meaningful action, not when they only say that the idea is good.”

7. Confirmed Phase 1 Assets

7.1 Landing Page

The landing page should explain:

What PetSaathi is

Who it serves

Which services are available

Why customers should trust it

Which areas are included

How customers can join the pilot

How sitters can apply

How societies can become partners

The landing page should have one clear purpose: converting visitors into measurable leads.

7.2 WhatsApp Booking Button

The WhatsApp button should allow a customer to start a direct conversation.

The pre-filled message may say:

“Hello, I am interested in the PetSaathi trial. I need [dog walking/pet sitting/pet boarding] in [area].”

The customer should then be directed to the correct booking form or qualification questions.

7.3 Booking Form

The customer booking form should collect structured information.

It should not replace a safety review.

Before confirming a service, the operations team should check:

Location

Pet details

Service requirement

Behaviour information

Preferred date

Sitter availability

Price acceptance

Emergency contact

7.4 Instagram Business Page

The Instagram page should be used to:

Explain the customer problem

Introduce the service

Show safety processes

Recruit sitters

Share educational pet-care content

Invite survey responses

Publish approved testimonials

Direct people to the landing page

The page should not make unsupported claims such as “completely safe” or “all sitters police verified” unless the statement is accurate.

7.5 Basic Brochure PDF

The brochure should contain:

PetSaathi introduction

Services

Trust features

Pilot areas

Trial prices

Contact information

Sitter application information

Society partnership information

A digital brochure is useful for WhatsApp, veterinary clinics, groomers and apartment managers.

7.6 Sitter Application Form

The sitter form should collect enough information for initial screening.

The form should not publicly expose identity documents or private applicant details.

8. Improved Validation System Architecture

The recommended Phase 1 flow is:

Landing Page↓Call-to-Action Button↓WhatsApp or Structured Form↓CRM Sheet↓Manual Qualification↓Trial Payment↓Manual Booking↓Service Updates and Report↓Review or Testimonial

Step 1: Landing Page

The customer discovers PetSaathi through Instagram, WhatsApp, a society, veterinarian, groomer or referral.

The landing page explains the service and directs the visitor toward the correct action.

Step 2: Call-to-Action

The visitor selects one of three actions:

Book a Trial

Become a Sitter

Partner Your Society

This prevents different user types from entering the same confusing process.

Step 3: WhatsApp or Form

The visitor provides the information required for their selected journey.

Pet parents complete the customer form.

Caregivers complete the sitter application.

Society managers complete the partnership form.

Step 4: CRM Entry

Each submission is stored in the relevant CRM table.

The team assigns a unique ID and records:

Source

Location

Service interest

Current status

Next action

Step 5: Manual Qualification

The operations team checks whether the lead matches the pilot.

A pet parent is checked for location, service need, budget and booking readiness.

A sitter is checked for area, availability, experience and verification readiness.

A society is checked for density, contact authority and genuine interest.

Step 6: Trial Payment

A qualified customer receives the complete offer.

The offer should include:

Service

Duration

Date

Price

Sitter status

Cancellation conditions

Refund conditions

Included updates

Payment should be linked to a specific customer or booking ID.

Step 7: Manual Booking

The team manually confirms the customer, sitter, service, price, location and instructions.

The booking should be entered in the booking CRM.

Step 8: Service Report

After the service, the sitter submits a structured report.

The report may include:

Arrival time

Departure time

Walk duration

Food and water

Toilet update

Pet mood

Behaviour

Photographs

Any concern

Step 9: Review and Testimonial

The customer receives a review form.

A separate question should ask whether the customer allows their comments or media to be used for marketing.

Simple Explanation

“This architecture creates a complete path from customer discovery to payment, service completion and review.”

9. Recommended Phase 1 Technology Stack

### Table 28

| Requirement | Recommended Tool |
| --- | --- |
| Landing page | Next.js and Tailwind CSS |
| UI design | Figma |
| Forms | Tally or Google Forms |
| CRM | Airtable or Google Sheets |
| Payment | Razorpay Payment Link or approved UPI flow |
| Customer communication | WhatsApp Business |
| Website analytics | Google Analytics 4 |
| Behaviour analysis | Microsoft Clarity |
| Brochure design | Canva or Figma |
| Social presence | Instagram Business profile |
| Live-location test | Manual WhatsApp live location |
| Customer reviews | Structured online form |

9.1 Next.js and Tailwind CSS

Next.js should be used for the premium landing page.

Tailwind CSS can be used to build the visual design efficiently.

The landing page can later become part of the main MVP instead of being discarded after validation.

9.2 Figma

Figma should be used to prepare:

Landing-page wireframes

Responsive page designs

Form layouts

Mobile screens

User-flow diagrams

Brochure layouts

The development should begin only after the basic page structure and conversion flow are clear.

9.3 Tally or Google Forms

Forms should be used for:

Customer bookings

Sitter applications

Society enquiries

Reviews

Service reports

Each form should connect to the correct CRM table.

9.4 Airtable or Google Sheets

The CRM should record:

Leads

Sitters

Bookings

Payments

Partnerships

Reviews

Google Sheets is suitable for a simple, low-cost pilot.

Airtable may be selected when the team requires more structured linked records and filtered operational views.

9.5 Razorpay Payment Link or UPI

The payment method should allow the customer to pay for a specific trial without requiring a complete checkout system.

Each transaction should contain:

Booking ID

Customer name or reference

Service description

Amount

Payment status

Payment date

Personal UPI transfers should not become the permanent operational system because reconciliation and booking matching may become difficult.

9.6 WhatsApp Business

WhatsApp Business should be used for:

Initial enquiries

Qualification

Booking confirmation

Service reminders

Updates

Follow-ups

Messages should remain structured and professional.

Customers who decline further communication should not receive repeated outreach.

9.7 Google Analytics 4

Analytics should measure:

Landing-page visits

CTA clicks

Form starts

Form submissions

WhatsApp clicks

Payment-link clicks

Successful trial registrations

9.8 Microsoft Clarity

Clarity should be used to understand:

Where visitors click

How far visitors scroll

Which section causes confusion

Whether mobile users face layout problems

Whether visitors reach the CTA

Sensitive form fields should not be unnecessarily exposed to behavioural analytics tools.

9.9 Manual WhatsApp Live Location

Manual live location may be used only as a temporary validation method for approved dog-walking trials.

The customer and sitter should understand:

When location sharing starts

Who can view it

When it ends

That it is a temporary pilot process

The future MVP should use a controlled in-application tracking system.

10. Professional Technical Recommendation

Build the Phase 1 landing page using Next.js and Tailwind CSS, but do not build the complete backend yet.

Reasons

Fast Launch

A focused landing page can be developed and deployed faster than the full marketplace.

Premium Brand Presentation

A custom frontend can communicate stronger trust and professionalism than an unstructured collection of forms.

Reusability

The components can later be reused in the full PetSaathi product.

Analytics Readiness

The page can support structured event tracking.

Form Integration

The CTA buttons can connect to external forms without requiring a complete database.

Portfolio and Business Credibility

A professional responsive website improves the presentation of the project to customers, societies, partners and academic evaluators.

Important Limitation

Do not spend several weeks developing complex animations or backend features before payment demand is validated.

Simple Explanation

“Build only the customer-facing layer required to test the business, and delay expensive marketplace development until the evidence is strong.”

11. Landing-Page Main Message

Recommended Headline

Trusted pet care for busy Indian pet parents

Supporting Sentence

“Book verified dog walkers, pet sitters and controlled boarding services with clear updates and safety-focused support.”

The supporting sentence should describe the current pilot accurately.

For example, the page should not promise live in-application tracking when the pilot only provides manual location sharing.

12. Landing-Page Sections

12.1 Hero Section

The hero section should immediately explain:

What PetSaathi offers

Who the service is for

Where the pilot is available

What action the visitor should take

It should include:

Main headline

Supporting sentence

Book a Trial button

Become a Sitter button

Trust indicators

12.2 Problem Section

This section should explain the customer’s main difficulties.

Examples include:

Difficulty finding a trusted caregiver

No clear service updates

Last-minute cancellation

Unclear prices

No emergency plan

Limited local availability

The wording should reflect actual interview findings.

12.3 Services Section

The services section should explain the first three offerings.

Dog Walking

Scheduled 30-minute or 60-minute walks with agreed service updates.

Pet Sitting

Home visits or extended care for feeding, companionship and supervision.

Pet Boarding

Controlled overnight care with approved providers, activated only after operational and local compliance checks.

12.4 Trust-System Section

This section should explain the checks and service proof actually available during the pilot.

Possible items include:

Identity-checked caregiver

Interviewed applicant

Service instructions

Location update

Photographs

Pet report card

Emergency contact process

Customer support

Every badge and claim must match the checks genuinely completed.

12.5 How-It-Works Section

Step 1: Select a Service

The customer chooses walking, sitting or boarding.

Step 2: Submit Pet Details

The customer provides the pet’s location, behaviour and care requirements.

Step 3: Receive a Match

The operations team identifies a suitable available caregiver.

Step 4: Confirm and Pay

The customer reviews the service conditions and makes the trial payment.

Step 5: Receive Updates

The sitter completes the service and submits the agreed updates and report.

12.6 Pricing Section

The pricing section should show:

Trial price

Service duration

Number of pets included

Additional charges

Cancellation conditions

Pilot limitations

Customers should not need to contact the team merely to discover the basic starting price.

12.7 City and Area Availability

This section should clearly state the current pilot locations.

Example:

“Phase 1 is available only in selected societies within [Area 1], [Area 2] and [Area 3].”

The page should not create the impression that the service is available across all of India.

12.8 Testimonials

This section should initially remain hidden or contain only genuine approved testimonials.

After completed trials, it can include:

Customer name or permitted identifier

Service used

Area

Review

Rating

Approved pet photograph

12.9 Become a Sitter

This section should explain:

Who can apply

Which services are available

Verification requirements

Expected professionalism

Application process

Earnings structure

12.10 Society Partnership

This section should target:

Apartment managers

Resident-welfare associations

Community managers

Premium residential societies

The section should explain possible benefits such as organised walking plans, verified local caregivers and pet-care camps.

12.11 Frequently Asked Questions

The FAQ should answer:

How are sitters checked?

Which areas are available?

What happens if a sitter cancels?

How are payments handled?

What updates will I receive?

What happens during an emergency?

Can I meet the sitter first?

What is the refund policy?

Are boarding facilities inspected?

Can I request the same sitter again?

12.12 Contact Section

The contact section should include:

WhatsApp

Telephone number

Email address

Business hours

Service areas

13. Landing-Page CTA Structure

PetSaathi should use three primary calls to action.

### Table 29

| CTA | Target User | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Book a Trial | Pet parent | Validate demand and payment |
| Become a Sitter | Caregiver | Validate service supply |
| Partner Your Society | Apartment manager | Validate local B2B demand |

Book a Trial

This button should open the pet-parent booking flow.

Become a Sitter

This button should open the sitter application.

Partner Your Society

This button should open the society partnership form.

Simple Explanation

“The three CTA buttons allow PetSaathi to test customer demand, caregiver supply and society partnerships at the same time.”

14. Pet-Parent Booking Form

The customer form should collect the following information.

Full Name

This field identifies the customer.

Phone Number

This field is used for calls and booking coordination.

WhatsApp Number

This field is used only when the customer agrees to receive WhatsApp communication.

When the same number is used for both phone and WhatsApp, the form can provide a checkbox.

City

This field confirms whether the customer lives inside the selected launch city.

Area or Locality

This field helps measure micro-market demand.

Society or Apartment Name

This field helps identify customer density inside individual communities.

Pet Type

Options may include:

Dog

Cat

Other

The selected service should be compatible with the pet type.

Pet Breed

This field helps the team understand the pet’s general size and care requirements.

Breed should not replace individual behaviour assessment.

Pet Age

This field helps identify puppies, adults and senior pets.

Pet Weight

This field helps match large or strong pets with suitable sitters.

Service Needed

Options include:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Pet boarding

Preferred Date

This field records when the customer wants the service.

Preferred Time

This field helps check sitter availability.

Budget Range

The budget field helps measure price acceptance.

Suggested options should match the service selected.

Biggest Concern

Options may include:

Trust

Safety

Price

Availability

Emergency support

Communication

Pet anxiety

Need Live GPS?

This field measures demand for location tracking.

It should not be presented as available unless the pilot can genuinely provide it.

Need Emergency-Vet Support?

This field measures whether emergency support affects the purchase decision.

Ready for a Paid Trial?

Options include:

Yes

Maybe

No

“Yes” responses should be prioritised for qualification.

Additional Notes

The customer can provide behaviour, medical or service information not covered by other fields.

Output

A structured pet-parent record ready for qualification.

15. Sitter Application Form

Full Name

The applicant should enter their legal name.

Phone Number

This number will be used for application communication.

City

This field confirms whether the applicant lives in the pilot city.

Area

This field helps determine travel distance and local supply density.

Age

The applicant must meet the applicable working-age and platform requirements.

Gender

This field should be optional unless there is a clear operational reason for collecting it.

Occupation

This field helps the team understand the applicant’s routine and availability.

Pet-Care Experience

The applicant should explain:

Animals handled

Length of experience

Services previously provided

Difficult situations managed

Owns a Pet

This answer may provide useful context but should not determine approval automatically.

Services Offered

Options include:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Pet boarding

Applicants should receive approval only for suitable services.

Availability

Options may include:

Morning

Afternoon

Evening

Night

Weekend

Expected Earnings

This field helps PetSaathi compare sitter expectations with the proposed payout model.

Can Provide ID Proof?

A “No” answer may prevent approval.

Sensitive documents should be collected through a secure process rather than an open public form whenever possible.

Can Attend a Video Interview?

The interview helps assess communication, judgement and service suitability.

Can Share an Introduction Video?

The video may help the internal screening process.

Public use should require clear permission.

Can Provide Home Photos for Boarding?

This field applies only to boarding applicants.

Photos alone should not replace a home-safety assessment.

Emergency Contact

This field records a trusted person who can be contacted during a sitter-related emergency.

Motivation

The applicant explains why they want to become a PetSaathi sitter.

The answer helps evaluate professionalism, expectations and attitude.

Output

A structured sitter application ready for screening.

16. Society Partnership Form

Society Name

This identifies the residential community.

City

This confirms whether the society is inside the pilot city.

Area

This helps compare society location with sitter supply.

Manager or RWA Contact

This records the authorised contact person.

Approximate Number of Flats

This gives a basic indication of community size.

Approximate Pet Families

This helps estimate potential demand.

The number may be an estimate and should be labelled accordingly.

Current Pet Issues

Possible issues include:

Lack of walkers

Stray-animal conflict

Lift or common-area concerns

No trusted sitter

Festival-care problems

Emergency-vet access

Interested Services

Options may include:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Boarding

Veterinary camp

Grooming camp

Can PetSaathi Run a Pet-Care Camp?

This question tests whether the society is open to an on-site activity.

Output

A structured society lead ready for partnership follow-up.

Badges should reflect completed checks accurately.

Applied

19. Booking CRM Structure

### Table 32

| Column | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Booking ID | Unique service reference |
| Customer ID | Links customer record |
| Pet name | Identifies the pet |
| Sitter ID | Links caregiver record |
| Service | Booked service |
| Date | Scheduled date |
| Time | Scheduled time |
| Customer price | Revenue |
| Sitter payout | Caregiver cost |
| Platform margin | Gross booking margin |
| Payment status | Payment tracking |
| Service status | Operational tracking |
| Review | Quality feedback |

Booking ID

Every booking should receive a unique reference such as BK-001.

Customer ID and Sitter ID

These IDs connect the booking to the correct customer and caregiver records.

Customer Price

This is the complete amount charged before refunds.

Sitter Payout

This is the amount owed to the sitter according to the agreed rules.

Platform Margin

The basic gross margin is calculated as:

Customer price − Sitter payout

This is not the final profit because payment, support, marketing, refund and operational costs still need to be deducted.

Payment Status

Suggested values include:

Payment pending

Deposit received

Paid

Failed

Refund pending

Refunded

Service Status

Suggested values include:

Requested

Sitter assigned

Confirmed

In progress

Completed

Cancelled

Disputed

Review

The record may store the rating or link to the complete review response.

Simple Explanation

“These three CRM tables can later become the foundation of the real application database.”

20. Phase 1 Pricing Test

The following prices are experimental starting points.

### Table 33

| Service | Trial Price | Possible Future Price |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 30-minute dog walk | ₹99–₹149 | ₹149–₹299 |
| 60-minute dog walk | ₹199–₹299 | ₹249–₹499 |
| One-hour pet sitting | ₹199–₹299 | ₹299–₹599 |
| Half-day pet sitting | ₹499–₹799 | ₹799–₹1,499 |
| Boarding beta | ₹699–₹999 per night | ₹999–₹2,499 per night |

20.1 Why Use Trial Pricing?

Trial pricing reduces the customer’s risk during the first test.

It helps the team evaluate:

Customer willingness to pay

Price sensitivity

Service expectations

Sitter payout requirements

Operational costs

Conversion rate

20.2 Important Pricing Rule

The team should not use an unrealistically low price only to generate bookings.

A trial price should still cover a meaningful part of:

Sitter payout

Payment cost

Customer support

Travel or communication cost

Service-report process

20.3 Price-Test Questions

After presenting the offer, ask:

Does this price feel low, reasonable or high?

Would you book at this price today?

What price would make you hesitate?

Would you pay more for stronger verification?

Would you continue at the future normal price?

Would you prefer a monthly plan?

21. Payment Validation

Do not ask only:

“Are you interested?”

Ask a specific action-based question:

“Would you like to book a verified 30-minute trial walk for ₹99 this week?”

The question contains:

A defined service

A defined duration

A defined price

A defined time period

A real decision

Strong Validation Levels

Level 1: Positive Opinion

The customer says the idea is useful.

This is weak validation.

Level 2: Contact Permission

The customer agrees to receive pilot information.

This is an early lead.

Level 3: Date Selection

The customer selects a preferred service date.

This is stronger intent.

Level 4: Reservation Deposit

The customer pays a small amount to reserve the trial.

This is financial validation.

Level 5: Full Trial Payment

The customer pays the complete trial price.

This is strong validation.

Level 6: Completed Booking

The service is completed successfully.

This proves operational capability.

Level 7: Repeat Booking

The customer books another service at the normal or revised price.

This is the strongest early indication of repeat value.

22. Recommended 15-Day Sprint Structure

Days 1–3: Launch and Outreach

Publish landing page

Test all forms

Activate analytics

Start WhatsApp outreach

Publish Instagram content

Contact societies

Contact vets and groomers

Days 4–6: Customer Research

Conduct customer interviews

Analyse survey responses

Qualify leads

Identify common trust concerns

Update landing-page messaging

Days 7–9: Sitter Recruitment

Review applications

Conduct interviews

Check references

Shortlist caregivers

Match sitter availability with demand

Days 10–12: Payment and Booking Tests

Present trial offers

Collect payments

Confirm bookings

Conduct approved services

Record operational problems

Days 13–14: Reviews and Analysis

Collect reviews

Request permitted testimonials

Calculate conversion metrics

Review sitter performance

Review pricing and costs

Day 15: Go or No-Go Decision

Compare results with success targets

Identify the strongest service

Identify the strongest area

Decide whether to extend to 30 days

Decide whether to begin Phase 2

23. Phase 1 Decision Rules

Move to Phase 2

Proceed when:

Customers demonstrate real demand.

At least five paid trials are completed or firmly booked.

Suitable sitters are available.

One area shows customer and sitter density.

Operations can complete services safely.

Customers accept the service promise.

The expected price and payout model appear workable.

Extend Phase 1

Extend the sprint to 30 days when:

Interest is strong but paid-booking volume is still low.

Customers request future dates.

Sitter recruitment needs additional time.

Society discussions are promising but incomplete.

Pricing requires further testing.

Pause or Redesign

Do not proceed directly to full MVP development when:

Customers like the idea but refuse to pay.

The pilot area has insufficient demand.

Suitable sitters cannot be recruited.

Service delivery produces serious safety issues.

Pricing cannot cover sitter and operational costs.

Boarding requirements remain unclear.

Customers do not trust the proposed system.

24. Simple Explanation for Professor

“Phase 1 is the PetSaathi Validation Sprint. Its purpose is to confirm that real customers need the service, trust the proposed safety features and are willing to pay before a complete application is developed. I will use a professional landing page, separate customer, sitter and society forms, a CRM sheet, WhatsApp Business and trial-payment links. The most important result is not the number of followers or website visitors. The most important result is the number of suitable customers who pay for and complete a trial booking. I recommend a 15-day sprint first. If the early response is promising but incomplete, the validation period can be extended to 30 days. PetSaathi should move to Phase 2 only after demand, sitter supply, pricing and operations have been validated.”

PetSaathi Phase 1 — Day 1: Build Foundation Assets

Main Goal of Day 1

The main goal of Day 1 is to prepare all the basic assets required to begin the PetSaathi validation sprint.

By the end of the day, I should have:

One confirmed launch city

Two or three selected launch areas

A final brand name and tagline

Complete landing-page content

Three structured forms

A landing-page wireframe

A customer, sitter and booking CRM structure

Ready-to-use WhatsApp messages

A final checklist showing that the project is ready for development

09:00–09:30 — Finalise the City and Two or Three Launch Areas

During this session, I will make the final decision about where the first PetSaathi validation sprint will be conducted.

I will not attempt to launch across the entire city.

Instead, I will select two or three nearby areas where customers, sitters, apartment societies and veterinary services are located close together.

The city decision should be based on the previously prepared scorecard.

The city should be evaluated according to:

Pet-parent density

Premium apartment density

Customer spending capacity

Competitor pressure

Sitter availability

Veterinary and grooming support

Operational difficulty

Founder network advantage

After selecting the city, I will compare different areas inside it.

The selected launch areas should contain:

Premium or high-density apartment societies

Visible pet-owning households

Working professionals and travelling pet parents

Nearby veterinary clinics

Available walkers or sitters

Manageable travel distances

Potential society partnerships

Example Decision Format

Primary launch city: Surat

Selected launch areas:

Vesu

Adajan

Pal

Reason for selection:

These areas contain organised residential societies, suitable target customers, veterinary services and possible founder-network advantages.

The actual city and areas should be selected only after comparing the available evidence.

Questions to Answer

Which city received the strongest weighted score?

Which areas contain the highest concentration of potential customers?

Can sitters travel between these areas easily?

Are veterinary clinics available nearby?

Does the founder have useful contacts in these locations?

Can the team manually support bookings in these areas?

Are there any major legal or operational restrictions?

Output

One confirmed launch city and a shortlist of two or three launch areas.

Simple Explanation for Professor

“I selected one city and two or three concentrated areas so that PetSaathi can test customer demand without trying to serve the entire city.”

09:30–10:30 — Finalise the Brand Name and Tagline

Easy to pronounce

Relevant to pets and care

Suitable for Indian customers

Different from existing competitors

PetSaathi

The word “Pet” clearly identifies the industry.

The word “Saathi” means companion or trusted partner.

Together, the name communicates that the platform supports both pets and pet parents.

This tagline highlights verification and convenience.

Option 3

Your pet’s trusted companion.

Option 4

Safe walks, trusted stays and caring hands.

This tagline explains the main services more clearly.

Template 1: Customer Survey Message

Hello [Name],

I am Prince, and I am working on PetSaathi, a project focused on trusted dog walking, pet sitting and pet boarding.

I am conducting a short survey to understand the problems pet parents face while arranging care.

The survey takes approximately three minutes.

Survey link: [Insert link]

Thank you for your support.

Hello [Name],

Thank you for showing interest in PetSaathi.

I would like to understand your experience with dog walking, pet sitting or boarding through a short 10–15 minute conversation.

This is a research discussion and not a sales call.

Please share a suitable time if you are interested.

Hello [Name],

Based on your pet-care requirement, we are offering a limited PetSaathi trial in [Area].

Service: [Service]Duration: [Duration]Trial price: ₹[Amount]Preferred date: [Date]

The trial includes [service updates/report details].

Would you like to continue with the booking process?

Applicants will complete:

An application form

Identity verification

A video interview

Basic safety training

Application link: [Insert link]

Please apply only if you can provide reliable and professional pet care.

Template 5: Society Partnership Message

Hello [Name],

I am working on PetSaathi, a local pet-care project for verified dog walking, pet sitting and controlled boarding.

We are studying the needs of pet-owning families in selected residential societies.

Could we discuss:

A resident pet-care survey

A small awareness session

A limited society pilot

Please let me know a suitable time for a short conversation.

Template 6: Booking Confirmation

Hello [Name],

Your PetSaathi trial booking has been confirmed.

Booking ID: [Booking ID]Service: [Service]Pet: [Pet name]Date: [Date]Time: [Time]Sitter: [Sitter name]Price: ₹[Amount]

Please review the service instructions and contact us if any pet information has changed.

Template 7: Follow-Up Message

Hello [Name],

I am following up regarding your interest in the PetSaathi [Service] trial.

Please reply with one option:

Ready to book

Interested later

Not interested

I will update your preference accordingly.

Template 8: Review Request

Hello [Name],

Thank you for completing your PetSaathi trial.

Please share your experience through this short review form:

[Review link]

Your feedback will help us improve the service.

Template 9: Respectful Closure

Hello [Name],

Thank you for your response.

I have updated your preference and will not send further trial messages.

Your time and feedback are appreciated.

2. Create the Folder Structure

A simple project structure may contain:

src/

├── app/

│ ├── layout.tsx

│ ├── page.tsx

│ └── globals.css

├── components/

│ ├── layout/

│ ├── sections/

│ ├── ui/

├── data/

├── lib/

├── types/

└── assets/

Folder Explanation

app

This folder contains the main page, shared layout and global styling.

components/layout

This folder contains elements used across the complete page, such as:

Header

Navigation

Footer

Mobile menu

components/sections

This folder contains the major landing-page sections, such as:

Services

Trust

Pricing

How It Works

components/ui

This folder contains reusable design elements, such as:

Buttons

Cards

Section headings

Input fields

components/forms

This folder contains booking, sitter and society form components when forms are placed directly on the website.

data

This folder stores temporary static information, including:

Service details

Pricing plans

Trust features

Frequently asked questions

Launch areas

lib

This folder contains helper functions, constants and external-link configuration.

types

This folder contains TypeScript types for services, prices, form links and other structured information.

Clear supporting text

Highly visible buttons

Sufficient spacing

High-quality imagery

A clear pilot-location label

Mobile-friendly button sizes

No unsupported safety claims

Output

A complete first section containing the headline, supporting copy, pilot location, visuals and three calls to action.

Simple Explanation for Professor

“I created the hero section so visitors can immediately understand what PetSaathi offers and select the correct action.”

11:00–12:00 — Create the Services Section

During this session, I will create cards for the first three PetSaathi services.

The purpose of this section is to help customers understand exactly what can be booked.

Section Heading

Pet care designed around your schedule

Choose a service based on your pet’s routine, your working hours or your travel requirements.

Dog Walking

Short walk report

Service-Card Design Requirements

Each card should contain:

Service icon

Service name

Short description

Main inclusions

Starting trial price

CTA button

The cards should use the same size, spacing and visual hierarchy.

On desktop screens, the cards may appear in three columns.

On mobile screens, the cards should appear one below another.

Important Pricing Note

The section should include a note such as:

Final prices may depend on the service area, duration, number of pets, pet-care requirements and sitter availability.

This avoids presenting experimental pilot prices as permanently fixed prices.

Output

Three reusable service cards for dog walking, pet sitting and controlled boarding.

Simple Explanation for Professor

“I created clear service cards showing what each service includes, its starting price and the next booking action.”

12:00–13:00 — Create the Trust Section

During this session, I will build the section explaining how PetSaathi creates customer confidence.

Trust is important because pet parents are allowing another person to handle their pet or enter their home.

Section Heading

Built around trust, transparency and responsible care

Supporting Text

PetSaathi uses structured caregiver screening and clear service updates to make every pilot booking easier to understand and monitor.

Trust Feature 1: Identity Check

Heading

Identity-Checked Caregivers

Description

The caregiver’s submitted identity information is reviewed before service approval.

The website should not use the phrase “police verified” unless official police verification has actually been completed.

Trust Feature 2: Sitter Interview

Heading

Structured Sitter Interview

Description

Applicants complete an interview covering experience, availability, communication and basic safety judgement.

Trust Feature 3: Service-Specific Approval

Heading

Approved for Specific Services

Description

A person approved for dog walking is not automatically approved for pet boarding.

Each service requires the relevant experience, assessment and safety checks.

Trust Feature 4: Service Updates

Heading

Clear Service Updates

Description

Pet parents receive the agreed photographs, messages or location updates during the service.

Trust Feature 5: Pet Report Card

Heading

Pet Report Card

Description

After the service, the caregiver records important information such as:

Food and water

Walk duration

Toilet activity

Pet mood

Behaviour

Health concerns

Trust Feature 6: Emergency Process

Heading

Emergency-Support Process

Description

The sitter follows a defined process for contacting the pet parent, emergency contact, PetSaathi support and the relevant veterinarian.

Trust Promise

A highlighted statement may say:

Every sitter profile will show only the checks and approvals that have genuinely been completed.

This is more accurate than using general claims such as:

Completely safe

Risk-free

Fully verified

Guaranteed protection

Trust Section Design

The trust section may use:

Six feature cards

Verification icons

Small process illustrations

A highlighted trust statement

A caregiver profile preview

A sample report-card preview

Output

A professional trust section explaining PetSaathi’s verification, updates, reports and emergency process.

Simple Explanation for Professor

“I created the trust section to explain how sitters are screened and how customers receive proof and updates during a service.”

13:00–14:00 — Lunch Break

The project runs without errors.

The services appear correctly.
