Simple explanation for professor

“On Day 5, I conducted the first PetSaathi sitter-recruitment batch. I first published the sitter opportunity through Instagram, WhatsApp and approved local communities. I then contacted students, pet lovers, animal-care groups, societies and referral partners. Interested people received a structured application form covering experience, services, availability and verification readiness. In the afternoon, I called applicants to assess their motivation, safety judgement, reliability and earning expectations. I scored and shortlisted suitable profiles using consistent criteria. Finally, I scheduled video interviews with the strongest candidates so that the detailed screening and approval process could continue.”

PetSaathi Phase 1 — Day 6: Vet, Groomer and Society Outreach

Main goal of Day 6

The main goal of Day 6 is to build relationships with local pet-care professionals and residential societies.

The daily target is to contact:

Five veterinary clinics or groomers

Five society managers or RWA representatives

These contacts can help PetSaathi with customer referrals, emergency support, local credibility, sitter recommendations and concentrated demand inside apartment communities.

PetSaathi should verify veterinary partners carefully. The Veterinary Council of India maintains the Indian Veterinary Practitioners Register and publishes the professional-conduct regulations applicable to registered veterinary practitioners. A groomer should never be presented as a veterinarian or be asked to provide veterinary diagnosis or treatment.

WhatsApp outreach should be permission-based. Meta’s official guidance states that businesses should obtain opt-in before initiating business messages. Therefore, PetSaathi should contact publicly listed business numbers, existing contacts, referred businesses or recipients who have agreed to receive information.

Day 6 targets

By the end of the day, PetSaathi should have:

A final partner-pitch message

Five veterinary or grooming businesses contacted

Five society managers contacted

A completed partner CRM

At least a few scheduled follow-up meetings

A clear pet-care camp proposal

A list of objections raised by partners

A classification of interested, follow-up and uninterested partners

Specific next actions for every contact

09:00–10:30 — Create the Partner Pitch Message

During this session, I will prepare separate outreach messages for veterinary clinics, groomers and apartment societies.

One general message should not be used for every partner because each partner has a different interest.

A veterinarian may be interested in emergency referrals and preventive-care awareness.

A groomer may be interested in customer referrals, service packages and local promotions.

A society manager may be interested in resident convenience, organised service delivery and reducing pet-related operational complaints.

1. Define the partnership purpose

Before writing the message, I will clearly decide what I am requesting from each partner.

Possible veterinary partnership requests

PetSaathi may request:

An emergency-clinic contact

Availability information

Customer referrals

Sitter-safety guidance

Participation in a pet-care awareness session

Vaccination or preventive-care education

Support for an appointment-based society camp

PetSaathi should not promise that a veterinary clinic will always be available unless a written arrangement has been confirmed.

Possible grooming partnership requests

PetSaathi may request:

Cross-referrals

Discounted introductory packages

Grooming-camp participation

Sitter or pet-parent referrals

Brochure placement

Social-media collaboration

Society event participation

A grooming partner should remain within the grooming services it is qualified to provide.

Medical concerns noticed during grooming should be referred to a registered veterinarian.

Possible society partnership requests

PetSaathi may request permission to:

Share a resident survey

Publish a voluntary pilot-registration message

Conduct a pet-care awareness session

Set up an information desk

Run an appointment-based pet-care camp

Recruit local sitters

Test a society-level walking plan

Meet interested pet-owning residents

The society should not be asked to share residents’ private phone numbers without their permission.

A better approach is for the society manager to circulate an opt-in form or approved message.

2. Prepare a short PetSaathi introduction

The introduction should explain PetSaathi in one or two sentences.

Recommended introduction

PetSaathi is a trust-focused pet-care pilot that connects pet parents with screened caregivers for dog walking, pet sitting and controlled boarding in selected areas of [City].

This sentence explains:

The business name

The current pilot stage

The services

The trust-based positioning

The limited launch area

3. Create the veterinarian outreach message

Suggested veterinarian message

Hello Dr. [Name],

I am Prince, and I am working on PetSaathi, a local pet-care pilot for dog walking, pet sitting and controlled boarding in selected areas of [City].

We are building a small network of credible veterinary and pet-care partners for emergency referrals, customer education and local pet-parent support.

I would like to understand:

Whether your clinic handles pet emergencies

Your operating hours and service area

Whether you are open to receiving appropriate PetSaathi referrals

Whether you would consider participating in a society pet-care awareness session

Could we schedule a short 10–15 minute conversation?

Thank you.

The message does not promise a formal partnership before the clinic has agreed.

4. Create the groomer outreach message

Suggested groomer message

Hello [Name],

I am working on PetSaathi, a local pilot for trusted dog walking, pet sitting and controlled boarding in [City].

We are speaking with selected groomers about local referrals, resident pet-care events and possible service collaborations.

I would like to understand your service areas, grooming packages and interest in participating in a small society pet-care camp.

Would you be available for a short discussion?

5. Create the society-manager outreach message

Suggested society-manager message

Hello [Name],

I am Prince, and I am working on PetSaathi, a local pet-care pilot focused on organised dog walking, pet sitting and controlled boarding.

We are studying the needs of pet-owning families in selected residential societies in [City].

We would like to discuss:

A voluntary resident pet-care survey

A small pet-care awareness session

A limited service pilot for interested residents

A possible appointment-based pet-care camp

Could we schedule a short conversation at a convenient time?

6. Add a clear call to action

Every pitch message should contain only one main next step.

Possible calls to action include:

Schedule a 15-minute call

Confirm the appropriate contact person

Complete a partner-interest form

Select a meeting time

Approve circulation of the resident survey

The first message should not contain several long requests.

7. Avoid unsupported partnership claims

PetSaathi should not say:

“We are partnered with the leading vets in the city.”

“Emergency veterinary care is guaranteed.”

“This society has officially approved PetSaathi.”

“All pets attending the camp will receive medical clearance.”

These statements should be used only when the arrangement genuinely exists and the wording is accurate.

Output

Separate professional pitch messages for veterinarians, groomers and society managers.

Simple explanation for professor

“I created different partnership messages because veterinary clinics, groomers and societies receive different benefits from working with PetSaathi.”

10:30–12:00 — Contact Five Veterinarians or Groomers

During this session, I will contact five suitable veterinary clinics or grooming businesses.

The target can contain:

Three veterinary clinics and two groomers

Four veterinary clinics and one groomer

Another suitable combination based on the local market

Emergency veterinary support should receive higher priority because it is important for the PetSaathi safety process.

Step 1: Select relevant businesses

I will prioritise businesses that:

Operate near the selected launch areas

Have clear business contact information

Have suitable operating hours

Provide relevant services

Appear professionally managed

Are willing to communicate with pet parents

Can realistically receive local referrals

For veterinarians, I should record the veterinarian’s name and registration details where available.

The VCI website provides access to registration resources, State Veterinary Council links and the Indian Veterinary Practitioners Register.

Step 2: Prepare a contact list

The initial list should include:

### Table 42

| Field | Information |
| --- | --- |
| Partner ID | Unique reference |
| Business name | Clinic or groomer name |
| Partner type | Veterinarian or groomer |
| Contact person | Owner, doctor or manager |
| City | Business location |
| Area | Locality |
| Phone number | Official business contact |
| Website or profile | Public business page |
| Services | Main services provided |
| Operating hours | Normal availability |
| Emergency availability | Yes, no or unknown |
| Contact source | Referral, search or personal contact |

Step 3: Make the first contact

I will use the most suitable communication channel:

Business phone call

Official WhatsApp number

Business email

In-person visit

Referral introduction

If I visit the clinic, I should avoid interrupting a consultation or emergency.

I can ask the receptionist or manager for a suitable time to speak.

Step 4: Explain the purpose clearly

During the call, I will explain:

PetSaathi is currently conducting a controlled validation pilot. We are not requesting an exclusive partnership. We are trying to understand local service availability and identify reliable contacts for referrals, emergency support and community awareness activities.

This wording reduces pressure and makes the request easier to evaluate.

Step 5: Ask the veterinarian appropriate questions

Suggested veterinary questions include:

What types of companion animals does the clinic treat?

What are the normal operating hours?

Does the clinic accept emergency or urgent cases?

Is emergency support available outside normal hours?

Which locations does the clinic normally serve?

Does the clinic provide home visits?

Can PetSaathi refer pet parents to the clinic?

What information should accompany an emergency referral?

Who should PetSaathi contact during an urgent incident?

Would the clinic participate in an educational society event?

Would the clinic consider providing basic pet first-aid guidance for sitter training?

Are there particular services that should not be represented as part of the partnership?

PetSaathi should record the answers but should not interpret them as a formal commitment until the partner confirms the arrangement.

Step 6: Ask the groomer appropriate questions

Suggested grooming questions include:

Which grooming services are available?

Which areas does the business serve?

Is grooming provided at the salon, at home or through a mobile service?

What are the usual price ranges?

Does the groomer handle cats as well as dogs?

Are difficult or anxious pets accepted?

What vaccination or health information is required?

Would the groomer accept customer referrals from PetSaathi?

Would the business share PetSaathi’s survey or brochure?

Would the groomer participate in a society camp?

Is the groomer interested in offering a pilot discount?

How should complaints or appointment problems be handled?

Step 7: Discuss possible value exchange

PetSaathi should explain how both sides may benefit.

Possible value for veterinarians

Relevant local referrals

Increased visibility among pet parents

Participation in educational events

Introduction to apartment societies

A structured emergency-referral channel

Possible value for groomers

New local customers

Cross-promotion

Society-event opportunities

Package testing

Referrals from PetSaathi users

Possible value for PetSaathi

Emergency contacts

Professional guidance

Local credibility

Referral leads

Partner participation in events

Better service coverage

Step 8: End with one next action

Possible next actions include:

Send the PetSaathi brochure

Schedule a detailed meeting

Share a draft referral process

Request the clinic’s emergency details

Discuss a society-camp date

Add the business to a provisional partner list

Output

Five veterinary or grooming contacts completed, with responses and next actions recorded.

Simple explanation for professor

“I contacted five local veterinarians or groomers to discuss referrals, emergency support, awareness activities and possible society events.”

12:00–13:00 — Update the Partner CRM

During this session, I will enter every morning contact into the partner CRM.

Information should be recorded immediately because verbal discussions can easily be forgotten or misunderstood.

Recommended partner CRM columns

### Table 43

| Column | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Partner ID | Unique partner reference |

Data-protection rule

The CRM should contain only information needed for partner communication and operations.

The DPDP Act provides a framework for lawful processing of digital personal data and recognises consent and withdrawal rights. Business-contact data should therefore be stored securely, used for the stated purpose and removed from future outreach when a person asks not to be contacted.

Output

A fully updated CRM containing all five veterinarian and groomer contacts.

Simple explanation for professor

“I recorded every partner conversation, objection and follow-up task in the CRM so no opportunity or commitment would be lost.”

13:00–14:00 — Lunch Break

I will take a lunch break after completing the first partner-outreach batch and updating the CRM.

Before the break, I will review:

How many businesses responded

Which pitch received the best response

Whether the clinic understood the partnership request

Whether any claim sounded too strong

Which questions caused confusion

Which partner requested more documentation

Which objections appeared repeatedly

Output

No new outreach activity is required during this period.

14:00–15:30 — Contact Five Society Managers

During this session, I will contact five apartment managers, facility managers, RWA representatives or society committee members.

The objective is to understand society-level pet-care demand and obtain permission for a voluntary next step.

The Animal Welfare Board of India publishes guidance concerning pet dogs, caregivers, RWAs and apartment-owner associations. Society discussions should therefore be respectful of residents, animal welfare and existing law rather than presenting PetSaathi as an authority that can create or enforce society rules.

Step 1: Identify the correct contact person

Possible contacts include:

Society manager

Facility manager

RWA president

RWA secretary

Committee member

Community manager

Resident pet committee representative

A security guard or reception employee may help identify the correct person but may not have authority to approve an event or resident communication.

Step 2: Introduce the pilot

A suitable opening statement is:

PetSaathi is conducting a limited pet-care validation pilot in selected areas. We would like to understand the needs of pet-owning residents and explore a voluntary survey, awareness session or small service pilot.

This makes it clear that:

The service is at the pilot stage

Participation is voluntary

No society-wide commitment has been assumed

The first step is research

Step 3: Ask about the society

Suggested questions include:

Approximately how many flats are in the society?

Approximately how many households own pets?

Does the society have a residents’ pet group?

What pet-care problems are commonly reported?

Do residents already use local walkers or sitters?

Is there demand for emergency pet-care contacts?

Do residents experience problems during festivals or travel periods?

Is there a suitable common area for an awareness event?

What permissions are required for an external service provider?

Can the society share a voluntary resident-interest form?

Would the committee consider a limited dog-walking or sitting pilot?

Who would approve a pet-care camp?

The estimate of pet-owning households should be marked as an estimate unless verified.

Step 4: Explain the society benefits

Possible benefits include:

Easier access to local caregivers

Better-organised resident enquiries

A common emergency-contact directory

Pet-care education

Concentrated sitter availability

A voluntary resident survey

A structured complaint and escalation contact

Reduced dependence on random unverified contacts

Access to veterinary or grooming partners

PetSaathi should not promise that the service will eliminate all pet-related conflicts.

Step 5: Request a small first commitment

The first request should be simple.

Possible first commitments include:

Allowing a survey link to be shared

Scheduling a committee presentation

Providing an event-permission process

Identifying a pet-parent representative

Allowing an information desk

Discussing a pilot with interested residents

A manager who only accepts the brochure should be classified differently from a manager who agrees to share the survey.

Step 6: Record the contact result

Each society contact should be marked as:

Meeting scheduled

Survey sharing approved

Camp discussion approved

Committee approval required

Brochure requested

Follow-up requested

Not currently interested

No response

Wrong contact person

Do not contact again

Output

Five society managers contacted, with interest and approval requirements recorded.

Simple explanation for professor

“I contacted five society representatives to understand resident pet-care needs and request permission for a survey, meeting or limited pilot.”

15:30–16:30 — Offer the Pet-Care Camp Idea

During this session, I will present a simple pet-care camp concept to interested veterinary, grooming and society partners.

The camp should initially be positioned as a small, appointment-based community event rather than a large public gathering.

Proposed camp name

PetSaathi Community Pet-Care Day

Main objectives of the camp

The event may be used to:

Meet local pet parents

Understand resident needs

Collect voluntary pilot registrations

Introduce PetSaathi’s services

Recruit local sitters

Provide preventive-care education

Introduce veterinary or grooming partners

Test society-level demand

Possible camp formats

Format 1: Pet-care information desk

This is the simplest format.

It may include:

PetSaathi service information

Customer survey

Sitter applications

Society pilot registration

Emergency-contact information

Pet-care educational material

This format does not require animals to attend.

Format 2: Veterinary awareness session

A registered veterinary practitioner may discuss general topics such as:

Vaccination awareness

Parasite prevention

Heat safety

Basic emergency warning signs

Responsible pet care

Individual diagnosis or treatment should be conducted only through an appropriate veterinary consultation process.

The professional’s registration and participation should be confirmed before advertising the event as a veterinary camp. The VCI maintains practitioner-registration resources and professional-conduct rules that can be used during partner verification.

Format 3: Grooming consultation camp

A grooming partner may provide:

Coat-care guidance

Nail-care awareness

Grooming demonstrations

Appointment booking

Introductory offers

The groomer should refer medical skin, ear or health concerns to a veterinarian rather than diagnosing them.

Format 4: Combined appointment-based event

The combined event may include:

PetSaathi information desk

Veterinary awareness

Grooming consultations

Sitter-interest registration

Resident survey

Trial-booking desk

Appointment slots can reduce crowding and prevent excessive interaction between unfamiliar animals.

Camp proposal details

The proposal should include:

Event name

Proposed date

Start and end time

Venue

Expected attendance

Partner names

Activity list

Required tables and electricity

Registration process

Pet attendance policy

Safety procedure

Cleaning process

Emergency contact

Marketing plan

Data-collection process

Costs and responsibilities

Permission requirements

Camp safety rules

The initial rules may include:

Pets must remain under the owner’s control.

Dogs should use a secure leash or carrier as appropriate.

Owners must disclose known aggression or health concerns.

Animals showing serious distress should not remain at the event.

Unfamiliar pets should not be forced to interact.

Water and shaded waiting space should be available when appropriate.

The event should have a clear incident contact.

Photography should require appropriate permission.

Medical services should be provided only by qualified professionals.

Waste should be removed promptly.

AWBI guidance addresses responsibilities involving pet dogs, caregivers and residential associations, including responsible handling in shared residential environments.

Camp lead form

The camp registration form may collect:

Name

Contact number

Society

Pet type

Service interest

Veterinary or grooming interest

Trial-booking interest

Permission for follow-up

Marketing consent should be separate from basic event registration.

Output

A clear PetSaathi camp proposal that can be shared with interested clinics, groomers and societies.

Simple explanation for professor

“I created a small pet-care camp idea that can bring residents, veterinarians, groomers and PetSaathi together in one organised event.”

16:30–18:00 — Record Objections and Interest

During the final session, I will review every partner conversation and identify the main objections, interest level and next action.

An objection does not always mean rejection.

It often shows what information, protection or commercial benefit the partner needs before agreeing.

Common veterinarian objections

Objection 1: “Who is responsible during an emergency?”

The clinic may want clear information about:

Who authorises treatment

Who pays the bill

How the pet parent is contacted

Who transports the pet

What information is shared

Whether PetSaathi guarantees payment

Recommended response

PetSaathi should provide a written emergency-referral process and avoid guaranteeing costs unless a formal arrangement supports that promise.

Objection 2: “We cannot guarantee 24-hour availability.”

Recommended response

The clinic should be listed only according to its confirmed operating hours.

PetSaathi may maintain more than one emergency contact instead of depending on one clinic.

Objection 3: “We do not want inappropriate or unqualified referrals.”

Recommended response

PetSaathi should explain what information will be collected before referral and when an issue should be directed to a veterinarian.

Objection 4: “We need a formal agreement.”

Recommended response

Record the requested agreement topics and prepare a simple referral or event memorandum for legal review.

Common groomer objections

Objection 1: “We need a minimum number of appointments.”

Recommended response

Do not guarantee volume before demand is known.

Offer advance registration and confirm the event only after reaching an agreed number.

Objection 2: “Who handles cancellations?”

Recommended response

Define who collects bookings, how reminders are sent and what happens after a customer cancellation.

Objection 3: “The camp may not be financially worthwhile.”

Recommended response

Discuss potential customer leads, marketing exposure, appointment deposits and the minimum viable event size.

Common society objections

Objection 1: “The committee must approve the event.”

Recommended response

Ask for the proposal format, committee meeting date and authorised decision-maker.

Classify this lead as pending approval rather than uninterested.

Objection 2: “We cannot share residents’ phone numbers.”

Recommended response

Agree with the concern.

Ask the society to circulate an opt-in survey or registration link instead.

Objection 3: “Residents may complain about pets in common areas.”

Recommended response

Offer a controlled, appointment-based format and discuss venue, timing, pet attendance and safety rules before approval.

Objection 4: “External commercial activities are restricted.”

Recommended response

Ask whether a non-selling awareness session or resident survey is permitted.

Do not attempt to bypass society rules.

Objection 5: “We already have local walkers or service providers.”

Recommended response

Ask whether residents still experience problems involving availability, verification, emergencies or backup care.

PetSaathi may position itself as an optional structured service rather than demanding exclusivity.

Interest classification

Hot partner

A partner should be classified as hot when they have agreed to a specific next step, such as:

Scheduling a meeting

Sharing the resident survey

Providing emergency details

Participating in the camp

Reviewing a written proposal

Offering a referral arrangement

Warm partner

A partner should be classified as warm when:

The idea is relevant

More information is required

Committee approval is pending

A later date is preferred

Commercial conditions need discussion

The partner requested the brochure or proposal

Cold partner

A partner should be classified as cold when:

The service is not relevant

The business operates outside the pilot area

The society does not allow the proposed activity

The partner declines further communication

No suitable partnership model exists

Suggested partner-interest score

### Table 46

| Condition | Points |
| --- | --- |
| Located in a target area | +15 |
| Serves the required pet-parent audience | +15 |
| Agrees to a follow-up meeting | +20 |
| Will share the survey or brochure | +15 |
| Interested in referrals | +10 |
| Interested in a camp | +15 |
| Provides an authorised decision-maker | +5 |
| Gives permission for future contact | +5 |

Classification

70–100 points: Hot partner

40–69 points: Warm partner

0–39 points: Cold partner

The score should support judgement rather than automatically deciding the partnership.

Objection-tracking table

### Table 47

| Field | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Objection ID | PO-001 |
| Partner type | Society |
| Objection | Committee approval required |
| Number of mentions | 3 |
| Current response | Send written proposal |
| Required action | Prepare one-page camp plan |
| Owner | Founder |
| Deadline | [Date] |
| Status | Open |

Output

A ranked list of partner objections, interest levels and follow-up actions.

Simple explanation for professor

“I reviewed every discussion and classified partners according to whether they were ready to act, needed more information or were not suitable.”

Day 6 success checklist

Day 6 is complete when:

Separate partner-pitch messages are ready.

Five veterinarians or groomers have been contacted.

Veterinary credentials are recorded where relevant.

Five society representatives have been contacted.

Every contact has a CRM record.

Contact permission and follow-up status are recorded.

No resident contact list has been collected without permission.

A pet-care camp proposal is prepared.

Partner objections have been ranked.

Hot, warm and cold partners are identified.

Each interested partner has a specific next action.

No unconfirmed partnership is advertised publicly.

Simple explanation for professor

“On Day 6, I conducted PetSaathi’s first partner-outreach batch. I first created separate pitch messages for veterinary clinics, groomers and residential societies. I then contacted five veterinary or grooming businesses to discuss emergency referrals, customer referrals and pet-care events. After recording these conversations in the partner CRM, I contacted five society managers to understand resident needs and request permission for a survey, meeting or limited pilot. I also prepared an appointment-based pet-care camp proposal. Finally, I recorded each partner’s objections, classified their level of interest and assigned the next follow-up action.”

PetSaathi Phase 1 — Day 7: First Review Checkpoint

Main goal of Day 7

The purpose of Day 7 is to review the first week of the PetSaathi validation sprint and determine whether the project is producing meaningful evidence.

This checkpoint should answer seven questions:

Are pet parents willing to discuss their problems?

How many customers show genuine interest?

How many customers are close to booking?

Is enough sitter supply available?

Are local pet-care partners responding?

Are residential societies open to collaboration?

Has anyone made a real payment?

The targets below are internal validation targets, not universal pet-care industry benchmarks. Missing one target does not automatically mean the idea has failed. The reason behind the result is more important than the number alone.

Day 7 target dashboard

### Table 49

| Metric | Target by Day 7 | What should be counted |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pet-parent conversations | 30–40 | Completed, meaningful research conversations |
| Interested leads | 15–20 | People who take a clear next step |
| Booking-ready leads | 5–8 | Qualified customers prepared to select a service, date and price |
| Sitter applicants | 10–15 | People who submit the complete application |
| Vet or groomer contacts | 5 | Relevant businesses that were actually contacted |
| Society contacts | 5 | Managers or authorised representatives contacted |
| Paid trials | 1–3 | Real payments received for specific trial bookings |

1. Pet-Parent Conversations — Target: 30–40

By Day 7, PetSaathi should have completed approximately 30 to 40 meaningful conversations with pet parents.

A conversation should count only when the respondent provides useful information about their pet-care experience.

The discussion should cover subjects such as:

The type of pet they own

Their current pet-care arrangement

The last time they needed outside help

The service they require

Their main problem

Their trust concerns

Their expected budget

Their willingness to try PetSaathi

An unanswered message should not be counted as a conversation.

A person who replies only with “not interested” may be recorded as an outreach result, but this should not be treated as a completed customer interview.

Why this metric matters

A sufficient number of conversations helps PetSaathi identify patterns instead of relying on the opinion of only a few friends or relatives.

For example, repeated comments may show that customers mainly need:

Morning dog walks

Boarding during travel

A regular backup caregiver

A sitter meet-and-greet

Live service updates

Emergency support

Information to record

For every completed conversation, the CRM should include:

Lead ID

Customer area

Pet type

Service required

Current solution

Main problem

Trust concern

Acceptable price

Trial response

Follow-up permission

Checkpoint interpretation

30–40 conversations: The research volume is on target.

20–29 conversations: The project needs additional outreach during Week 2.

Below 20 conversations: The outreach channels, message or target audience may need to be changed.

Simple explanation for professor

“By Day 7, I should have spoken with 30 to 40 pet parents and collected structured information about their actual needs, concerns and budgets.”

2. Interested Leads — Target: 15–20

By Day 7, approximately 15 to 20 pet parents should demonstrate meaningful interest in PetSaathi.

Interest should be based on customer behaviour, not on polite comments.

A person may be counted as an interested lead when they take at least one concrete action, such as:

Asking for service details

Requesting the trial price

Completing the booking-interest form

Sharing a preferred service

Asking when the pilot will begin

Requesting sitter information

Giving permission for follow-up

Asking to join the pilot list

A person should not be counted merely because they:

Liked an Instagram post

Viewed the website

Said that the idea sounded good

Forwarded the message

Followed the social-media account

Why this metric matters

This metric shows whether the customer problem is important enough to generate an active response.

It also measures whether PetSaathi’s message, services and trust promise are understandable.

Questions to review

At the checkpoint, I will ask:

Which service generated the most interest?

Which area generated the most leads?

Which source generated the best leads?

Which trust feature attracted the most attention?

Why did interested customers stop before becoming booking-ready?

Checkpoint interpretation

15–20 interested leads: The service is generating promising initial demand.

8–14 interested leads: The value proposition or targeting may need improvement.

Below 8 interested leads: The customer problem, outreach message, area or offer should be investigated carefully.

Simple explanation for professor

“An interested lead is a pet parent who does more than give a positive opinion and takes a real next step toward using the service.”

3. Booking-Ready Leads — Target: 5–8

By Day 7, PetSaathi should identify approximately five to eight booking-ready customers.

A booking-ready lead is more valuable than a generally interested lead.

The customer should meet most of the following conditions:

Lives in a selected pilot area

Owns a pet suitable for the available service

Needs the service within the next 30 days

Has selected walking, sitting or boarding

Accepts the approximate trial price

Is willing to provide the required pet information

Can suggest a preferred date and time

Is willing to review a sitter match

Has given permission for follow-up

Is willing to make a payment or deposit

A lead-scoring model can combine customer characteristics, such as location and service requirement, with customer actions, such as requesting a date or agreeing to pay. HubSpot similarly describes lead scoring as assigning values based on record properties or actions to estimate which contacts are more likely to become customers.

Suggested booking-readiness score

### Table 50

| Condition | Points |
| --- | --- |
| Lives in the selected area | 15 |
| Needs service within 30 days | 20 |
| Has previously paid for pet care | 10 |
| Accepts the proposed price | 15 |
| Selects a service | 10 |
| Provides a possible date | 10 |
| Wants to meet or review a sitter | 5 |
| Agrees to a paid trial | 10 |
| Allows follow-up | 5 |
| Total | 100 |

Suggested classification

70–100 points: Booking-ready

40–69 points: Interested but not ready

Below 40 points: Early or unsuitable lead

The score should support human judgement rather than making the final decision automatically.

Checkpoint interpretation

5–8 booking-ready leads: The project has a usable early sales pipeline.

2–4 booking-ready leads: Interest exists, but trust, timing or pricing may be blocking action.

Zero or one booking-ready lead: The offer requires significant investigation before further development.

Simple explanation for professor

“A booking-ready lead is a suitable customer who has a real service requirement, accepts the approximate price and is prepared to choose a booking date.”

4. Sitter Applicants — Target: 10–15

By Day 7, PetSaathi should receive approximately 10 to 15 completed sitter applications.

Only completed forms should be counted as applications.

The following should be recorded separately as sitter enquiries:

Social-media likes

Recruitment-post views

People who ask one question

People who receive the form but do not submit it

People who say they may apply later

A valid application should include

Applicant name

City and area

Age and occupation

Pet-care experience

Requested services

Availability

Travel radius

Expected earnings

Verification readiness

Interview availability

Emergency contact

Motivation

Why this metric matters

A marketplace cannot complete bookings without enough suitable caregivers.

However, application volume is not sufficient by itself.

PetSaathi must also measure:

How many applicants live near customer demand

How many are available at required times

How many can provide verification

How many demonstrate good safety judgement

How many accept the proposed payout

How many should be shortlisted

Additional sitter metrics

The checkpoint should show:

### Table 51

| Sitter metric | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Applications received | Total completed forms |
| Screening calls completed | Applicants contacted |
| Strong shortlist | Suitable for video interview |
| Conditional shortlist | Requires more information |
| Rejected or held | Does not currently meet requirements |
| Service distribution | Walking, sitting or boarding supply |
| Area distribution | Where sitter availability exists |

Checkpoint interpretation

10–15 applications: Recruitment is producing an initial supply pool.

5–9 applications: More targeted sourcing is required.

Below 5 applications: The recruitment message, earning offer or sourcing channels may need to change.

Simple explanation for professor

“By Day 7, I should have 10 to 15 completed sitter applications, but only suitable and screened applicants should move to the interview stage.”

5. Veterinary or Groomer Contacts — Target: 5

By Day 7, PetSaathi should have contacted at least five veterinary clinics or grooming businesses.

A contact should be counted when PetSaathi attempts communication through an appropriate channel, such as:

A completed telephone call

A business WhatsApp conversation

An email sent to the correct contact

An in-person discussion

A referral introduction

Simply saving the business telephone number should not be counted as a completed contact.

Information to record

For every partner, the CRM should include:

Partner name

Partner type

Area

Contact person

Contact method

Services offered

Emergency availability

Referral interest

Camp interest

Main objection

Follow-up status

Expected Day 7 outcomes

The five contacts may produce different results:

Interested in referrals

Interested in an awareness event

Interested in a pet-care camp

Requests a written proposal

Requires a formal agreement

Not available for emergency support

Not currently interested

No response

A partner should be treated as interested only when they agree to a specific next step.

Checkpoint interpretation

Five contacts completed: Outreach activity is on schedule.

Two or more positive follow-ups: The partner value proposition is showing promise.

Five contacts but no meaningful response: The pitch, partner selection or proposed benefit should be revised.

Simple explanation for professor

“I contacted five veterinary or grooming businesses to test interest in referrals, emergency support and community pet-care activities.”

6. Society Contacts — Target: 5

By Day 7, PetSaathi should have contacted at least five society managers, RWA representatives or authorised committee members.

The objective is to find societies with concentrated pet-parent demand.

The first conversation should explore:

Approximate number of flats

Estimated number of pet families

Existing walkers or sitters

Common pet-care problems

Permission to share a resident survey

Interest in a pet-care camp

Interest in a limited service pilot

Society approval requirements

What counts as a society contact

A society contact may be counted when:

The correct manager or committee member receives the pitch

A meaningful telephone or in-person conversation occurs

A proposal is formally submitted

The society asks for a follow-up

The manager explains the approval process

Speaking only with a security guard or collecting the society’s general telephone number should not be counted as a completed manager contact.

Levels of society interest

Strong interest

The society agrees to:

Share the resident survey

Schedule a committee meeting

Discuss a camp date

Introduce PetSaathi to pet-owning residents

Review a pilot proposal

Moderate interest

The society:

Requests the brochure

Requires committee approval

Asks for more information

Wants to reconsider later

No current interest

The society:

Rejects outside services

Does not permit the proposed activity

Has no relevant demand

Requests no further contact

Checkpoint interpretation

Five contacts completed: Society outreach is on target.

One or more specific next steps: The society channel is worth continuing.

No follow-up interest: The pitch, selected societies or partnership model may need revision.

Simple explanation for professor

“I contacted five authorised society representatives to understand local pet-care demand and test whether they would support a survey, camp or pilot.”

7. Paid Trials — Target: 1–3

By Day 7, PetSaathi should aim to receive one to three real paid trial bookings.

This is the most important metric in the checkpoint.

A paid trial should count only when:

The customer has selected a service.

The service price has been confirmed.

A preferred or confirmed date is recorded.

A suitable sitter can be assigned.

The customer has paid the required amount or approved deposit.

The payment is connected to a unique booking record.

Razorpay Payment Links can contain details such as the amount, description, expiry and reference ID. These fields can help PetSaathi connect a payment to the correct customer and booking during a manually managed pilot.

What should not be counted as a paid trial

The following are not completed payment validation:

The customer says they may pay later.

The customer asks for the payment link but does not pay.

The customer agrees verbally.

The customer submits the interest form only.

A friend makes a test payment that is immediately returned without a genuine service intention.

The customer chooses a date but does not complete payment.

Information to record

Each paid trial should have:

Booking ID

Customer ID

Service

Pet name

Date and time

Customer price

Amount received

Payment reference

Sitter assignment status

Cancellation conditions

Service status

Why this metric matters

A paid trial demonstrates that the customer sees enough value to make a financial commitment.

It is stronger evidence than:

Website visits

Social-media engagement

Survey responses

Verbal interest

Positive feedback

Checkpoint interpretation

Three paid trials: Strong first-week validation for a small pilot.

One or two paid trials: Early payment evidence exists and should be examined carefully.

Zero paid trials with several booking-ready leads: The team should investigate payment friction, trust, timing and the offer.

Zero paid trials and very few booking-ready leads: The value proposition or target market may require substantial revision.

Simple explanation for professor

“A paid trial is the strongest Day 7 signal because it proves that a customer is willing to exchange real money for the PetSaathi service.”

Day 7 Conversion Review

The checkpoint should not review only total numbers. It should also calculate how customers move through the validation funnel.

1. Interest Rate

Formula:

Interested leads ÷ Pet-parent conversations × 100

Example

If PetSaathi completes 35 conversations and identifies 18 interested leads:

18 ÷ 35 × 100 = 51.4%

This means approximately 51% of interviewed pet parents showed meaningful interest.

2. Booking-Readiness Rate

Formula:

Booking-ready leads ÷ Interested leads × 100

Example

If 18 people are interested and six are booking-ready:

6 ÷ 18 × 100 = 33.3%

This means one-third of interested customers are close to booking.

3. Paid-Trial Conversion Rate

Formula:

Paid trials ÷ Booking-ready leads × 100

Example

If six customers are booking-ready and two pay:

2 ÷ 6 × 100 = 33.3%

This means one-third of booking-ready leads completed the payment step.

4. Sitter Application Completion Rate

Formula:

Completed sitter applications ÷ Application links sent × 100

This shows whether the application form or recruitment offer is creating friction.

5. Partner Follow-Up Rate

Formula:

Partners agreeing to a next step ÷ Partners contacted × 100

This should be calculated separately for veterinary or grooming businesses and societies.

Analytics connection

Important website actions such as form submissions, contact clicks and confirmed payments can be recorded as analytics events. GA4 allows particularly important actions to be marked as key events so the team can measure them separately from ordinary page activity.

Day 7 Traffic-Light Decision

Green Status — Continue the Sprint

The checkpoint may be marked green when:

Customer conversations are near or above target.

At least 15 customers show interest.

At least five leads are booking-ready.

Sitter applications are sufficient for screening.

Partner contacts are complete.

At least one genuine paid trial exists.

No major safety issue has appeared.

Decision

Continue to Week 2 and focus on converting leads into completed bookings.

Amber Status — Continue with Corrections

The checkpoint may be marked amber when:

Customer interest exists but payment is still zero.

Customers are ready, but suitable sitters are unavailable.

Sitters are available, but customer demand is weak.

One launch area performs well while the others perform poorly.

Partners show interest but require additional documentation.

Trust or pricing objections repeatedly block bookings.

Decision

Continue the sprint, but correct the specific weak stage before increasing outreach.

Red Status — Pause and Rework

The checkpoint may be marked red when:

Customer-conversation volume is very low.

Few customers recognise the problem.

No one becomes booking-ready.

Customers reject the offer even after clarification.

Suitable sitters cannot be found.

Safety requirements cannot be met.

Paid bookings would require promises PetSaathi cannot deliver.

Decision

Pause payment promotion and revise the customer segment, location, service, pricing or operating model.

Day 7 Review Questions

At the end of the checkpoint, I should answer:

Which service received the most demand?

Which area produced the most interested customers?

Which outreach source produced the best leads?

What was the most common trust objection?

What was the most common pricing objection?

Why did interested leads fail to become booking-ready?

Why did booking-ready leads fail to pay?

Are sitters available where customer demand exists?

Which sitter service has insufficient supply?

Which partner type showed the strongest interest?

Should any launch area be removed?

What must be changed before Day 8?

Recommended Day 7 Report

### Table 52

| Metric | Target | Actual | Status | Main reason | Next action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Pet-parent conversations | 30–40 | — | Green/Amber/Red | — | — |
| Interested leads | 15–20 | — | Green/Amber/Red | — | — |
| Booking-ready leads | 5–8 | — | Green/Amber/Red | — | — |
| Sitter applicants | 10–15 | — | Green/Amber/Red | — | — |
| Vet/groomer contacts | 5 | — | Green/Amber/Red | — | — |
| Society contacts | 5 | — | Green/Amber/Red | — | — |
| Paid trials | 1–3 | — | Green/Amber/Red | — | — |

Simple Explanation for Professor

“Day 7 is the first formal review checkpoint of the PetSaathi validation sprint. I will compare the actual results with the planned targets. I should have completed 30 to 40 pet-parent conversations, identified 15 to 20 interested customers and found five to eight booking-ready leads. I should also have received 10 to 15 sitter applications and contacted five pet-care partners and five society representatives. The most important target is obtaining one to three genuine paid trials. After reviewing these results, I will mark each area as green, amber or red and decide what must be improved during the second week.”

PetSaathi Phase 1 — Days 8–10: Push Paid-Booking Validation

Main goal

The main objective of Days 8–10 is to convert interested pet parents into five to ten genuine paid trial bookings.

During the previous days, PetSaathi tested customer problems, service interest, sitter availability and partner interest. The next step is to determine whether customers are willing to make a real financial commitment.

The proposed prices are validation hypotheses, not confirmed permanent market prices. They should be tested against sitter payouts, operating costs, customer feedback and completed service quality.

Customers must receive clear information about the service, price, refund conditions and limitations before paying. The Department of Consumer Affairs describes consumers’ right to be informed about the quality, standard and price of a service before making a decision.

Target for Days 8–10

By the end of Day 10, PetSaathi should aim to achieve:

### Table 53

| Metric | Target |
| --- | --- |
| Qualified customers contacted | 20–30 |
| Specific trial offers sent | 15–20 |
| Payment links sent | 8–15 |
| Paid trial bookings | 5–10 |
| Dog-walking trials | 3–6 |
| Pet-sitting trials | 1–3 |
| Priority reservations | 1–3 |
| Society-based bookings | 2–5 |
| Referrals received | 5–10 |

A paid trial should be counted only when the customer has selected a service, accepted the terms, chosen a possible date and completed the required payment.

Action 1 — Offer a ₹99 First Dog Walk

What this action means

PetSaathi will offer a first-time customer a 30-minute dog-walking trial for ₹99.

The purpose is to make the first purchase decision easier for customers who are interested but uncertain about trusting a new service.

The offer should be presented as a limited pilot price rather than the permanent price of the service.

What the ₹99 trial should include

The offer should clearly specify that it includes:

One 30-minute dog walk

One assigned and screened walker

Arrival confirmation

Start and end confirmation

One photograph or approved service update

Basic toilet and water information

A short walk report

The customer should also be informed about anything that is not included, such as additional pets, travel charges outside the pilot area or special behavioural handling.

Customer eligibility

The offer should initially be provided only when:

The customer lives in a selected launch area.

A suitable sitter is available.

The dog’s behaviour and health information have been reviewed.

The dog can be safely handled during the pilot.

The customer accepts the service and cancellation conditions.

The customer provides an emergency contact.

Not every pet should automatically be accepted for the first trial.

For example, a dog with a recent bite incident, serious escape behaviour or specialised medical needs may require a more experienced sitter and additional assessment.

Recommended sales question

Instead of asking:

Are you interested in dog walking?

Ask:

We have a 30-minute PetSaathi trial walk available in your area for ₹99 this week. It includes an assigned walker, service confirmation, one update and a short walk report. Would you like to select a date?

This question requires the customer to make a more specific decision.

Why this offer is useful

A low-entry trial can help PetSaathi test:

Whether customers are willing to pay

Whether the booking process works

Whether sitters arrive on time

Whether customers value updates

Whether the customer will book again

Whether the future normal price is acceptable

Important financial check

The ₹99 offer should not be used if it creates an unsafe or unsustainable service.

PetSaathi should calculate:

Customer payment − sitter payout − payment cost − support cost = contribution per trial

For example:

### Table 54

| Item | Example amount |
| --- | --- |
| Customer pays | ₹99 |
| Sitter trial payout | ₹70 |
| Payment and communication cost | ₹5–₹10 |
| Remaining contribution | ₹19–₹24 |

These numbers are planning examples only.

Output

A simple and clearly defined ₹99 dog-walking trial ready to offer to qualified customers.

Simple explanation for professor

“I used a ₹99 first dog walk to make the customer’s initial decision easier and to test whether interest could become a real paid booking.”

Action 2 — Offer a ₹199 Pet-Sitting Trial

What this action means

PetSaathi will offer a one-hour home pet-sitting trial for ₹199.

This offer is primarily a trust test.

Pet sitting requires the customer to trust a caregiver inside or around their home and to allow the caregiver to follow instructions for their pet.

What the trial should include

The one-hour sitting trial may include:

Arrival and departure confirmation

Feeding according to written instructions

Fresh drinking water

Basic playtime or companionship

Toilet or litter-area support

One photograph or video update

A final sitting report

Medication should not be included unless the task is clearly authorised, appropriate and assigned to someone with the necessary training.

Suitable customers

The offer may be suitable for:

Working pet parents

Customers planning future travel

Pet parents who want to test a sitter before a longer booking

Owners of pets that do not require a full walk

Customers who need feeding or companionship visits

Customers considering future overnight care

Recommended trust-first approach

Before confirming the trial, PetSaathi should:

Review the customer’s pet information.

Match an appropriate sitter.

Explain the sitter’s completed screening level.

Offer a video introduction or meet-and-greet where practical.

Confirm which rooms or areas the sitter may access.

Explain the service-update process.

Confirm emergency contacts.

Collect payment only after the details are accepted.

Recommended sales question

We are offering a one-hour home pet-sitting trial for ₹199 in selected areas. It includes feeding or companionship support, one update and a final service report. Would you like to test the service before booking a longer sitting period?

What PetSaathi should measure

After the trial, the team should ask:

Did the customer feel comfortable with the sitter?

Was the sitter punctual?

Were the instructions followed?

Were the updates sufficient?

Would the customer allow the same sitter to return?

Would the customer pay the future normal price?

Would the customer book a longer sitting service?

Output

A structured ₹199 pet-sitting trial designed to test customer trust and service quality.

Simple explanation for professor

“The ₹199 pet-sitting trial tests whether customers are comfortable allowing a PetSaathi caregiver to care for their pet inside the home.”

Action 3 — Offer a Refundable ₹49 Priority Booking

What this action means

Customers who need a service in the future but are not ready to confirm the full booking may pay a ₹49 refundable reservation amount.

The purpose is to measure payment intent.

A person who pays even a small refundable amount demonstrates stronger interest than someone who only asks for information.

Recommended structure

The ₹49 payment should be handled as follows:

The amount reserves priority for a selected service period.

The amount is credited toward the final booking when the service is confirmed.

The amount is refunded when PetSaathi cannot provide a suitable sitter.

The customer may request a refund before final confirmation.

The reservation expires after a clearly stated period.

Payment does not guarantee a sitter until PetSaathi sends confirmation.

Example reservation statement

Pay ₹49 to reserve priority for your preferred service date. The amount will be adjusted against your final booking. If PetSaathi cannot provide a suitable sitter, the amount will be refunded. Your booking becomes final only after the sitter, service time and complete price are confirmed.

Information required before collecting the amount

PetSaathi should record:

Customer ID

Service required

Preferred date

Preferred area

Pet type

Reservation amount

Refund conditions

Reservation expiry

Payment reference

Current status

Using a payment link

Razorpay Payment Links can be created with an amount, a unique reference number and an optional expiry date. The link can later show states such as paid, cancelled or expired, which can help PetSaathi match each payment to the correct lead or booking.

A payment reference may use a format such as:

PRIORITY-PP014-WALK

This connects the payment to the relevant customer record.

What should be avoided

PetSaathi should not:

Call the amount refundable without defining the refund process.

Hide the fact that a sitter is not yet confirmed.

Retain the amount indefinitely.

create false urgency by claiming that only one slot remains when this is not true.

Convert the payment into a non-refundable charge without customer agreement.

Make refunds dependent on unnecessary customer actions.

Output

A transparent ₹49 reservation test that measures genuine customer payment intent.

Simple explanation for professor

“The refundable ₹49 reservation helps PetSaathi separate serious potential customers from people who are only casually interested.”

Action 4 — Give a Society Discount

What this action means

PetSaathi will offer a limited discount when several customers from the same apartment society request a service.

The objective is not only to reduce the price.

The society offer is intended to build group trust and improve local operating efficiency.

Why a society offer may work

When several customers live in the same society:

Sitters travel shorter distances.

Walks can be scheduled more efficiently.

Residents may trust a service recommended within their community.

The same sitter may serve several regular customers.

Customer-acquisition costs may decrease.

PetSaathi can build stronger service density.

Possible society offer

Individual trial

A normal first dog-walking trial may cost ₹149.

Society pilot price

The first trial may cost ₹99 when at least three residents from the same society register.

Example offer

PetSaathi is running a limited society pilot in [Society Name]. If at least three pet parents register, each eligible resident can receive a 30-minute first dog-walking trial for ₹99.

Alternative group offer

PetSaathi may offer:

First walk at a discounted price

No travel charge within the society

Meet-and-greet event for registered residents

One common pet-care awareness session

Referral credit after completed bookings

Priority access to a regular society walker

Conditions to define

The offer should explain:

Minimum number of residents required

Eligible services

Pilot dates

Supported time slots

Number of pets included

Sitter availability

Cancellation rules

Whether the discount applies once or repeatedly

Whether the society officially supports the pilot

PetSaathi should not imply that a society has approved or endorsed the service unless an authorised representative has actually done so.

What should be measured

Record:

Number of society residents contacted

Number who register

Number who pay

Number who complete a service

Most requested time slot

Cost per society customer

Repeat-booking interest

Society manager’s feedback

Output

A clearly defined society offer that tests group demand and local service density.

Simple explanation for professor

“The society discount tests whether customers are more willing to use PetSaathi when several residents join the pilot together.”

Action 5 — Ask Customers for Referrals

What this action means

After a positive conversation or completed service, PetSaathi should ask the customer to introduce another pet parent who may need the service.

Referrals may reduce customer-acquisition cost because the new customer receives information from someone they already know.

However, PetSaathi should not ask customers to share another person’s telephone number without permission.

A better method is to give the customer a message or link that they can forward voluntarily.

Recommended referral request

Thank you for supporting the PetSaathi pilot. Do you know another pet parent in your area who may need dog walking or pet sitting? You may forward them this trial link. They can contact us directly if interested.

Referral incentive options

PetSaathi may test:

₹50 credit after the referred customer completes a paid booking

Discount on the next dog walk

Free service add-on

Society group discount

Priority access during peak periods

The reward should become valid only after the referred customer completes the required qualifying action.

Referral information to record

The CRM should contain:

Referrer customer ID

Referred lead ID

Referral date

Service requested

Referral status

Payment status

Reward eligibility

Reward issued

WhatsApp communication rule

WhatsApp states that customers should opt in before receiving business marketing messages. Therefore, PetSaathi should ask existing customers to forward the information or obtain the referred person’s permission before initiating repeated promotional communication.

Output

A permission-based referral process that produces relevant local leads without uncontrolled messaging.

Simple explanation for professor

“I asked satisfied or interested customers to introduce other pet parents so PetSaathi could attract leads through trusted local relationships.”

Recommended Day 8 Plan — Prepare and Send the Offers

Morning: Select Qualified Leads

I will review the CRM and identify customers who:

Live in the launch areas

Need a service within 30 days

Accept the approximate price

Have provided pet details

Have allowed follow-up contact

Are classified as hot or strong warm leads

Afternoon: Send Specific Offers

Each customer should receive the offer most relevant to their requirement.

Examples include:

Dog owner needing regular walks: ₹99 dog-walking trial

Customer preparing for travel: ₹199 pet-sitting trial

Customer with uncertain dates: ₹49 priority reservation

Society resident: society pilot offer

End-of-Day Output

Qualified leads selected

Offers sent

Responses recorded

Payment links prepared

Follow-up dates assigned

Recommended Day 9 Plan — Follow Up and Convert

Morning: Follow Up with Interested Customers

I will contact customers who:

Requested the payment link

Asked to meet the sitter

Needed family approval

Wanted another date

Requested service details

The follow-up should answer the specific concern rather than repeating the complete sales message.

Afternoon: Confirm the Booking Details

Before payment or final confirmation, I will verify:

Customer name

Pet details

Service

Date and time

Address or society

Assigned sitter

Complete price

Trial inclusions

Cancellation conditions

Emergency contact

End-of-Day Output

Payment links sent

Payments received

Meet-and-greets arranged

Sitters provisionally assigned

Remaining objections recorded

Recommended Day 10 Plan — Close and Prepare the Trials

Morning: Final Conversion Push

I will contact only customers who previously showed clear interest and allowed follow-up.

I will not send repeated messages to cold leads or people who declined contact.

Afternoon: Complete Booking Confirmation

Every paid customer should receive:

Booking ID

Service name

Pet name

Date and time

Assigned sitter

Amount paid

Remaining balance, when applicable

Cancellation conditions

Emergency contact details

Expected service updates

End-of-Day Review

I will calculate:

Number of offers sent

Number of payment links opened

Number of payments completed

Conversion rate by service

Conversion rate by area

Conversion rate by lead source

Most common payment objection

Most successful offer

Improved WhatsApp Trial Message

The original message is understandable, but the verification and safety wording should match the checks that have actually been completed.

Recommended version

Hello [Name],

PetSaathi is launching a limited pet-care pilot in [Area]. 🐾

You can currently request:

• A 30-minute dog walk• Home pet sitting• Controlled pet-boarding availability

Before confirmation, we will share the assigned sitter’s completed screening level, service details, price and cancellation conditions.

The service includes the agreed updates, such as arrival confirmation, photographs or a short pet-care report.

We are offering a 30-minute first dog-walking trial for ₹99 this week, subject to sitter availability and review of your pet’s requirements.

Would you like to check an available date?

Reply:1 — Yes, share dates2 — I need more information3 — Not required currently

Why this version is stronger

This message:

Identifies the service as a limited pilot.

Avoids claiming that every possible applicant is fully verified.

Explains that availability is not guaranteed.

States what the customer will receive.

Presents one specific price.

Gives the customer an easy way to decline.

Moves interested customers toward selecting a date.

Paid-Trial Booking Record

Every payment should create a structured booking record.

### Table 55

| Field | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Booking ID | BK-008 |
| Customer ID | PP-014 |
| Service | 30-minute dog walk |
| Area | Whitefield |
| Trial price | ₹99 |
| Payment status | Paid |
| Payment reference | PAY-BK008 |
| Preferred date | 19 July |
| Sitter status | Provisional match |
| Customer terms accepted | Yes |
| Service status | Awaiting confirmation |

Conversion Metrics

Offer-to-Payment Rate

Formula:

Paid trials ÷ Specific offers sent × 100

Example:

If PetSaathi sends 15 trial offers and receives six payments:

6 ÷ 15 × 100 = 40%

Payment-Link Conversion Rate

Formula:

Payments completed ÷ Payment links sent × 100

Service-Specific Conversion

Calculate the conversion rate separately for:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Priority reservation

Society offer

Referral Conversion

Formula:

Paid referred customers ÷ Referred leads × 100

Go, Adjust or Stop Decision

Continue the Offer

Continue when:

At least five paid trials are received.

Suitable sitters are available.

Customers understand the offer.

Trial pricing covers the necessary sitter and operational costs.

No major safety issue is present.

Adjust the Offer

Adjust when:

Customers request the payment link but do not pay.

Customers need to meet the sitter first.

The service is attractive but the date is unsuitable.

Price objections appear repeatedly.

One area converts significantly better than another.

Pet sitting converts better than dog walking, or the reverse.

Stop or Pause

Pause the offer when:

No suitable sitter is available.

Customer or pet information is incomplete.

The service cannot be delivered safely.

The advertised updates cannot be provided.

Refund conditions are unclear.

The trial price cannot cover basic service delivery.

Local boarding requirements have not been checked.

Simple Explanation for Professor

“During Days 8 to 10, I focused on converting customer interest into genuine paid trial bookings. I offered a ₹99 first dog walk to reduce the customer’s initial purchase risk and a ₹199 pet-sitting trial to test whether customers trusted a caregiver inside their home. I also offered a refundable ₹49 priority reservation to measure payment intent among customers whose dates were not yet confirmed. For apartment societies, I prepared a group offer to test whether community trust and concentrated demand improved conversion. Finally, I requested referrals from interested and satisfied customers. Every payment was connected to a booking ID, service, date and clear refund conditions. The main target was to receive five to ten genuine paid trials before moving further with product development.”

PetSaathi Phase 1 — Days 11–12: Run the First Bookings Manually

Main goal

The purpose of Days 11 and 12 is to complete PetSaathi’s first paid services manually from beginning to end.

At this stage, a complete application is not required. The team can coordinate customers and sitters through the CRM, WhatsApp, payment links and structured forms.

The objective is to prove that PetSaathi can:

Confirm the customer’s requirements

Assign an appropriate sitter

Collect and record payment

Complete the service safely

Provide evidence and updates

Collect customer feedback

Generate repeat bookings and referrals

Established pet-care platforms use structured service updates containing photographs, walk maps, total distance, toilet breaks, and food or water information. PetBacker similarly promotes GPS walk reports, daily photographs and videos, and reviews following completed services. These are useful models for PetSaathi’s manual report card.

Recommended Manual Booking Status Flow

Every booking should move through the following stages:

Requested → Qualified → Sitter Assigned → Payment Pending → Paid → Confirmed → Service Started → Service Completed → Report Sent → Review Requested → Closed

If a problem occurs, the booking may instead move to:

Cancelled

Replacement sitter required

Incident reported

Refund pending

Refunded

Disputed

Every status change should be recorded in the booking CRM with the date, time and responsible team member.

1. Confirm the Pet Details

Before accepting the booking, PetSaathi must confirm that the team and sitter have complete and accurate information about the pet.

The team should not depend only on information collected during the first customer conversation. The final booking confirmation should contain the most recent details.

Basic pet information

Confirm:

Pet’s name

Pet type

Breed

Age

Approximate weight

Sex

Sterilisation status, where relevant

Photograph for identification

Behaviour information

Ask the customer to explain:

Behaviour around strangers

Behaviour around children

Behaviour around other animals

Pulling behaviour during walks

Fear or anxiety triggers

Bite or aggression history

Escape history

Resource guarding

Reaction to traffic, lifts and loud noises

The sitter should know what behaviour to expect before entering the customer’s home or handling the pet.

Health information

Confirm:

Vaccination status

Current illnesses

Allergies

Recent injuries

Medication

Physical limitations

Heat sensitivity

Feeding restrictions

Veterinary contact

The sitter should not diagnose or independently treat a medical problem.

Service instructions

For a dog walk, confirm:

Collar or harness to be used

Leash location

Approved route

Areas to avoid

Whether the dog may meet other dogs

Water requirements

Toilet-cleanup process

Entry and exit instructions

For pet sitting, confirm:

Food quantity

Feeding time

Water instructions

Litter or toilet instructions

Rooms the sitter may enter

Items the sitter may use

Doors and windows that must remain closed

Alarm, key or access process

Emergency information

Confirm:

Pet parent’s primary number

Secondary emergency contact

Preferred veterinarian

Nearby emergency clinic

Veterinary transport permission

Emergency-spending limit

Relevant medical history

A meet-and-greet is especially useful for first bookings because it allows the owner to explain the pet’s behaviour, care requirements and home routine directly to the sitter. Rover also recommends discussing the pet’s needs and behaviours with a prospective sitter before the service.

Output

A complete and approved pet-care instruction record attached to the booking.

Simple explanation

“I confirmed the pet’s health, behaviour, equipment, routine and emergency information before starting the service.”

2. Confirm the Sitter

After reviewing the pet’s requirements, PetSaathi should assign a sitter who is suitable for that specific booking.

A sitter approved for small-dog walking should not automatically be assigned to a large, reactive dog.

Confirm the sitter’s suitability

Check:

Sitter ID

Current approval status

Approved service category

Pet sizes the sitter can handle

Relevant experience

Availability

Location and travel time

Completed training

Verification level

Previous reliability record

Confirm the booking with the sitter

The sitter should receive:

Booking ID

Service type

Date and time

Approximate location

Pet type, breed and size

Behaviour summary

Special care instructions

Required service updates

Expected payout

Emergency process

The sitter should explicitly accept the booking.

Silence should not be treated as acceptance.

Prevent unauthorised substitutions

The confirmed sitter must personally complete the service.

The sitter must not send a friend, family member or another caregiver without PetSaathi’s review and the customer’s approval.

If the assigned sitter becomes unavailable, PetSaathi should:

Inform the customer immediately.

Identify a suitable replacement.

Share the replacement sitter’s details.

Obtain the customer’s approval.

Update the CRM.

Output

One suitable sitter formally assigned to the booking.

Simple explanation

“I matched the pet with a sitter who had the correct experience, approval and availability.”

3. Collect and Record the Payment

Payment should be completed according to the agreed trial conditions before the booking is treated as fully confirmed.

Confirm the complete price

The customer should see:

Service name

Service duration

Trial price

Additional-pet charge, if any

Travel charge, if any

Complete payable amount

Cancellation conditions

Refund conditions

No unexpected charge should be introduced after the service has started.

Connect the payment to the booking

Every payment should include:

Booking ID

Customer ID

Service description

Amount

Payment-link reference

Payment date

Payment status

Razorpay Payment Links can contain the amount, description, expiry date and a unique reference ID. Using the booking ID as the reference helps connect each payment with the correct PetSaathi record.

Suggested payment statuses

Payment link not sent

Payment link sent

Payment pending

Paid

Payment failed

Refund pending

Refunded

The booking should be marked Paid only after successful payment confirmation has been received.

Output

A confirmed payment connected to the correct customer and booking IDs.

Simple explanation

“I collected the agreed trial payment and linked it with the correct booking record.”

4. Share the Sitter Details with the Customer

After payment and assignment, the customer should receive the relevant sitter information.

Information to share

The customer may receive:

Sitter’s first name

Profile photograph

Relevant experience

Approved services

Completed verification level

Interview or training status

Expected arrival time

PetSaathi contact channel

Meet-and-greet details, where applicable

Use accurate verification language

Use descriptions such as:

Identity checked

Address checked

Interview completed

PetSaathi training completed

Approved for dog walking

Do not say:

Completely safe

Risk-free sitter

Fully verified

Police verified

unless the exact stated check has genuinely been completed.

Protect sitter privacy

The customer should not receive:

Complete identity-document copies

Complete residential address

Bank details

Internal assessment notes

Unnecessary personal information

Sample sitter-introduction message

Hello [Customer Name],

Your PetSaathi trial has been assigned to [Sitter First Name].

Approved service: Dog walkingRelevant experience: [Brief description]Completed checks: [Accurate verification details]Expected arrival: [Time]

The sitter will follow the confirmed pet instructions and provide the agreed service updates.

Please contact PetSaathi immediately if any information about your pet has changed.

Output

The customer receives accurate and privacy-conscious sitter information.

Simple explanation

“I introduced the assigned sitter to the customer and clearly stated which checks and approvals had been completed.”

5. Start the Service

The sitter should formally check in when arriving at the service location.

Arrival process

The sitter should:

Arrive within the confirmed time window.

Notify the customer or PetSaathi.

Confirm the correct pet.

Review any last-minute instructions.

Inspect the collar, harness, leash or carrier.

Confirm that the pet appears suitable to begin the service.

Record the actual start time.

Dog-walk start checklist

Before leaving the property, the sitter should check:

Harness or collar is secure

Leash is undamaged

Door and gate are controlled

Identification tag is present where applicable

Weather is suitable

Water is available if required

Route is safe

No unauthorised off-leash activity is planned

Pet-sitting start checklist

The sitter should confirm:

Entry access works

Pet is present

Doors and windows are secure

Food and water instructions are visible

Restricted rooms are understood

Emergency numbers are available

The customer has been notified of arrival

Start-location tracking only when promised

When location sharing is part of the service, it should start when the walk begins and stop when the service ends.

The customer should know who can view the location and for how long.

Output

A recorded service start time and arrival confirmation.

Simple explanation

“The sitter formally checked in, reviewed the safety requirements and recorded the actual service start time.”

6. Obtain Photo or Video Proof

The sitter should provide the agreed evidence that the service is taking place.

Photographs and videos should demonstrate service delivery without unnecessarily exposing the customer’s home, family or location.

Recommended evidence for a dog walk

The sitter may provide:

Arrival photograph

One photograph during the walk

End-of-walk photograph

Location or route screenshot, when included

Short video when useful and permitted

Recommended evidence for pet sitting

The sitter may provide:

Arrival confirmation

Feeding or water photograph

Pet activity photograph

Final photograph

Short written update

Evidence timing

A simple pilot standard may be:

One update near the beginning

One update during the service

One final update after completion

The sitter should not spend excessive service time creating content.

Photo updates are widely used in established pet-care services to build trust and keep owners informed during a booking. Rover encourages sitters to communicate frequently and provide photo updates, while PetBacker advertises daily photo and video reports.

Separate service and marketing consent

The customer may consent to receive private service updates without consenting to public marketing use.

PetSaathi should therefore use separate options:

Service-update consent: permits private updates to the owner.

Marketing consent: permits selected media to be published on the website or social media.

Rover’s help information shows why this distinction matters: booking photographs may be made public unless privacy controls are used. PetSaathi should use private-by-default handling and obtain separate promotional permission.

India’s DPDP Act requires consent requests to be presented clearly and in plain language.

Output

Time-linked service evidence sent privately to the customer and stored with controlled access.

Simple explanation

“The sitter sent photographs or videos as service proof, but promotional use required separate customer permission.”

7. Send the Pet Report Card
