Do not compensate for weak payment or safety evidence using high Instagram engagement or large application numbers.

Recommended Final Decision Report

### Table 89

| Decision area | Finding | Evidence | Decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Demand | [Strong/Medium/Weak] | Paid and booking-ready customers | — |
| Primary service | [Service] | Payment and repeat demand | — |
| Primary area | [Area] | Customer and sitter density | — |
| Pricing | ₹[Amount] | Conversion and contribution | — |
| Sitter supply | [Strong/Medium/Weak] | Shortlist and availability | — |
| Operational readiness | [Ready/Needs work] | Completed bookings | — |
| Safety readiness | [Ready/Blocked] | Incidents and SOP compliance | — |
| Society channel | [Strong/Testing/Weak] | Agreed next actions | — |
| Overall decision | — | Combined evidence | Go/Conditional Go/No-Go |

Day 15 Final Questions

Before approving Phase 2, answer:

Did at least five real customers pay?

Were those bookings completed successfully?

Which service produced the strongest paid demand?

Which area produced the strongest customer density?

Are suitable sitters available in that area?

Will the future price cover sitter and operational costs?

What was the main reason customers did not pay?

Did any customer request a repeat booking?

Can the team manually complete the next 20 bookings?

Is the validated problem important enough to justify product development?

Which features are genuinely required in the MVP?

Which features should remain outside Phase 2?

Final Principle

Phase 2 should not attempt to build every idea discussed during Phase 1.

It should build the smallest system required to improve the process that has already produced:

Paying customers

Successful services

Repeat demand

Reliable sitters

Concentrated local activity

Simple Explanation for Professor

“Day 15 is the final go or no-go decision for the PetSaathi validation sprint. I will compare the actual results with six targets: five to ten paid bookings, at least twenty booking-ready leads, fifty interested pet parents, twenty-five sitter applications, ten shortlisted sitters and three to five interested societies. Paid bookings are the most important evidence because they demonstrate real willingness to pay. I will also review completed-service quality, sitter reliability, area density, customer objections and unit economics. If the hard conditions are achieved, PetSaathi can move to a focused Phase 2 MVP. If the results are promising but incomplete, the validation sprint should be extended. If payment, safety or supply evidence is weak, full product development should be paused and the business assumptions should be revised.”

PetSaathi Phase 1 — Corrected Strategy and Decision Framework 🐾

Important evidence note

The city recommendations, launch-area choices and exact trial prices in your plan should be treated as validation hypotheses, not confirmed market facts.

Current public listings show that established pet-care platforms already offer walking, sitting and boarding in cities such as Bengaluru, Pune, Mumbai and Ahmedabad. For example, PawSpace publicly advertises dog walking, sitting, boarding, background checks and live updates in Bengaluru, while PetBacker lists providers and services across several Indian cities. This confirms that demand and competition exist, but it does not prove that a particular neighbourhood, price or service will work for PetSaathi.

1. Do Not Move to Phase 2 If These Conditions Exist

1.1 Nobody Pays

PetSaathi should not begin full MVP development when customers say that the idea is useful but nobody is willing to pay.

Positive comments, survey responses, Instagram likes and WhatsApp enquiries demonstrate awareness or curiosity. They do not demonstrate commercial demand.

A real payment means that the customer has:

Selected a service

Accepted a price

Chosen a possible date

Trusted the service enough to commit money

Accepted the trial conditions

Correct decision

When nobody pays, the team should investigate:

Whether the wrong customer segment was targeted

Whether the price is unsuitable

Whether trust is too weak

Whether customers need the service at a later date

Whether the offer is unclear

Whether the selected city or area is incorrect

The correct response is to repeat or redesign Phase 1, not to build a complete application.

1.2 Sitters Are Unreliable

PetSaathi should not scale when sitter applicants repeatedly:

Miss interviews

Arrive late

Cancel bookings

Give inconsistent information

Refuse verification

Ignore service instructions

Fail to provide reports

Use unsafe animal-handling methods

Attempt to send unauthorised substitutes

A marketplace cannot solve unreliable supply merely by creating better software.

Correct decision

The team should strengthen:

Sitter sourcing

Verification

Interviews

Practical assessments

Training

Probation bookings

Performance monitoring

Suspension rules

Phase 2 should begin only when PetSaathi has a small group of dependable caregivers who can complete bookings consistently.

1.3 Users Only Want Free Services

A free-service test can show whether customers are willing to try the experience, but it cannot prove that the business has paying demand.

Customers who accept a free dog walk may refuse even a modest regular price later.

Correct decision

PetSaathi should test at least one genuine payment action:

₹49 refundable priority reservation

₹99 first dog-walking trial

₹149 paid walking trial

₹199 pet-sitting trial

The trial price should be clearly described as temporary.

PetSaathi should also ask whether the customer would continue at the expected normal price. This prevents the team from mistaking discount demand for sustainable demand.

1.4 Demand Is Too Scattered Across the City

Suppose PetSaathi has:

One customer in Whitefield

One in HSR Layout

One in Yelahanka

One in Electronic City

One in Rajajinagar

This may produce five leads, but it does not create an efficient operating zone.

Scattered demand creates:

Long sitter travel times

Higher travel costs

Late arrivals

Difficult replacement arrangements

Weak society-level trust

Poor repeat scheduling

Low contribution per booking

Correct decision

PetSaathi should identify one or two concentrated micro-markets where customers, sitters and partners overlap.

A smaller service area with ten nearby customers may be more valuable than twenty customers spread across an entire city.

1.5 No Single Area Has Density

Area density means that enough suitable customers and sitters exist within a manageable distance.

A strong area should ideally contain:

Several interested pet parents

Paid trial bookings

Shortlisted sitters

Apartment societies

Veterinary support

Repeat-service demand

Short travel routes

Correct decision

When no area shows density, PetSaathi should:

Continue testing selected areas.

Stop advertising city-wide.

Focus outreach on individual societies.

Recruit sitters in the same neighbourhoods.

Compare lead-to-payment conversion by locality.

Place unsupported areas on a waitlist.

The business should not scale geographically before proving one workable local cluster.

1.6 Trust Objections Are High and Unsolved

Customers may repeatedly ask:

Who is the sitter?

How was the sitter checked?

Can I meet the sitter first?

What happens if my dog escapes?

What happens during an emergency?

What happens if the sitter cancels?

How do I know the walk happened?

Who can see my home address?

If these questions repeatedly stop payment, PetSaathi has not yet solved the trust problem.

Correct decision

Before moving to Phase 2, PetSaathi should introduce specific trust evidence:

Identity-check status

Interview-completed status

Reference-check status

Training status

Service-specific approval

Meet-and-greet option

Arrival and completion confirmation

Photo updates

Pet report card

Emergency process

Cancellation and replacement policy

The platform must display only checks that were genuinely completed.

2. City-Specific Phase 1 Strategies

Bengaluru

Proposed focus

Begin with:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Controlled boarding beta

Suggested test areas:

Whitefield

HSR Layout

Sarjapur Road

Why this direction is reasonable

Bengaluru already has visible organised competition. PawSpace advertises dog walking with background checks, meet-and-greets, live updates and 24-hour support. It also advertises background-verified in-home pet sitting. PawSpace currently states an approximate Bengaluru walking range of ₹130–₹200 per walk, which makes a ₹149 trial a plausible test price rather than a proven permanent price.

Expected customer characteristics

The following are working hypotheses that must be tested:

Customers may be comfortable using digital booking flows.

Working professionals may value scheduled walking.

Customers may compare PetSaathi with established platforms.

Users may expect a polished website and professional communication.

Apartment-society access may influence acquisition.

Recommended first offer

30-minute trial walk for ₹149

Main trust feature

Screened sitter plus location or service updates

Do not promise live GPS unless the operational process can reliably provide it.

Recommended message

Verified dog walkers for busy pet parents in Whitefield, HSR and Sarjapur Road, with clear updates after every walk.

Main caution

Avoid competing mainly on the lowest price. Bengaluru already has organised providers, so PetSaathi should differentiate through local density, sitter continuity and transparent service proof.

Pune

Proposed focus

Begin with:

Trial dog walking

Monthly walking plans

Weekend pet sitting

Suggested test areas:

Baner

Wakad

Kharadi

Why this direction is reasonable

Public marketplace listings confirm existing walking, boarding and pet-sitting supply in Pune. PawSpace also advertises trained and verified sitters for in-home care in Pune. This indicates that PetSaathi would enter an active market rather than an empty category.

Expected customer and supply conditions

The following remain hypotheses:

IT-area demand may support regular walking.

Student communities may help sitter recruitment.

Customers may be more price-sensitive than those in some premium Bengaluru areas.

Apartment societies may be an effective acquisition channel.

Recommended first offer

₹99–₹129 trial walk

This range should be tested against sitter payout and travel cost.

Main trust feature

Ability to request the same walker repeatedly

Recommended message

Book a trusted daily dog walker near Baner, Wakad or Kharadi, with photo updates after every walk.

Main caution

Do not introduce premium pricing before proving that customers value the additional verification and updates.

Mumbai

Proposed focus

Begin with hyperlocal dog walking within approximately one or two kilometres.

Suggested test areas:

Powai

Bandra

Andheri West

Why this direction is reasonable

PetBacker and PawSpace both publicly list sitting or boarding services in Mumbai, confirming organised competition.

The one-to-two-kilometre service radius is an operational hypothesis. It should be tested through actual travel time, sitter availability and booking contribution.

Recommended first offer

₹199 trial walk

Main trust feature

A sitter based in the same locality

Recommended message

Screened dog walkers within your locality, designed for busy Mumbai pet parents.

Main caution

Do not match customers with distant sitters merely because both are located in Mumbai.

Travel delays can damage punctuality and service economics.

Boarding should remain restricted because space, host suitability and local operating conditions may create greater complexity.

Gurugram

Proposed focus

Begin with premium, verification-led pet care inside selected gated societies.

Suggested test areas:

DLF phases

Golf Course Road

Sectors 56 and 57

Expected conditions

The assumptions to test are:

Customers may have stronger spending capacity.

Residents may demand detailed sitter verification.

Society permission may influence customer access.

Premium positioning may work when backed by real proof.

The Gurugram district is a large urban market, but public city data alone does not confirm demand for PetSaathi or these exact areas. The assumptions must therefore be validated through interviews and paid trials.

Recommended first offer

₹199 trial walk

Main trust feature

Use exact verification labels such as:

Identity checked

Address checked

Interview completed

Reference checked

Use Police Verification Completed only after a valid official check has actually been reviewed.

Recommended message

Premium screened pet care for selected Gurugram societies, with walking, sitting and a documented emergency process.

Main caution

Avoid weak or vague statements such as “fully verified.”

Premium pricing without premium operational evidence will reduce trust.

Ahmedabad

Proposed focus

Begin with:

Pet sitting

Dog walking

Controlled boarding beta

Suggested test areas:

Satellite

Bopal

South Bopal

Why this direction is reasonable

PetBacker already lists walking, sitting and boarding providers in Ahmedabad, meaning PetSaathi should assume some competition.

The claim that Ahmedabad has “lower competition” than Bengaluru is a hypothesis and should not be presented as confirmed without a structured competitor count.

Recommended first offers

₹99 dog-walking trial

₹199 pet-sitting trial

Main trust feature

Nearby caregiver with clearly documented checks and updates

Recommended message

Trusted local pet sitters and walkers for Ahmedabad families, with screened caregivers and clear service updates.

Main caution

Do not rely only on an app-style brand image.

Local referrals, veterinary contacts, pet shops and societies may be important, but this should be measured through acquisition data.

Surat

Proposed focus

Begin with:

Home pet sitting

Trial dog walking

Carefully controlled local boarding

Suggested test areas:

Vesu

Adajan

Citylight

Expected conditions

The statements that Surat’s market is less mature or requires more offline education are hypotheses. They need to be verified through interviews.

Surat is a major and rapidly growing Gujarat city, but general city growth does not automatically prove demand for a pet-care marketplace.

Recommended first offers

₹99 dog walk

₹199 pet-sitting trial

Main trust feature

Family-oriented, local and screened caregiver

Recommended message

Safe local pet care for Surat families when you are travelling, working or unavailable.

Main caution

The landing page and outreach should explain what pet sitting means.

Do not assume every customer already understands the difference between pet sitting, daycare and boarding.

3. What Is Wrong with Many Founders’ Phase 1 Approach?

Mistake 1: Building the Application Before Validation

Wrong approach

Building:

Customer dashboards

Sitter dashboards

In-app chat

Payment automation

GPS systems

Recommendation engines

Complex admin panels

before obtaining genuine paid bookings.

Correct approach

Start with:

Landing page

Three forms

WhatsApp Business

CRM

Payment link

Manual matching

Manual report card

Razorpay Payment Links can accept payments without requiring PetSaathi to build a complete checkout application. They support amounts, link expiry and unique reference IDs that can be associated with booking records.

The software should automate a process that has already shown evidence of working.

Mistake 2: Counting Likes as Validation

Weak metrics

Instagram likes

Followers

Page views

Video views

Comments

Post shares

These metrics help measure reach, but they do not prove that a customer will book.

Strong metrics

Booking-ready leads

Paid reservations

Paid trials

Completed services

Repeat-booking interest

Sitter applications

Approved sitters

Society next steps

Customer referrals

A hundred likes with zero payments is weaker validation than five genuine paid trials.

Mistake 3: Offering Too Many Services

Wrong approach

Launching:

Walking

Sitting

Boarding

Grooming

Training

Pet food

Veterinary services

Taxi

Funeral services

Adoption

Products

simultaneously.

Correct approach

Start with:

Dog walking

Pet sitting

Controlled boarding beta

This allows PetSaathi to learn one operational model without dividing attention among unrelated services.

Mistake 4: Using a Weak or Unsupported Trust System

The sentence:

ID-verified, video-interviewed, trained, reviewed and report-card enabled.

is not automatically wrong.

It becomes wrong when PetSaathi uses it as a general promise without proving that every assigned sitter completed those checks.

Better system

Display individual, evidence-based statuses:

Identity checked

Interview completed

Reference checked

Training completed

Approved for dog walking

Three trial bookings completed

Customer rating: 4.8/5

Each badge should represent a completed action.

Mistake 5: Launching Without Area Density

Wrong approach

Advertising:

All Bengaluru

All Gujarat

All India

during Phase 1.

Correct approach

Choose:

One city

Two or three nearby areas

A small set of societies

Sitters within a defined travel radius

The aim is to prove repeatable local operations before geographical expansion.

4. Major Proposed Improvements

Improvement 1: Add Payment Validation

Recommendation: Approve

PetSaathi should use:

₹49 refundable priority reservation, or

₹99/₹149 trial booking

Important correction

Payment should not be mandatory for every research participant.

It should be mandatory only when a person is counted as:

A paid lead

A paid reservation

A confirmed trial booking

Customers should receive clear information about:

What the amount reserves

Whether it is refundable

When it expires

When the booking becomes confirmed

How the refund will be issued

Why this improvement matters

It:

Filters casual interest

Measures real purchase intent

Tests the payment process

Produces stronger validation

Creates a booking record

Decision: Approved for Phase 1.

Improvement 2: Make Boarding a Controlled Beta

Recommendation: Approve

Boarding should not be open to every sitter.

Only the following should be considered:

Home-assessed hosts

Professionally operated boarding partners

Providers approved for the relevant area

Hosts with safe capacity and emergency arrangements

Why this improvement matters

Boarding involves:

Overnight responsibility

Property safety

Other animals

Feeding and medication

Escape risk

Emergency decisions

Local operating rules

Tamil Nadu’s February 2026 policy shows how strict state-level regulation can become: it establishes registration and operating requirements for pet-boarding facilities. The policy applies to Tamil Nadu, not all of India, but it demonstrates why boarding must be checked state by state and city by city.

Decision: Approved as controlled beta only.

Improvement 3: Use Three Separate CTAs

Recommendation: Approve

The landing page should use:

### Table 90

| CTA | User | Validation purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Book a Trial | Pet parent | Customer demand and payment |
| Become a Sitter | Caregiver | Supply validation |
| Partner Your Society | Manager or RWA | B2B and density validation |

These three paths should connect to separate forms and CRM records.

Decision: Approved.

Improvement 4: Make the CRM Structure Official

Recommendation: Approve

The CRM should contain separate tables or sheets for:

Pet-parent leads

Sitter applicants

Society leads

Partners

Bookings

Payments

Reviews

Incidents

Why this improvement matters

A proper CRM:

Prevents lost follow-ups

Separates interest from payment

Measures area density

Tracks sitter performance

Supports unit-economics analysis

Provides the future database structure

Only necessary personal information should be collected, access should be restricted and the intended purpose should be explained. India’s digital-data framework governs the processing of digital personal data and should be considered when designing forms, CRM access and retention practices.

Decision: Approved as the official Phase 1 operating system.

Fastest premium-market validation

Bengaluru

Larger premium opportunity

Higher digital expectations

Ability to handle stronger competition

Recommended initial service:

Dog walking

Recommended initial service:

Pet sitting

6. Recommended First-City Decision

Recommended default: Ahmedabad

Based on your stated objective of maintaining Gujarat-first operational control, Ahmedabad is the most appropriate initial choice.

Initial launch areas

Satellite

Bopal

South Bopal

Initial services

Pet sitting

Dog walking

Controlled boarding beta

Why Ahmedabad is the recommended default

It allows PetSaathi to test:

Local referral trust

Premium-family demand

Society partnerships

Caregiver recruitment

Sitting and walking economics

Controlled boarding operations

with closer founder oversight.

This is still a hypothesis. If your team lacks a meaningful local network or operational presence in Ahmedabad, Pune may be a better balanced choice.

7. Phase 1 Deliverables Explained

### Table 91

| Deliverable | Why it is required |
| --- | --- |
| Landing page live | Presents the offer and collects measurable actions |
| WhatsApp booking active | Supports quick manual qualification |
| Booking form active | Collects structured customer requirements |
| Sitter form active | Measures caregiver supply |
| Society form active | Measures community and B2B opportunity |
| Instagram page live | Supports awareness and outreach |
| Brochure PDF | Supports society, vet and offline sharing |
| CRM sheet | Tracks leads, payments, bookings and outcomes |
| Customer interviews | Identifies real problems and willingness to pay |
| Sitter applications | Measures potential supply |
| Paid bookings | Demonstrates financial validation |
| Testimonials | Provides service-quality evidence |
| Validation report | Supports the final go/no-go decision |

The numeric targets—such as 100 interviews, 25 sitter applications and 5–10 paid bookings—are internal planning thresholds. They are useful for discipline but are not universal industry rules.

8. Phase 1 Final Validation Report

Product and market information

City tested:The city where the sprint was conducted.

Areas tested:The exact localities and societies included.

Customer results

Total pet-parent conversations:Count only meaningful completed conversations.

Interested leads:Customers who requested details or allowed a next step.

Booking-ready leads:Qualified customers who accepted the price and could select a service date.

Paid bookings:Confirmed payments connected to booking IDs.

Most requested service:Walking, sitting or boarding.

Average willing price:The typical acceptable amount reported or paid.

Top trust concern:The objection most frequently preventing booking.

Top customer segment:For example, working couples, travellers or senior pet parents.

Supply results

Sitter applications:Completed applications received.

Shortlisted sitters:Applicants suitable for further verification.

Approved sitters:Sitters cleared for specific services.

Partnership results

Society interest:Societies agreeing to a real next step.

Veterinary or grooming interest:Partners agreeing to referrals, meetings or events.

Marketing results

Best acquisition channel:The channel producing paid customers, not merely enquiries.

Customer-acquisition cost:Channel cost divided by paid customers.

Operational results

Completed services:Successfully delivered bookings.

Repeat-booking interest:Customers requesting another service.

Complaints or incidents:Problems reported during the trial.

Average customer rating:Feedback from completed services.

Final decision

Choose one:

Move to Phase 2

Extend Phase 1

Repeat Phase 1 with a revised offer

Change launch area

Change primary service

Change city

Pause the project

9. Final Recommended Phase 1 Product

PetSaathi should initially be:

A premium, mobile-friendly landing page for screened pet walking, home pet sitting and controlled boarding in one city, connected to WhatsApp, structured forms, a CRM, payment links and manual service report cards.

It should not yet be:

A nationwide marketplace

A complete native mobile application

A fully automated matching engine

A complex live-tracking platform

A multi-service pet super-app

10. What Phase 1 Must Prove

Phase 1 should demonstrate that:

Customers have a real pet-care problem.

Customers trust PetSaathi’s process.

Customers are willing to pay.

Suitable sitters are available.

One micro-market has enough density.

Services can be delivered manually and safely.

Customers show repeat or referral behaviour.

The expected regular price can cover sitter and operational costs.

Final Decisions

### Table 92

| Decision item | Recommended answer |
| --- | --- |
| First city | Ahmedabad |
| Initial areas | Satellite, Bopal and South Bopal |
| Payment validation | Yes — use a ₹49 refundable reservation or ₹99/₹199 paid trial |
| Payment mandatory? | Only for counting genuine paid validation, not for every interview participant |
| Boarding status | Controlled beta only |
| Landing-page CTAs | Book a Trial + Become a Sitter + Partner Your Society |
| CRM structure | Approve as the official Phase 1 system |
| Full application development | Do not begin until hard validation gates are passed |

Simple explanation for professor

“PetSaathi should not move into Phase 2 simply because people like the idea. The project must first prove that customers will pay, sitters can deliver reliable services, one area contains enough demand and the main trust concerns have been solved. I compared six possible cities and treated each city plan as a hypothesis to be tested rather than a guaranteed result. For Gujarat-first execution, Ahmedabad is the recommended initial city, with Satellite, Bopal and South Bopal as test areas. Phase 1 should use real payment validation, controlled boarding, three separate calls to action and an official CRM. A complete application should be developed only after PetSaathi has demonstrated genuine paid demand, reliable sitter supply, safe operations and repeat potential.”
